# C57: nonlinear ordered repairs have exactly the Huber leading scale

Status: **complete scoped proof candidate; not verifier-accepted**.
No optimization, installation, or fact submission is allocated. The only
accepted mathematical predecessor used below is `1252b8823bb5a290`, for
the necessity and sufficiency of smooth-convex interpolation inequalities
and their explicit conjugate interpolant. Everything specific to the
trajectory, the repair, and its quantitative bound is proved here.

This continues the actual-primal lane. It enlarges the previous single
monotone quadratic auxiliary coordinate to arbitrarily many nonlinear
coordinates, and even to vectors ordered by any fixed acute cone. It gives
an explicit all-pair legal repair and identifies the sharp leading payoff
of the whole stated class. It does not upper-bound unrestricted W_n(H_m).

## 1. The actual schedule and the scalar data that are kept

Fix an integer m>=128, and define

    epsilon=1/m, lambda=1+sqrt(2),
    c=lambda(1+epsilon), a=(1-epsilon)/c,
    d_j=a c^j,
    H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+d_j,H_j).

Let h=H_m, n=2^m-1, S=sum_(t<n)h_t, M=S+1. Define

    b0=1+a, b1=1+d_1=2-epsilon.

The rightmost three steps are (b0,b1,b0). Every step exceeds one.
In particular S>=n, so S tends to infinity with m. The elementary bounds
needed below are

    1<b0<sqrt(2), 1<b1<2, 7/3<c<5/2, d_1+c>3.       (1)

Indeed a<(1+sqrt(2))^(-1)=sqrt(2)-1; d_1=1-epsilon;
c>1+sqrt(2)>12/5; and, using sqrt(2)<10/7,
c<(17/7)(129/128)<5/2. Since epsilon<=1/128, these also
give d_1+c>3.

Here is a complete definition of the scalar formal transcript. Put

    M_2=1+2b0+b1, k_2=M_2+6epsilon,
    k_(j+1)=-k_j^2/d_j, j>=2.

For an H_2 block with terminal scalar gradient v, use successive gradients

    ((2-c+6epsilon/b0)v, v, c v, v),                 (2)

start at k_2 v, and use the three actual GD steps (b0,b1,b0).
The last entry in (2) is the block's endpoint gradient. Its endpoint is v,
because

    b0(2-c+6epsilon/b0)+b1+b0 c+1=k_2.

For H_(j+1), give the right H_j child terminal gradient v and the left
child terminal gradient -k_j v/d_j; start the parent at k_(j+1)v.
The left endpoint is -k_j v/d_j. The bridge 1+d_j moves it to k_j v,
the right child's required start. The right endpoint is v. This verifies
the full chronological GD recursion inductively.

Choose root terminal gradient 1. Denote the resulting scalar positions
by x_i and gradients by g_i, 0<=i<=n. Thus

    x_0=k_m, x_n=g_n=1,
    x_(i+1)=x_i-h_i g_i.

Set y_i=x_i-g_i; in particular y_n=0. No legality is asserted for these
scalar data. We keep x_i for all i, and g_i for the QUERIED indices i<n,
when adding auxiliary coordinates. The final gradient and value may be
chosen freely by a legal interpolating function.

The two final queried indices r=n-2 and s=n-1 satisfy

    g_r=1, g_s=c, x_r-x_s=b1, x_s=1+b0 c.

For the normalized interpolation residual

    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<G_i,X_j-X_i>+||G_i-G_j||^2/2,

their scalar contribution to Q_rs+Q_sr is, for EVERY choice of values,

    D=(c-1)^2+b1(c-1)=(c-1)(d_1+c)>4.              (3)

This pair is inside the queried prefix. Terminal completion cannot
delete it.

## 2. The ordered auxiliary class, including its distance convention

Work in any finite auxiliary Euclidean dimension q>=1, which may depend
arbitrarily on m. Let K be a convex cone that is acute:

    <u,v>>=0 for every u,v in K.

No assertion is made that every cone has this property. The nonnegative
orthant is an example. Consider data

    X_i=(x_i,z_i), 0<=i<=n,
    G_i=(g_i,p_i), 0<=i<n,
    z_(i+1)=z_i-h_i p_i.

Require

    z_i in K for 0<=i<=n,
    p_i in K for 0<=i<n,
    p_t-p_i in K for every 0<=t<i<n.                (4)

These are constraints on the auxiliary coordinates of one JOINT
trajectory. They do not require a separable objective, independent
one-dimensional auxiliary functions, a fixed rank, a quadratic model,
or prescribed function values. For the orthant, (4) means nonnegative
positions and nonnegative, coordinatewise nonincreasing queried gradients.

Assume these queried jets arise from ONE convex differentiable 1-smooth
function F with F(0)=0=min F. Initially write

    R^2=||X_0||^2=k_m^2+||z_0||^2,
    P=2F(X_n)/R^2.

The bound below always holds with this specified-minimizer normalization.
For a statement about the true closest-minimizer distance, explicitly
require that 0 is a closest minimizer to X_0. The constructions in
Sections 4 and 5 have a UNIQUE minimizer 0, so their R is exactly the
true distance. No automatic closest-minimizer claim is made for arbitrary
other interpolants.

The final gradient is unrestricted. In particular the class allows the
pointwise maximal unqueried-terminal completion of any legal prefix
that satisfies (4), whenever the stated normalization condition holds.

## 3. A uniform upper bound for the entire class

Put

    N=S-b0-b1=sum_(t<r)h_t,
    C=1+2b0-b0^2,
    A=2c+(b0^2-1)c^2,
    D0=2S+1-b0^2.

All of N,C,D0 are positive, and 0<A<12 by (1).

First add the two necessary interpolation inequalities for r and s.
Write Delta=p_r-p_s. The actual step has auxiliary displacement
z_r-z_s=b1 p_r. Combining its contribution with (3) gives

    D<=b1<p_r,Delta>-||Delta||^2
       =b1^2||p_r||^2/4-||Delta-(b1/2)p_r||^2.

Consequently

    ||p_r||^2>=4D/b1^2.                             (5)

This necessary bound uses no ordering assumptions and no function values.
The ordering assumption next makes the cost accumulate over the actual
earlier steps:

    z_0-N p_r=z_r+sum_(t<r)h_t(p_t-p_r) in K.

Since p_r is in K and K is acute, expansion of the squared norm yields

    ||z_0||^2>=N^2||p_r||^2,
    R^2>=4D N^2/b1^2>4N^2.                         (6)

For the objective, interpolation at the last query with the optimum and
smoothness on the actual last step give, respectively,

    F(X_s)<=<G_s,X_s>-||G_s||^2/2,
    F(X_n)<=F(X_s)-b0(1-b0/2)||G_s||^2.

These are necessary for every legal completion, with no terminal gradient
assumed. Adding and inserting the displayed scalar last-query data gives

    2F(X_n)<=A+2<p_s,z_s>-C||p_s||^2.               (7)

For t<s, acuteness and p_t-p_s in K imply
<p_t,p_s>>=||p_s||^2. Therefore

    <z_s,p_s>
      =<z_0,p_s>-sum_(t<s)h_t<p_t,p_s>
      <=<z_0,p_s>-(S-b0)||p_s||^2.

Equation (7) becomes

    2F(X_n)<=A+2<z_0,p_s>-D0||p_s||^2
             <=A+||z_0||^2/D0,                     (8)

where the final inequality is the exact completion of the square in p_s.
Dividing by R^2 and using (6) proves the uniform bound

    P <= 1/(2S+1-b0^2) + A b1^2/(4D N^2)
      < 1/(2S+1-b0^2) + 3/(S-b0-b1)^2.             (9)

Since b0,b1 stay bounded and M=S+1, it follows that

    limsup_(m->infinity) M P <= 1/2                 (10)

uniformly over all dimensions, acute cones, jointly legal function values,
and final completions in this class, with the distance convention stated
in Section 2. This is stronger in scope than a fixed-curvature analysis:
there is no curvature parameter in (5)--(10).

## 4. An explicit nonlinear repair of EVERY interpolation pair

The class in Section 2 is nonempty for the actual scalar formal transcript.
Here is an analytic construction with a closed rule and a closed initial
distance formula. It is not a nearest-feasible-point optimization.

Define finite scalars

    Gmax=max(1,max_(0<=i<=n)|g_i|),
    w_l=Gmax|y_(l+1)-y_l|+1,
    u_l=sqrt(2w_l/(h_l-1)),       0<=l<n.

Use n auxiliary coordinates, one for each chronological cut l. In
coordinate l define the 1/h_l-smooth, convex, differentiable function

    psi_l(t)=0                              if t<=0,
             t^2/(2h_l)                    if 0<=t<=h_l u_l,
             u_l t-h_l u_l^2/2              if t>=h_l u_l.

Its two joining values and derivatives agree. Its derivative is
nondecreasing and is Lipschitz with constant 1/h_l<1. Prescribe

    z_i^(l)=u_l sum_(v=i)^l h_v, p_i^(l)=u_l,   if i<=l,
    z_i^(l)=0,                  p_i^(l)=0,     if i>=l+1.

The auxiliary GD recursion holds at every step, including the extinction
step l: its position before that step is h_l u_l and its position after
the step is zero. Earlier sample positions lie in the affine branch of
psi_l. The positions and gradients lie in the nonnegative orthant, and
each gradient decreases exactly once, to zero. Thus (4) holds.

Now prescribe full-vector jets and values, including the terminal,

    X_i=(x_i,z_i), G_i=(g_i,p_i),
    f_i=g_i^2/2+sum_(l<n)psi_l(z_i^(l)), 0<=i<=n,
    X_star=G_star=0, f_star=0.                      (11)

The scalar expression g_i^2/2 is not asserted to come from a legal
one-dimensional function. The final function will be a joint interpolant.

Here is the complete interpolation law. The scalar part of Q_ij is

    g_i(y_j-y_i).

For two finite indices i<j, an auxiliary coordinate with l<i or l>=j
contributes zero in BOTH directions. A cut i<=l<j contributes

    Q_ij^(l)=-w_l,
    Q_ji^(l)=-w_l-u_l^2 sum_(v=i)^(l-1)h_v.        (12)

To verify (12), the earlier point has gradient u_l and value
u_l z_i^(l)-h_l u_l^2/2, whereas the later point has zero position,
gradient, and value. Direct substitution into Q gives the two formulas;
the sum in the second is empty if l=i.

The triangle inequality supplies

    |g_i(y_j-y_i)|, |g_j(y_i-y_j)|
       <=Gmax sum_(l=i)^(j-1)|y_(l+1)-y_l|.

By the definition of w_l, (12) therefore makes BOTH full residuals at
most -(j-i)<0. This checks every distinct finite pair, including pairs
with the terminal; it is not only an adjacent-pair check.

For the optimum pair and i<n, the scalar contributions are

    Q_(i,star)^scalar=-g_i y_i,
    Q_(star,i)^scalar=0.

Because y_n=0, |g_i y_i|<=Gmax sum_(l=i)^(n-1)|y_(l+1)-y_l|.
In each l>=i the forward auxiliary contribution is -w_l, and the reverse
one is -w_l-u_l^2 sum_(v=i)^(l-1)h_v. All l<i contributions are zero.
Thus both optimum residuals are strictly negative. At the terminal,
X_n=G_n=(1,0,...,0), f_n=1/2, and the two optimum residuals are zero.
All ordered interpolation inequalities for (11) have now been checked.

For completeness the actual function is explicit. Let

    Y_i=X_i-G_i, delta_i=f_i-||G_i||^2/2,
    H(v)=||v||^2/2+max_(i in {star,0,...,n})
                            [<Y_i,v>-delta_i],
    F_base(X)=sup_v[<X,v>-H(v)].                    (13)

By accepted fact `1252b8823bb5a290`, this is an everywhere finite convex
differentiable 1-smooth function with all the prescribed jets and the
same actual GD trajectory. It has a UNIQUE minimizer 0, as can also be
checked from (13): for i<n, the l=i term contributes w_i>0 to delta_i,
and every other term is nonnegative. Hence delta_i>0. The star and terminal
have Y=0 and delta=0. In a neighborhood of v=0 their zero affine function
strictly dominates every nonzero queried affine function. Thus H(v)
equals ||v||^2/2 in that neighborhood and its subgradient at 0 is {0}.
The maximizing-vector characterization of (13), proved in the accepted
predecessor, identifies the minimizers of F_base with this subgradient.

Consequently the true squared initial distance is exactly

    R_base^2=k_m^2+
        sum_(l=0)^(n-1) [2w_l/(h_l-1)]
                              [sum_(v=0)^l h_v]^2. (14)

The exact normalized objective is 1/R_base^2. This pure extinction repair
loses the formal large payoff: (6) yields 1/R_base^2<1/(4N^2).
Nevertheless it is a fully specified actual legal object with one common
function, all values, all gradients, and the true initial distance.

## 5. The coefficient 1/2 is sharp for the whole ordered repair class

The uniform upper bound (10) cannot be replaced by a smaller leading
constant for this class. Add one further coordinate to F_base, using the
symmetric Huber function with threshold tau>0,

    Huber_tau(t)=t^2/2                     if |t|<=tau,
                 tau|t|-tau^2/2            if |t|>=tau.

This function is convex and differentiable, its derivative is 1-Lipschitz,
and its minimizer is uniquely 0. Choose any amplitude T>0 and put

    tau=T/(2S+1), z_i^H=T-tau sum_(t<i)h_t.

All sampled positions, including the terminal, exceed tau because
z_n^H=T(S+1)/(2S+1)>tau. All its queried gradients therefore equal tau.
The actual recursion and the nonnegative ordering requirements hold.

On the enlarged space use the direct sum

    F_total(X,t)=F_base(X)+Huber_tau(t).

It is convex and 1-smooth in the product Euclidean norm, as follows by
squaring and adding the separate gradient Lipschitz inequalities. Its
minimizer is uniquely 0, and its first-coordinate queried transcript is
unchanged. Its exact normalized payoff and true distance are

    P_T=[1+T^2/(2S+1)]/[R_base^2+T^2].              (15)

For fixed m, letting T tend to infinity yields 1/(2S+1). For an explicit
horizon-dependent choice take T^2=m R_base^2, which gives

    P_T>= [m/(m+1)]/(2S+1).

Let V_m denote the supremum of the true-distance-normalized payoff over
the ordered repair class of Section 2 with 0 a closest minimizer. The
explicit unique-minimizer examples above and (9) give

    1/(2S+1) <= V_m
       <= 1/(2S+1-b0^2)+3/(S-b0-b1)^2,

and therefore the exact class asymptotic

    lim_(m->infinity) M V_m = 1/2.                 (16)

The lower endpoint is a supremum assertion; no attainment at finite T is
claimed. The explicit T choice already suffices for the limiting lower
bound. This calibrates the obstruction against an actual legal function.

## 6. What a non-ordered repair would have to change

There are exact identities that expose the assumptions used above.
For arbitrary auxiliary positions and gradients obeying GD, define

    J=sum_(t<s)h_t <p_t-p_s,p_s>.

Equation (7), with no ordering assumption, gives

    2F(X_n)<=A+2<z_0,p_s>-D0||p_s||^2-2J
             <=A+||z_0||^2/D0-2J.                 (17)

For the earlier queried gradient define

    B=<z_r,p_r>+sum_(t<r)h_t<p_t-p_r,p_r>.

The exact GD identity is

    <z_0,p_r>=N||p_r||^2+B.                        (18)

In the ordered class J>=0 and B>=0. Equations (17)--(18) hold without
that class restriction, whereas the conclusions J>=0 and B>=0 do not.
For example, retaining the unit formal terminal payoff with R^2 of order
M would require, by (5),(18) and Cauchy--Schwarz,

    B <= R||p_r||-N||p_r||^2,

which is negative for all sufficiently large m whenever R=O(sqrt(M)).
Thus such a normalization-saving repair must supply genuine cancellation
in its cumulative positions/gradients, or modify the scalar queried data.
Similarly, if R^2/M tends to infinity and a family has M P>=1+eta for
some fixed eta>0, then (17) forces

    limsup [M J/R^2] <= -1/4-eta/2.                 (19)

To check (19), divide (17) by R^2, use ||z_0||^2<=R^2,
M/D0->1/2, and M A/R^2->0. This statement is a necessary condition,
not an existence claim for an escaping legal repair.

The original all-schedule profile goal remains open. Equation (16) only
diagnoses this fully specified repair class on this critical positive
schedule. It does not address arbitrary signed or turning auxiliary
gradients, changed first-coordinate queried trajectories, all possible
minimizer locations, or unrestricted smooth convex adversaries. It does
not replace the original zero-step or large-step quantifiers, and is not
a theorem about W_n(H_m). All new conclusions here remain unverified
draft evidence, pending an actual authorized verifier submission.

# Full four-step seed: a compact domain from legal cut functions

Status: complete joint proof DRAFT, not verifier-accepted. This round has no
numerical or fact_submit allowance. No unverified finding is used as a premise.
The accepted fact1252b8823bb5a290 supplies the interpolation convention Q<=0;
the particular functions and all algebra below are derived explicitly.

## 1. Interface and energy

Let h=(h_0,h_1,h_2,h_3)>=0, s=sum h_i, C=1+s>=193/25.
Indices i,j range over0,...,4. Set p_i=1+sum_(t<i)h_t, so p_0=1,p_4=C.
Let e=(1,1,1,1,1)^T. Define T_ii=1,T_it=h_t for t<i,T_it=0 for t>i,
Lambda_ii=0,Lambda_ij>=0,L=diag(Lambda e)-Lambda,
omega=(-2h_0,-2h_1,-2h_2,-2h_3,2s)^T, and
D=diag(h_0(2-h_0),...,h_3(2-h_3),-s(s+2)).
The full interface is

    L^T e=omega, M=D+(LT+T^T L^T)/2 >=0.                 (1)

No multiplier support, rank, remainder, symmetry, or step order is fixed.
For arbitrary formal GD data x_(i+1)=x_i-h_i g_i, define

    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2,
    V=2sum_(i<4)h_i(f_i-f_4)+sum_(i<4)h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2
      -s(s+1)||g_4||^2.

Put delta_i=f_i-||g_i||^2/2 and y_i=x_i-g_i=x_0-(Tg)_i.
Then Q_ij=delta_i-delta_j+<g_i,y_j-y_i>, while
V=-omega^T delta+sum_i D_ii||g_i||^2. Value flow in(1) therefore gives

    V+sum_(i!=j)Lambda_ij Q_ij = sum_(i,j)M_ij<g_i,g_j>. (2)

In particular every feasible full certificate implies V>=0 on every legal
convex differentiable1-smooth trajectory with a minimizer. This direction
requires neither certificate necessity nor any duality assertion.

## 2. Legal cut witnesses at every position

Fix j<4, put b=h_j,P=sum_(i<j)h_i,R=sum_(i>j)h_i, and first assume b>1.
Set a=(b-1)/(R+1)>0. Define the scalar function

    f(x)=x-1/2                     if x>=1,
         x^2/2                     if -a<=x<=1,
         -a*x-a^2/2                if x<=-a.

Its derivative is clamp(x,[-a,1]), which is continuous, nondecreasing,
and1-Lipschitz. The function is convex1-smooth and has a minimizer at0.
Start at x_0=P+1. Before and at j all gradients are1, and x_j=1.
After step j, x_(j+1)=1-b=-a(R+1). All subsequent gradients equal-a,
since the remaining nonnegative steps move monotonically to x_4=-a.
Every sampled value obeys f_i=x_i g_i-g_i^2/2, and x_4=g_4.

For any vector-valued data obeying these two identities, GD telescoping gives

    2sum_(i<4)h_i f_i+sum_(i<4)h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2
      =||x_0||^2-||x_4||^2,
    V=||x_0||^2-C^2||g_4||^2.                         (3)

Applied to this scalar function, nonnegative V implies

    C(h_j-1)_+ <= (1+P)(1+R), for every j=0,...,3.      (4)

If h_j<=1 the same inequality is automatic. Thus(4) holds on every
zero-step boundary too. Failure of any inequality is a specific legal
negative-V function, hence rules out the full interface(1).

## 3. Prefix bounds and compact capacity

For fixed C>1 define the increasing rational map

    R_C(z)=1+2Cz/(C+z), z>=0,
    r_k=R_C iterated k times at1, k=0,1,2,3.

Rearrangement of(4) gives p_(i+1)<=R_C(p_i). Conversely this inequality
is equivalent to(4) on the nonnegative schedule domain. Induction yields
p_i<=r_i. The cut constraints are invariant under reversing the four
steps, regardless of whether the original certificate admits reversal.
Applying the same induction to the reversed cut inequalities gives

    p_i >= C+1-r_(4-i).                               (5)

Only the cases i=1,2,3 are needed; r_0=1. Explicitly,

    r_1=(3C+1)/(C+1),
    r_2=(7C^2+6C+1)/(C^2+4C+1),
    r_3=(15C^3+23C^2+9C+1)/(C^3+11C^2+7C+1).          (6)

Taking i=2 in(5) and p_2<=r_2 gives C+1<=2r_2, hence

    C^3-9C^2-7C-1<=0.                                (7)

This polynomial is strictly increasing for C>=7: its derivative
3C^2-18C-7 is positive there and increasing. At C=39/4 its value is
131/64>0. Since C>=193/25>7, we conclude

    193/25 <= C < 39/4.                               (8)

In particular this argument covers all larger candidate sums; it did not
replace the target by the slice sum h=168/25.

## 4. All four steps are uniformly positive and bounded

The first and last cut inequalities give

    h_0,h_3 <= 2C/(C+1) < 78/43.                      (9)

For the lower bounds, subtract the upper prefix bound from the lower
bound for the next prefix in(5). This gives

    h_0,h_3 >= C-r_3 = N(C)/(C^3+11C^2+7C+1),
    h_1,h_2 >= C+1-r_1-r_2 = N(C)/((C+1)(C^2+4C+1)), (10)
    N(C)=C^4-4C^3-16C^2-8C-1.

The endpoint lower bound exceeds1/2 exactly when

    A(C)=2C^4-9C^3-43C^2-23C-3>0.

The middle lower bound exceeds4/5 exactly when

    B(C)=5C^4-24C^3-100C^2-60C-9>0.

At C=77/10 these polynomials respectively equal1922412/10000 and
2197285/10000. They are strictly increasing for C>=7: their derivatives
are bounded below respectively by29C^2-86C-23 and68C^2-200C-60,
both positive and increasing on that interval. Since193/25>77/10,

    h_0,h_3>1/2, h_1,h_2>4/5.                         (11)

For completeness, the middle upper bound uses the same cuts, without
assuming either middle step exceeds1. Write

    G_C(z)=R_C(z)-z=1+z(C-z)/(C+z).

For1<=z<=r_1<3 its derivative is
(C^2-2Cz-z^2)/(C+z)^2>0: its numerator is at least C^2-6C-9,
which is positive for C>=15/2. Thus h_1<=G_C(p_1)<=G_C(r_1), and
the reversed cut constraints give the same bound for h_2. Calculation yields

    G_C(r_1)=4C^3/((C+1)(C^2+4C+1)).                  (12)

The right side is strictly increasing in C>0: its logarithmic derivative
has positive numerator5C^2+10C+3 after multiplication by positive
denominators. At39/4 its value is237276/92923<13/5, with the difference
21619/464615. Consequently

    1/2<h_0,h_3<78/43, 4/5<h_1,h_2<13/5.             (13)

This excludes every zero-step boundary for this threshold by actual
witnesses. It does not assert h_i>1, ordering, or reversal of certificates.

## 5. Full multiplier compactness from the common-gradient kernel

This step bounds all20 original multipliers, including every cycle.
The vector p=Te has entries p_i. Since

    sum_i D_ii=-s^2-sum_(i<4)h_i^2,
    omega^T p=s^2+sum_(i<4)h_i^2,

equations(1) give e^T M e=0. Positive semidefiniteness implies Me=0.
For clarity this implication follows by inserting e+t v into the quadratic
form: a nonzero linear coefficient would violate nonnegativity for one
sign of sufficiently small t. Calculation of each component gives

    2De+T^T omega=-diag(p)omega,
    Lp=diag(p)omega.                                  (14)

Define A_ij=Lambda_ij p_j for i!=j and A_ii=0. Since omega_i=out_i-in_i,
equation(14) says sum_j Lambda_ij p_j=p_i in_i. Therefore

    sum_j A_ij=sum_j A_ji for every i.                 (15)

Thus A is a nonnegative circulation. Write c_i=1/p_i. Substituting
Lambda_ij=A_ij c_j into the original value flow and using(15) gives

    sum_i c_i omega_i
      =sum_(i,j) A_ij(c_i c_j-c_i^2)
      =-1/2 sum_(i,j)A_ij(c_i-c_j)^2.

Equivalently, the entire circulation has the fixed Dirichlet energy

    sum_(i!=j)Lambda_ij (p_i-p_j)^2/(p_i^2 p_j)
       =4[sum_(i<4)h_i/p_i-s/C] <4s<35.              (16)

All steps exceed1/2 by(13). Consequently |p_i-p_j|>1/2 for i!=j.
Also every p_i<10 by(8), so p_i^2 p_j<1000. Every coefficient of
Lambda_ij on the left of(16) exceeds1/4000. Hence

    sum_(i!=j)Lambda_ij < 140000.                      (17)

This is a bound on the complete multiplier vector, not a choice of sparse
support. No skew freedom or PSD remainder is fixed. In particular all
possible solutions of(1) at the requested threshold belong to the closed
bounded box obtained by replacing the strict inequalities in(8),(13),(17)
by non-strict ones. The equations and PSD conditions are continuous, so
their solution set in this box is compact (possibly empty).

## 6. A suffix witness and a full two-coordinate mixed witness

The first new constraint is

    C |(1-h_2)(1-h_3)| <= 1+h_0+h_1.                  (18)

To prove it, take the1-smooth convex function with derivative min(x,1),
whose values are x^2/2 for x<=1 and x-1/2 for x>=1. Start at
x_0=1+h_0+h_1. The first two gradients are1, x_2=1, x_3=1-h_2<=1,
and x_4=(1-h_2)(1-h_3). Every sample satisfies f_i=x_i g_i-g_i^2/2.
Without imposing x_4=g_4, the same telescoping calculation as(3) gives

    V=||x_0||^2-||x_4+s g_4||^2.                     (19)

If x_4<=1, then g_4=x_4 and(19) is precisely(18). If x_4>1,
g_4=1 and x_4+s>C>x_0, since h_2+h_3>0 by(13), giving V<0.
This latter case is excluded, proving(18) for every candidate.

Here is a second necessary inequality with its complete interpolation
proof. Suppose u=h_0>1, b=h_1>1, put a=u-1,A=1+u,
R=h_2+h_3,D_tail=1+R, eta=(b-1)/D_tail, and assume a*eta<1.
Then every feasible seed satisfies

    H_mix(u,C,eta)=u^2+u-1+u(2-u^2)eta
                   -(u-1)C^2 eta^2 >=0.             (20)

Take the base cut data from Section2 with j=1. Its gradients are1
at i=0,1 and-eta at i=2,3,4; its initial point is A and its final
point and gradient are-eta. Its proximal points y_i=x_i-g_i are
y_0=u,y_1=0 and y_i=-eta*sum_(t=i)^3 h_t for i>=2.
Its energy is F_base=A^2-C^2 eta^2.

Define auxiliary scalar data by x_0=g_0=1, x_1=g_1=-a,
g_i=a*eta for i>=2, and actual GD recursion for x_i. Thus
x_2=a(b-1)=a*eta*D_tail, x_4=g_4=a*eta; all earlier auxiliary
proximal points are0 and y_i=a*eta*sum_(t=i)^3 h_t for i>=2.
Assign f_i=x_i g_i-g_i^2/2, and include the zero optimum jet.

For any data with this value assignment, direct expansion gives
Q_ij=(g_i-g_j)y_j. Optimum residuals are Q_(i,star)=0 and
Q_(star,i)=-g_i y_i. All auxiliary optimum residuals are nonpositive.
The only positive auxiliary finite residuals are, for j=2,3,

    Q_aux,(0,j)=(1-a*eta)a*eta*sum_(t=j)^3 h_t.

Every other finite residual is nonpositive: target j=0,1,4 has y_j=0;
two post-cut gradients agree; the pre-cut gradient-a is smaller than
a*eta. The corresponding base residuals are

    Q_base,(0,j)=-(1+eta)eta*sum_(t=j)^3 h_t.

With epsilon=(1+eta)/(a(1-a*eta))>0, take the orthogonal sum of the
base and sqrt(epsilon) times the auxiliary positions and gradients,
and the sum of their function values with weight epsilon. All Q add;
these two possible positive residuals cancel, and every other one remains
nonpositive. The full finite array, including the optimum, is realized
by one globally convex1-smooth function by fact1252b8823bb5a290.
Its energy is

    F_base+epsilon(1-C^2 a^2 eta^2)
       = u*H_mix(u,C,eta)/(a(1-a*eta)).                (21)

This equality follows by expanding both sides and using
1-a^2 A^2=u^2(2-u^2). All denominators are positive, proving(20).
Every original directed interpolation pair is included in this argument.

## 7. Both middle steps are strictly greater than one

For this section write a=h_0-1,b=h_1-1,c=h_2-1,d=h_3-1.
The previously proved bounds imply

    a,d<9/11; b,c<14/9; a+b+c+d=C-5>=68/25.           (22)

Indeed78/43<20/11 and237276/92923<23/9; the latter difference is
1745/836307. These bounds allow negative a,b,c,d initially.

First suppose h_1<=1, so b<=0. Then

    c+d >=68/25-a-b >68/25-9/11=523/275.

As c,d<14/9 and523/275>14/9, both c and d are positive.
The identity (14/9-c)(14/9-d)>0 implies

    cd > (14/9)(523/275-14/9)=11998/22275.

But(18) requires Ccd<=3+a+b<42/11. This is impossible, since

    (193/25)(11998/22275)-42/11=189364/556875>0.

Hence h_1>1.

Next suppose h_2<=1, so c<=0. From(22),
b>=68/25-a-d>68/25-18/11>0, so the second-step cut is available.
Its inequality is

    Cb <= (2+a)(3+c+d).

If a<=3/5, its left side is at least
(193/25)(68/25-3/5-9/11), while its right side is at most
(2+3/5)(3+9/11). The first exceeds the second by844/6875>0.
Thus a>3/5 and u=h_0 lies in(8/5,20/11).

Let C_0=193/25,D_0=42/11 and

    eta=b/(3+c+d)=(C-1-u)/(3+c+d)-1,
    eta_0(u)=(C_0-1-u)/D_0-1.

The denominator3+c+d is positive and at most D_0, while C-1-u>0.
Therefore eta>=eta_0(u)>0 on this interval. The cut itself yields

    a*eta <= a(1+u)/C=(u^2-1)/C<1.

Consequently the mixed necessary inequality(20) applies. Since
u>=8/5>sqrt(2), its coefficient u(2-u^2) is negative and u-1>0.
H_mix decreases as eta>0 increases and as C>0 increases. Thus

    0 <= H_mix(u,C,eta) <= H_mix(u,C_0,eta_0(u)).       (23)

The final expression is a rational quartic in u. Put
u=8/5+(20/11-8/5)z for0<=z<=1, and define
B_(4,k)(z)=binomial(4,k)z^k(1-z)^(4-k). Direct multiplication of the
displayed definition of H_mix gives

    H_mix(u,C_0,eta_0(u))=sum_(k=0)^4 beta_k B_(4,k)(z),

where the exact coefficients in increasing k are

    -124646528/95703125,
    -3776352043/3158203125,
    -1682896352/1654296875,
    -98764097577/127380859375,
    -36497765912/76428515625.

All are strictly negative. The Bernstein basis terms are nonnegative
and sum to1; hence this quartic is strictly negative throughout the
whole interval, contradicting(23). This proves

    h_1>1 and h_2>1.                                  (24)

No conclusion h_0>1 or h_3>1 has been assumed or obtained here.
The fixed quartic expansion is small exact algebra, not a numerical
parameter scan or a solver certificate.

## 8. General cut/quadratic repair and two additional tests

The mixed construction has a useful full-pair version at either middle
cut, retaining the entire quadratic prefix. Fix j in{1,2}, let h_j>1,
P=sum_(i<j)h_i,R=sum_(i>j)h_i,eta=(h_j-1)/(1+R), and define

    z_0=1,z_i=product_(t<i)(1-h_t) for1<=i<=j,
    q=-eta*z_j,
    beta=max(0,max_(0<=i<=j) q(z_i-q)).

Then another necessary inequality is

    beta*((1+P)^2-C^2 eta^2)
       +(1+eta)eta*(1-C^2 q^2) >=0.                  (25)

Here is a joint derivation, including beta=0. Use the cut function of
Section2 as the base. For auxiliary data set x_i=g_i=z_i for i<=j,
set g_i=q for i>j, and follow GD. The first post-cut point is
x_(j+1)=q(1+R), and x_4=q. All auxiliary proximal points before or
at j are0; later they equal q times the remaining step sum. Assign
f_i=x_i g_i-g_i^2/2 and include the zero optimum. The optimum residuals
are nonpositive. A possible positive finite residual has source i<=j
and target k>j and equals q(z_i-q) times that remaining step sum.
The corresponding base residual is-(1+eta)eta times that sum.

If beta>0, multiply the auxiliary Gram/values by
epsilon=(1+eta)eta/beta and orthogonally add to the base. Every original
residual is nonpositive; fact1252b8823bb5a290 gives one legal function.
Its energy is the left side of(25) divided by beta, proving the inequality.
If beta=0 the auxiliary data alone are legal, and their energy is
1-C^2 q^2. This proves(25) in this case too, without division by zero.
No Gram from a different schedule was reused.

Full-curvature quadratic data give the additional necessary condition

    C*|product_(i=0)^3(1-h_i)| <=1.                   (26)

This is the actual function f(x)=x^2/2 started at1; equation(3) applies.

There is also a nonlinear scalar condition that is stronger than merely
imposing(26). Since both middle steps exceed1 by Section7, put
q_mid=(h_1-1)(h_2-1)>0. Then

    C*|q_mid-h_3*min(q_mid,1)| <=1+h_0.              (27)

Use again the function with derivative min(x,1), this time started at
x_0=1+h_0. Its first step reaches1; the next two points are
x_2=1-h_1 and x_3=q_mid. Thus x_4=q_mid-h_3*min(q_mid,1).
All values have the saturated form, so(19) holds. If x_4>1 then
x_4+s>C>1+h_0 and V<0. Otherwise g_4=x_4 and(19) gives(27).
The inequality in(27) itself forces |x_4|<1, because 1+h_0<C.

The cuts yield a quantitative endpoint-sum constraint as well. Put
A=1+h_0,D_end=1+h_3,E_end=A+D_end and b=h_1-1,c=h_2-1.
Rearranging the cuts at1 and2 gives

    b<=A(C-A)/(C+A), c<=D_end(C-D_end)/(C+D_end).

The function z(C-z)/(C+z) has second derivative-4C^2/(C+z)^3<0.
Adding these two inequalities and using concavity at their midpoint yields

    C-E_end-1=b+c<=E_end*(2C-E_end)/(2C+E_end),
    E_end >= 2C(C-1)/(3C+1).                         (28)

In particular at least one endpoint exceeds1. Indeed if both were at
most1 then E_end<=4, and(28) would imply C^2-7C-2<=0. That polynomial
is positive at193/25 (value2224/625) and strictly increasing thereafter.
Thus a remaining candidate has three or four steps>1; the possible step
at most1 can only be one endpoint. No endpoint has been deleted.

## 9. A sharper capacity cap from cut products and the actual quadratic

The preceding constraints improve the capacity bound further to

    193/25<=C<87/10.                                 (29)

For a contradiction suppose C>=87/10. Define the following scalar
functions (distinct from the matrix and interpolation notation):

    d_cap(C)=(C+1)(C^2+4C+1),
    r_cap(C)=(C-1)/(C+1),
    t_cap(C)=4C^3/d_cap(C)-1,
    ell_cap(C)=2C(C-1)/(3C+1)-4,
    k_cap(C)=C-5-2r_cap(C).

Write a=h_0-1,b=h_1-1,c=h_2-1,d=h_3-1 as before. Equations(9),(12),(28)
give a,d<=r_cap, b,c<=t_cap, a+d>=ell_cap, and b+c>=k_cap.
Multiplication of the nonnegative factors r_cap-a,r_cap-d and
t_cap-b,t_cap-c yields

    ad>=r_cap*(ell_cap-r_cap),
    bc>=t_cap*(k_cap-t_cap).                         (30)

All four factors on the right are positive and increasing for C>=87/10.
Here are detailed sign bounds. Clearly r_cap>0,r_cap'>0 there, and
t_cap'>0 follows from

    t_cap'=4C^2(5C^2+10C+3)/d_cap(C)^2.

For C>=8, d_cap(C)>C^3 gives t_cap'<403/1024, while
k_cap'=1-4/(C+1)^2>=77/81; hence k_cap-t_cap is strictly increasing.
Also

    ell_cap'=(6C^2+4C-2)/(3C+1)^2>384/625,
    r_cap'=2/(C+1)^2<=2/81

for C>=8, proving ell_cap-r_cap is strictly increasing. The first lower
bound uses3C+1<=(25/8)C and6C^2+4C-2>6C^2. At C=87/10 the exact
values of r_cap,t_cap,ell_cap-r_cap,k_cap-t_cap are respectively

    77/97,
    1552559/1081453,
    19728/131435,
    7318711/10814530,

which are all positive. Positivity in(30) also forces a,d,b,c positive:
their pairwise sums are positive, and their pairwise products are positive.
Consequently the full-curvature constraint(26) requires C*ad*bc<=1.
But the increasing lower bound from(30), evaluated at87/10, exceeds1 by

    9782467331765167/1375525927835685125>0.

This proves(29) for every larger sum, without assuming saturation in(26).

The monotonic formulas(9),(12) now refine the step upper bounds to

    1/2<h_0,h_3<174/97,
    1<h_1,h_2<2634012/1081453.                        (31)

Finally p_i<9 and4s<154/5 in the exact circulation identity(16). Its
coefficients still exceed1/(4*9^3)=1/2916, so

    sum_(i!=j)Lambda_ij<449064/5<90000.               (32)

Thus(29),(31),(32) supersede the earlier coarse compact box; the sharper
pointwise rational cut bounds remain available inside that box.

## 10. Why the clipped three-step test cannot be omitted

An exact scalar example separates(27) from the preceding cuts, the
full-curvature test, the suffix pair test, and the two middle-cut repairs.
It is an explicitly constructed counterexample to the sufficiency of
that smaller necessary-condition list, not a candidate seed.

Take

    h=(1,217/100,41/20,3/2), s=168/25, C=193/25.

All four cuts(4) hold. At j=0 the left side is0. At j=1 the two sides
are22581/2500 and91/10; at j=2 they are4053/500 and417/40;
at j=3 they are193/50 and311/50. The suffix pair constraint(18)
has left side4053/1000 and right side417/100. The full-curvature
product in(26) vanishes. At both middle cuts the auxiliary prefix
product z_j is zero since h_0=1, so q=beta=0 and(25) is positive.
The first mixed test(20) requires h_0>1 and does not apply here.

Nevertheless the legal function with derivative min(x,1), started at2,
has the exact trajectory

    x=(2,1,-117/100,2457/2000,-543/2000),
    g=(1,1,-117/100,1,-543/2000).

Its energy equals

    V=4-(104799/50000)^2<0,

because104799>100000. This is precisely a violation of(27), arising
from the return to the affine part at x_3>1. It demonstrates the need
to retain nonlinear chronological transitions even after compactness,
original coefficient signs, and full-curvature screening are imposed.

## 11. Adjacent triangle witness, rederived in this joint argument

The shared UNVERIFIED C80 finding7b447329aac48edd suggested this construction.
It is awareness only: the geometry, every interpolation normal, and the
energy identity needed here are rederived in this section. The only fact
used for realization is1252b8823bb5a290.

Write h=(a,b,c,d), where b,c>1 and a,d>=0. Define

    P=a+1, B=b-1, E=c-1, D=d+1,
    U=b+c+d, C=P+U.

First suppose D>B*E and set

    Kappa=B^2*E+U*(D-B*E), t=B^2*E/Kappa,
    u=(1,0), w=(t,sqrt(t(1-t))), v=-(B*u+D*w)/c.

Kappa>0 and0<t<1. The convex hull of u,v,w contains0, since
B*u+c*v+D*w=0 with all three coefficients positive. Set

    x=(P*u,u,-B*u,D*w,w), g=(u,u,v,w,w),
    f_i=<x_i,g_i>-||g_i||^2/2,

and include the zero optimum jet. The four GD identities follow from
c*v=-B*u-D*w and D=d+1. Direct inner products give

    ||u||^2=1, <u,w>=||w||^2=t,
    <u,v>=-(B+D*t)/c, <v,w>=-(B+D)*t/c.

All sampled interpolation inequalities follow by checking that x_i-g_i
is an outward normal to this triangle at g_i. At i=0 that normal is
a*u, with nonpositive pairing with v-u and w-u. At i=1,4 it is zero.
At i=2 put y=-B*u-v. Expanding the displayed inner products gives

    <w-v,y>=(-B^2*E+Kappa*t)/c^2=0,
    <u-v,y>=-B*(1-t)*E/c<0.

For the first expansion, the coefficient of t is
D(2B+D)+c(D-BD+B)-B*c^2, which equals Kappa after substituting
E=c-1,U=B+c+D. For the second expansion the difference between the
two sides, multiplied by c^2, is-B^2*E+Kappa*t=0. At i=3 the normal
d*w pairs with u-w to zero and with v-w to
-d*t*(1+(B+D)/c)<=0. This exhausts the vertex comparisons. As0 is in
their convex hull, it also exhausts the optimum comparisons.

Indeed for these assigned values Q_ij=<g_i-g_j,x_j-g_j>, so all the
original ordered Q are nonpositive, Q_(i,star)=0, and Q_(star,i)<=0.
Fact1252b8823bb5a290 realizes one global convex1-smooth function with
a minimizer at0 and precisely these GD data. The terminal x_4=g_4=w,
so(3) gives V=P^2-C^2*t. A direct expansion gives

    C^2 B^2 E-P^2 Kappa
       =U*{B*E[P^2+B(P+C)]-P^2 D}.

Consequently feasible full certificates must satisfy

    B*E*[1+B(P+C)/P^2] <= D.                         (34)

This condition applies without imposing D>B*E in advance. If instead
D<=B*E, the legal function with derivative min(x,1), started at P,
has x=(P,1,-B,B*E,B*E-D+1) and g=(1,1,-B,1,1). Its final point
is at least1, and(19) gives

    V=P^2-[P+(B+1)(E+1)]^2<0.

Thus the complementary regime is impossible, proving(34) for every
candidate with b,c>1. The inequality itself implies D>B*E. No previous
word's Gram, an assumed primitive subword, or a numerical proposal has
been adopted in this argument.

The same construction proves(34) at any adjacent pair of steps>1, with
P=1+the sum of all earlier steps, D=1+the sum of all later steps,
B=the first selected step minus1, E=the second selected step minus1,
and C=1+the sum of the whole word. Before the selected pair every
gradient is u and each iterate is(1+the remaining prefix sum)u; its
proximal point is a nonnegative multiple of u, so the same two vertex
normal comparisons hold. After the selected pair every gradient is w
and each iterate is(1+the remaining suffix sum)w, with the same
nonnegative-multiple normal argument. All original intermediate samples
and all directed Q are thus retained. The initial point is P*u and the
terminal point is w, giving the same energy and(34). This paragraph
justifies applying the construction to the first pair as well as the
middle pair of the four-step word, without assuming either unselected
block is primitive.

## 12. The first endpoint also exceeds one

Suppose for a contradiction h_0<=1. The endpoint-sum consequence(28)
forces h_3>1. Since h_0-1<=0 and h_3-1<=r_cap(C), the same consequence
forces ell_cap(C)<=r_cap(C), or2C^3-15C^2-16C-3<=0. This polynomial
equals11/2 at17/2 and its derivative is positive for C>=7. Therefore
C<17/2. In the notation of Section11,

    1<P<=2, 2<D<3, B=h_1-1>0, E=h_2-1>0.

Rearranging the second-step cut and using monotonicity in P<=2 gives

    B<=P(C-P)/(C+P)<=2(C-2)/(C+2)<26/21.             (35)

The derivative with respect to P of P(C-P)/(C+P) has numerator
C^2-2CP-P^2>0 on C>=193/25,P<=2; the last expression increases in C.
Also(31) gives E<1552559/1081453<23/16, with the latter positive

We first show D<13/5. The suffix-pair inequality(18) becomes

    E <= (C-D)/(1+C(D-2)),                            (36)

since P+1+B=C-D-E and D>2. By the capacity identity and(35),

    E=C-P-D-1-B >= H(C)-D-1,

Therefore feasibility requires

For C>=193/25 and13/5<=D<=3, this separation expression strictly

of the final rational term with respect to C is

For C_0=193/25 the expression is concave in D: its second derivative

    F_sep(C_0,3)=1166/662175>0.

Concavity puts its value above the chord joining these positive values.
It is therefore positive throughout this interval, contradicting(37).
This proves D<13/5.

Now B+E=C-P-D-1>53/25. Since B,E<23/16, multiplication of

    B*E>6279/6400, B>273/400.

For P<=2 and C>=193/25, (P+C)/P^2>=243/100; the expression decreases

       >(6279/6400)*(106339/40000)
       =13/5+2102581/256000000 >D,

contradicting the full triangle condition(34). Hence

Together with Section7, the first THREE steps exceed1. No reversal of
the schedule or symmetry of the certificate was used.

## 13. The last endpoint also exceeds one

Suppose now h_3<=1. Set alpha=h_0-1>0,b=h_1-1>0,c=h_2-1>0,

We claim alpha<7/10. If7/10<=alpha<77/97<4/5, put

For the middle adjacent pair now put P=1+h_0=2+alpha<27/10,
D=1+h_3<=2, and x=b+c=C-P-D-1. Then

    x>101/50, c<26/21,
    821/1050<b<783/560,
    (P+C)/P^2>=1042/729.                             (40)

The lower bound on b is x-c. For the upper bound use the cut
b<=P(C-P)/(C+P); this function increases in P<=27/10 and C>=193/25
on the current rectangle, and its value at(P,C)=(27/10,17/2) is783/560.

Define the fixed cubic

    F_pair(b)=b*(101/50-b)*(1+(1042/729)b).

Since b<783/560<101/50, (40) implies

    b*c*[1+b(P+C)/P^2]>=F_pair(b).

The second derivative of F_pair is strictly decreasing in b and at
b=821/1050 equals-124646/42525<0. It is therefore concave on the

    F_pair(821/1050)-2=211017368/4219543125>0,
    F_pair(783/560)-2=266455141/439040000>0.

Thus the middle-pair left side in(34) is greater than2>=D, a contradiction.
This proves h_3>1. All four original steps therefore satisfy

    1<h_0,h_3<174/97,
    1<h_1,h_2<2634012/1081453.                        (41)

This conclusion is derived; it was not assumed during the search-domain
reduction. It uses actual legal functions with all interpolation pairs.
All prefix differences |p_i-p_j| now exceed1. Since p_i<9 and4s<154/5,

## 14. Remaining problem

The necessary cut domain is not asserted sufficient. The full original

# The released affine actual63 model: an original-row obstruction

Status: **COMPLETE JOINT UNVERIFIED PROOF CANDIDATE**. Worker max,
2026-09-30 analytical recovery round. This is one bounded recovery round
after 25 completed rounds and one separately preserved interrupted attempt.
It does not complete the interrupted attempt retroactively. There are zero
new model/solver evaluations, candidate scans, fixed numerical replays,
verifier calls, installations, or subagents in this recovery round.

The only mathematical fact used below is accepted `1252b8823bb5a290`:
the original smooth-convex interpolation inequalities and their explicit
global interpolant. The new algebra, including every auxiliary estimate,
is proved jointly here. Earlier unverified obstruction manuscripts are not
premises. Appendix A recovers the already published actual31 calibration
evidence without replaying it; none of its interval tables is needed for
the actual63 obstruction.

## 1. Result, freedoms, and limits

Take the fixed actual31 child defined in section 2. Allow independent
nonnegative spatial scales for its two copies, arbitrary linear isometric
embeddings into any common finite-dimensional Euclidean space, and their
physical relative translation determined by the actual bridge step.
Retain all original positions and queried gradients from these embeddings.
Allow every sample value, one common minimizer, and the actual final
gradient to be reselected. Keep every original interpolation pair and the
true initial-distance normalization described in section 3.

There is no such nonzero actual63 interpolable realization. More precisely,
write the squared left and right scales as ell and nu. If the original
interpolation inequalities at the auxiliary queried pairs used below hold
and the full Gram matrix is positive semidefinite, then, for ell>0,

 S_cycle = Q_(36,30)+Q_(30,31)+Q_(31,39)
                 +Q_(39,38)+Q_(38,37)+Q_(37,36)
          > (1/6) ell beta^2 e > 0.                    (1)

Here beta and e are positive and are defined in section 2. Consequently
the six inequalities in (1) cannot all hold. Every row in the proof has
both indices in {27,28,...,39}, all of which are genuinely queried at
horizon 63. There is no optimum or final-sample row in this contradiction.
This is an original-cycle/PSD obstruction obtained using auxiliary
original rows, not an assertion that the six-cycle alone is positive for
arbitrary frames without those auxiliary constraints.

When ell=0, two original rows at samples 31 and 32 force nu=0. The full
normalized model therefore has only the zero-trajectory geometric face,
with objective zero. It is not an infeasible SDP: the zero face exists.

In particular the proof never sets the relative translation to zero, never
sets nu to a prescribed multiple of ell, and never assumes projection
function values or a common child origin that is a minimizer. It proves
that the freedom released in the paid model is insufficient for these
fixed nonempty children.

This is a restriction on the displayed child geometry and joining model.
It is not an upper bound on the unrestricted W_63 of the physical word,
does not address every different child geometry, and does not settle the
original target over every nonnegative schedule and every sufficiently
large horizon. It is not a fact until accepted by the verifier. The current
TASK and guidance allocate zero verifier calls.

## 2. Exact defining data and the retained child identities

Put s=sqrt(2), lambda=1+s, theta=s-1=1/lambda, and

 a=(311s-16)/300, b=(590+8s)/300, rho=599/600,
 eps=1/100, Klen=a+rho, z_atom=b-1, eta=z_atom/Klen,
 m=a+b, A=1+2a+b=m+a+1.

The atom in R^3 is

 U=(1,0,0), V=(0,sqrt(eta),0), W=(0,rho sqrt(eta),eps),
 g=(U,U,V,W),
 x=(mU+aV+W,bU+aV+W,aV+W,W), h[0]=(a,b,a).

Define

 p=x_0, R^2=||p||^2=m^2+Klen*z_atom+eps^2,
 N=||W||^2=rho^2 eta+eps^2,
 D=<p,W>=rho*z_atom+eps^2,
 y_1=z_atom U+Klen V+eps*(0,0,1),
 y_2=(a-1)V+W,
 e=<V,y_2>=(Klen-1)eta,
 d=(1-rho)e-eps^2=e/600-eps^2.

The following atomic identities follow by substitution:

 <V-U,y_1>=0, <W-V,y_2>=-d, x_3-g_3=0.              (2)

For a nonzero vector v, define the symmetric orthogonal Householder matrix
B(v) by u=v/||v||, k=e_1-u, B(v)=I-2kk^T/(k^Tk), or B(v)=I when k=0.
It sends v/||v|| to e_1 and has first row v^T/||v||.

At depth j the child initial point is p_j, its endpoint is w_j=x_end=g_end,
its dimension is d_j=2^(j+1)+1, and its word has capacity
A_j=1+sum h[j]=lambda^j A. Set

 r_j=theta A_j, alpha_j=r_j ||w_j||/R.

For a (d_j-1)-square contraction C_j define isometries by

 L_j u=(u_l0,u_lT,0),       (u_l0,u_lT)=B(w_j)u,
 R_j v=(-v_r0,C_j v_rT,(I-C_j^T C_j)^(1/2)v_rT),
                                      (v_r0,v_rT)=B(p_j)v.

Concatenate all left samples mapped by L_j with all right samples mapped
by alpha_j R_j, and set h[j+1]=(h[j],1+r_j,h[j]). The bridge is exact since
alpha_j R_j p_j=-r_j L_j w_j. The initial norm remains R; the endpoint
norm is multiplied by alpha_j.

The released source fixes C_0=0_(2 by 2),

 C_1=(1/100)*[[7,0,7,0],[1,0,0,0],
              [18,-1,-10,0],[-17,0,-11,0]],

and C_2=(1/10000) times

 [-74,144,-360,369,107,675,1350,62;
   513,4,-187,255,-814,10,-854,-1;
   -81,-60,182,-156,21,-277,-135,-57;
   129,21,17,83,-400,-45,-370,-6;
   508,-98,-292,89,185,97,-309,6;
   -7,-1,-61,22,-64,2,-68,0;
   -300,-22,-447,97,211,270,341,35;
   40,0,-14,-18,57,-1,66,0].

Their squared Frobenius norms are 934/10000 and 5887407/10^8, both less
than one, so the positive symmetric square roots in the definition exist.
Apply the three joins j=0,1,2 to obtain the exact actual31 child
(Z_i,G_i), 0<=i<=31, in R^17. Write

 P=Z_0, w=Z_31=G_31, Y_i=Z_i-G_i,
 beta=alpha_0 alpha_1 alpha_2, v=G_4,
 r=r_3=lambda^2 A, h_parent=(h[3],1+r,h[3]).

The word h_parent has length 63. The child identities used in the proof
are the following. Their justifications use only the defining isometries
and the physical GD bridges, not child interpolation feasibility.

* Every i congruent to 3 modulo 4 has Y_i=0.
* The first atomic leaf gives P=mG_0+aG_2+G_3 and ||P||=R.
* The first right atomic leaf has
  mG_4+aG_6+G_7=-r_0 G_3 and ||v||=alpha_0.
* The last atomic leaf has
  mG_28+aG_30+G_31=-r_0 G_27.
* For some isometry J, (G_30,Y_30,w)=beta J(V,y_2,W).

Indeed each of the two displayed negative combinations equals the
initial point of the right atom at a first-level bridge; that initial
point is -r_0 times the preceding endpoint. Its atomic expression is
m times its U-gradient plus a times its V-gradient plus its W-gradient.
The last-leaf scale is the product beta of its three right-branch scales.

There is one further exact identity from C_0=0. The first join has

 <L_0 u,alpha_0 R_0 z>=-r_0<u,W><p,z>/R^2.

Taking u=p and z=U, and observing that subsequent left embeddings preserve
inner products, gives

 <P,v>=-D_v, D_v=r_0 D m/R^2,
 q_0=D_v/R^2=r_0 D m/R^4.                            (3)

Finally put c_y=<W,y_2>/N=(rho e+eps^2)/N. Orthogonal decomposition gives

 y_2=c_y W+y_perp, <y_perp,W>=0,
 ||y_perp||=(a-1)eps sqrt(eta/N).                     (4)

To obtain its norm, subtract the W component from (a-1)V and use
||V||^2-<V,W>^2/N=eta eps^2/N.

## 3. The genuinely affine model and its full normalization

Let E and F be arbitrary linear similarities from the fixed child space
R^17 into a common Euclidean space, with

 E^T E=ell I, F^T F=nu I, ell>=0, nu>=0.

The maps need not have the same scale. Their relative orientation is
unrestricted; when a scale is zero the corresponding map is zero. Define
the physical translation

 t=-r E w-F P.                                       (5)

The actual samples are

 X_i=E Z_i, g_i=E G_i,                         0<=i<=31,
 X_(32+j)=t+F Z_j, g_(32+j)=F G_j,             0<=j<=30,
 X_63=t+F Z_31, g_63=v_end free.

The bridge from X_31=Ew with gradient Ew is X_32=-r Ew, exactly as required
by step 1+r. Both child recursions remain exact. The right final gradient
is genuinely unqueried and has not been fixed to Fw.

To recover the saved full Gram program, use 36 formal vectors: the 17
columns of E, the 17 columns of F, a free optimum z_opt, and a free
v_end. Impose a positive semidefinite Gram Gamma with block constraints

 Gamma_LL=ell I_17, Gamma_RR=nu I_17;

the entire cross block and every block involving z_opt and v_end are free.
For each original sample the position coefficient rows are

 (Z_i,0,0,0),                                 0<=i<=31,
 (-r w,Z_j-P,0,0),                            i=32+j, 0<=j<=31,
 (0,0,1,0),                                  i=star=64.

The queried gradient coefficient rows are (G_i,0,0,0) on the left and
(0,G_j,0,0) on the right. Row 63 is (0,0,0,1), and the star gradient is
zero. These are exactly the coefficients generated by the saved source
and represented in its `position_coefficients` and `gradient_coefficients`
arrays. There are 65 samples and 4225 ordered rows including diagonals.

Allow all real f_i, fix f_star=0, and impose

 Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,X_j-X_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2<=0
                  for every original ordered pair,
 ||E P-z_opt||^2<=1.                                 (6)

The objective is 2 f_63. No PSD range condition is dropped. Fact
1252b8823bb5a290 supplies one global convex 1-smooth interpolant for every
feasible full Gram, after translating the star to zero. Conversely every
actual trajectory in this affine family gives these coefficient and Gram
conditions. Thus different independently chosen child functions have not
been glued without justification: all rows refer to one common function.

The norm in (6) is the actual distance to the chosen common minimizer.
For a constructed function its true nearest-minimizer distance may be
smaller, in which case (6) yields a conservative payoff. To attain the
true-distance normalization in the family, choose the nearest minimizer
and scale the entire realization by the reciprocal of that distance;
ell, nu, t, the optimum vector, gradients, and values all transform with
their appropriate first or second powers. The displayed family is closed
under this operation. There is no inherited radius from an unrelated
child optimum. For the two calibrations in section 8 the exact nearest
minimizer is identified, so their stated payoffs use true distance.

The contradiction below is stronger than any issue of normalization: it
uses only original queried rows before the optimum or terminal is used.

## 4. Rational bounds used in the separator

Squaring proves 41/29<sqrt(2)<99/70. Substitution and positive products give

 7/5<a<283/200, 2<b<401/200,
 29/5<A<6, 17/5<m<171/50,
 239/100<Klen<483/200, 1<z_atom<201/200,
 2/5<eta<201/478, 1/2<e<3/5,
 279/20<R^2<564943/40000<71/5,
 39/100<N<421/1000, D>99/100.                         (7)

For example e=z_atom(1-1/Klen) is less than
(201/200)(283/483)<3/5 and greater than (139/100)(2/5)>1/2.
The lower R^2 bound is (17/5)^2+239/100=279/20;
the upper bound is (171/50)^2+(483/200)(201/200)+1/10000.
The N bounds follow from rho>99/100 and the eta bounds.

These imply

 0<d<1/1000, d/e<1/600,
 12/5<r_0<5/2, alpha_0^2<1/5, alpha_0<1/2,
 841/25<r<36,
 q_0>5049/126025>1/25,
 0<c_y<8/5, 1+c_y/r<21/20.                           (8)

For d use e>1/2, eps^2=1/10000, and e<3/5. For alpha_0^2 use
(5/2)^2(421/1000)/(279/20)<1/5. For r use lambda^2>29/5
and A>29/5; for q_0 use (12/5)(99/100)(17/5)/(71/5)^2.
For c_y use (3/5+1/10000)/(39/100)<8/5 and r>33.

Define four dimensionless constants

 C=A+r_0, K=C-1,
 c_L=(A-1)/C, c_R=(A-1)alpha_0^2/C.

Since C=sqrt(2)A, we have

 C>8, K<8, c_L<5/8, c_R<1/8, K c_R<1.              (9)

Also

 (a+1+r_0)/m<3/2, a alpha_0^2/m<1/10.               (10)

The first follows from (283/200+1+5/2)/(17/5)<3/2;
the second follows already from (3/2)(1/5)/(17/5)<1/10.
These are rational inequalities, not results of a new numerical replay.

## 5. Intrinsic heights and how original rows price translation

For each queried left sample define

 aheight_L,i=f_i-<E G_i,E Z_i>+||E G_i||^2/2,

and for each queried right local sample j define

 aheight_R,j=f_(32+j)-<F G_j,F Z_j>+||F G_j||^2/2.

These are intrinsic heights, so the right translation is not included
inside aheight_R,j. Expanding the ORIGINAL rows yields

 Q_(i,k)=aheight_L,i-aheight_L,k+ell<G_i-G_k,Y_k>,
 Q_(32+j,32+l)=aheight_R,j-aheight_R,l+nu<G_j-G_l,Y_l>,
 Q_(i,32+j)=aheight_L,i-aheight_R,j+<E G_i,t>
                                      +<E G_i-F G_j,F Y_j>,
 Q_(32+j,i)=aheight_R,j-aheight_L,i-<F G_j,t>
                                      +<F G_j-E G_i,E Y_i>.
                                                               (11)

Suppose for contradiction that all original queried rows are nonpositive.
The zero-residual anchors 27 and 31 have equal left height; call it H_L.
The right anchors 3 and 7 (global 35 and 39) have equal right height; call it
H_R. Both equalities follow from the two opposite original rows, whose
normals are zero. Put Delta=H_R-H_L.

The three reverse atomic paths have precise costs from (2):

 aheight_L,28,aheight_L,30 >= H_L-ell beta^2 d,
 aheight_R,0,aheight_R,2   >= H_R-nu d,
 aheight_R,4,aheight_R,6   >= H_R-nu alpha_0^2 d.      (12)

For the first line the actual path is 31->30->29->28. Its first normal is
-ell beta^2 d and its next two are zero, so each displayed lower bound
follows separately. The other paths are 35->34->33->32 and
39->38->37->36. In particular no unqueried intrinsic endpoint 31 of the
right child is used.

Cross rows into the right anchor 3 and into the left anchor 31 imply

 <E G_i,t> <= H_R-aheight_L,i,
 <F G_j,t> >= aheight_R,j-H_L.                       (13)

Apply the first inequality to the positive dependency
r_0 G_27+mG_28+aG_30+G_31=0, using (12). Apply the second to
r_0 G_3+mG_4+aG_6+G_7=0. Their sums give

 -c_L ell beta^2 d <= Delta <= c_R nu d.              (14)

This is the step absent from the empty-child translation example. The
nonempty children carry queried positive gradient dependencies and small
internal height costs. Their values cannot be shifted independently of
the translation.

The same rows give two quantitative projection bounds. Since
-w=mG_28+aG_30+r_0 G_27, (12)-(14) yield

 <Ew,t> >= -K Delta-(A-1)ell beta^2 d
          >= -K c_R nu d-(A-1)ell beta^2 d
          > -nu d-5ell beta^2 d.                    (15)

Since P=mG_0+aG_2+G_3, the right first-atom path gives

 <FP,t> >= A Delta-(A-1)nu d.                        (16)

Finally -m v=aG_6+G_7+r_0 G_3 and the right second-atom path give

 <Fv,t> <= -((a+1+r_0)/m)Delta
                     +(a alpha_0^2/m)nu d
          <= (15/16)ell beta^2 d+(1/10)nu d.          (17)

Here (14), c_L<5/8, and (10) were used. Combining (15),(17) with (8)
gives the bound needed in the actual cycle:

 <Ew,t>-(1+c_y/r)<Fv,t>
       > -(6/5)nu d-6ell beta^2 d.                  (18)

Indeed the unsimplified coefficients are
1+(21/20)(1/10)=221/200<6/5 and
5+(21/20)(15/16)=1915/320<6. The upper bound on <Fv,t>
is positive, so replacing its positive coefficient by 21/20 preserves
the stated lower bound even when <Fv,t> itself is negative.

## 6. A necessary two-scale rule, without imposing origin alignment

Assume ell>0 and define T=nu/(ell beta^2). The bridge (5) gives

 nu R^2=r^2 ell beta^2 N+2r<Ew,t>+||t||^2.

Use nonnegativity of ||t||^2 and the second line of (15), before its
coarse simplification. Then

 T >= [r^2 N-2r(A-1)d]/[R^2+2rKc_R d].              (19)

The numerator is positive. By (7)-(9),

 2(A-1)d/(rN)<1/1000,
 2rKc_R d/R^2<3/500.

For the first inequality use rN>10, 2(A-1)<10, and d<1/1000.
For the second use r<36, Kc_R<1, and R^2>279/20.
Consequently

 T > (999/1006)(r^2 N/R^2)
   > (99/100)(r^2 N/R^2)>0.                         (20)

In particular nu=0 is impossible when ell>0.

The companion identity ||FP+t||^2=r^2 ell beta^2 N and (16) imply

 nu R^2 <= r^2 ell beta^2 N-2A Delta+2(A-1)nu d.

Use the lower bound in (14) to obtain

 T <= [r^2 N+2A c_L d]/[R^2-2(A-1)d] < 40.          (21)

The denominator is positive. To check the final bound, its numerator is
less than 36^2(421/1000)+1/100<546, while its denominator is
greater than 279/20-1/100>139/10; thus T<5460/139<40.

Equations (19)-(21) are necessary consequences of actual queried rows
and the PSD norm identities. They do not restrict the model by decree.
They quantify why an arbitrary free right scale cannot escape the old
scale regime while retaining these children and all their values.

## 7. The original six-cycle with the translation retained

Sum precisely the six original rows displayed in (1). Their heights
cancel. The anchor receiving normals at left 31 and right local 7 are
zero. The right reverse path has cost -nu alpha_0^2 d. Formula (11) gives
the exact identity, valid before any sign assertion,

 S_cycle=<Fv,EY_30>-ell beta^2 e
                       +<Ew-Fv,t>-nu alpha_0^2 d.   (22)

The translation term is present. In particular (22) is not the previous
fixed-origin cycle formula.

By (4), Y_30=c_y w+beta J y_perp. Substituting
rEw=-t-FP into its w component, and using (3), gives

 S_cycle=(c_y D_v/r)nu-ell beta^2 e
         +<Ew,t>-(1+c_y/r)<Fv,t>
         -nu alpha_0^2 d+<Fv,E(beta J y_perp)>.      (23)

The remaining cross term is controlled by the full Gram PSD condition,
equivalently Cauchy--Schwarz in any realization:

 <Fv,E(beta J y_perp)>
       >= -sqrt(ell nu) alpha_0 beta
                          (a-1)eps sqrt(eta/N).    (24)

Let E_rec=ell beta^2 e>0. Since T<40<49, alpha_0<1/2,
a-1<1/2, eps=1/100, sqrt(eta/N)<1/rho<100/99 and e>1/2,
the absolute value of the right side of (24), divided by E_rec, is

 sqrt(T)alpha_0(a-1)eps sqrt(eta/N)/e
       < 7/198 < 1/25.                              (25)

The positive term in (23) satisfies, by (20),(3),(4),(7),(8),

 (c_y D_v/r)nu/E_rec
   = (T/r)(c_y D_v/e)
   > (99/100)r(rho+eps^2/e)q_0
   > (99/100)^2 (841/25)(1/25)
   = 8242641/6250000 > 131/100.                      (26)

The total d-cost in (23), after applying (18), is strictly less than

 [(alpha_0^2+6/5)T+6](d/e)
      < [(7/5)40+6]/600=31/300.                    (27)

Combine (23)-(27), including the receiving term -E_rec. The strict lower
bound is

 S_cycle/E_rec > 131/100-1-1/25-31/300=1/6.

This proves (1) and contradicts nonpositivity of all six original rows.
The proof has used only the original queried rows and positivity of norms
from the full Gram. It neither needs nor alters any optimum/terminal data.

For ell=0, E=0 and both positions X_31 and X_32 are zero. Their gradients
are 0 and F G_0. Adding Q_(31,32) and Q_(32,31) gives
||FG_0||^2=nu, since ||G_0||=1. Thus nu=0. All queried positions and
gradients, and the final position, then vanish. Original rows between the
final sample and a queried sample force v_end=0; the star rows and equal
sample positions force every sampled value to be zero. A constant function
realizes this face. A PSD Gram with a free optimum can represent it with
||z_opt||<=1. There is no positive-gap trajectory on this face. Its true
nearest-minimizer distance is zero, so it is a conic degeneracy, not a
normalized positive-distance adversary.

## 8. Recovery of the two paid calibrations

### 8.1 Actual31 from the two nonempty actual15 children

The defining source is `max_affine_gluing_diagnostic.py`. Its first charged
case used the depth-2 child in R^9, the actual bridge 1+r_2, and the exact
depth-3 construction of section 2. In the full affine Gram model take

 E=L_2/R, F=alpha_2 R_2/R, z_opt=0,
 v_end=alpha_2 R_2 w_2/R,
 f_i=(<Z_i,G_i>-||G_i||^2/2)/R^2.

Its translation is zero because alpha_2 R_2 p_2=-r_2 L_2 w_2. The matrix
of these actual vectors factors the full Gram, so its PSD and all range
conditions are included. Appendix A reproduces the atom, seven-step,
fifteen-step and thirty-one-step original-row proof, complete cross-row
tables, and the exact enclosure rule already saved before the pause.
This recovery copies that earlier evidence; it does not rerun or claim an
independent verification of those interval calculations.

In that proof Kpoly=conv(0,G_0,...,G_31) and

 Fpoly(x)=max_(g in Kpoly)(<x,g>-||g||^2/2)

is one global convex 1-smooth function with gradient the projection onto
Kpoly. The displayed normals prove that it interpolates every sample.
Its minimizers are the polar of Kpoly. Because
P=mG_0+aG_2+G_3 is a positive combination of queried gradients,
<P,z><=0 for every minimizer z, and
||P-z||^2>=||P||^2=R^2, with equality at zero. Thus R is the TRUE
initial distance, not a distance imported from a child optimum.

Put kappa=A^2 N/R^2. Summing the actual words gives A_j=lambda^j A and
the endpoint recurrence N_(j+1)=r_j^2 N_j^2/R^2 gives
A_j^2 N_j/R^2=kappa^(2^j). The exact normalized actual31 payoff is

 2Fpoly(Z_31)/R^2=N_3/R^2=kappa^8/(lambda^3 A)^2.

Its benchmark multiple is B_31 times this expression, with B_31 the
original all-split benchmark, not a surrogate mass. This is a calibration
of the affine representation; it supplies no actual63 construction.

### 8.2 A genuinely translated nonempty scalar calibration

The intrinsic child has (Z_0,Z_1)=(9/8,5/8),
(G_0,G_1)=(1,5/8), and word (1/2). Take left scale one, signed right
similarity F=-5/16, bridge 2, and append one zero step. The physical
translation is

 t=-5/8-(-5/16)(9/8)=-35/128.

The full word and the actual data are

 h=(1/2,2,1/2,0),
 X=(9/8,5/8,-5/8,-15/32,-15/32),
 g=(1,5/8,-5/16,-25/128,-25/128).

Define mu=3/4, z=-5/24 and f(x)=(mu/2)(x-z)^2. Direct substitution
gives f'(X_i)=g_i for all five samples and the displayed exact GD updates.
This single function is convex, 1-smooth, and uniquely minimized at z.
For ANY two samples, including z, expansion gives

 Q_ij=-(mu(1-mu)/2)(X_i-X_j)^2=-(3/32)(X_i-X_j)^2<=0.

The true initial distance is 9/8+5/24=4/3, and the exact payoff is

 2(f(X_4)-f(z))/(4/3)^2=1875/65536.

After division by 4/3, the full one-row vector factor for the frame,
optimum and free terminal Gram is

 (3/4,-15/64,-5/32,-75/512),

exactly the saved calibration factor. Every original row and the free
terminal entry is accounted for. This is a nonempty translated legal
join, showing why imposing t=0 would have been invalid. It has no pair
of internally joined atomic leaves with the positive dependencies used
in (14), so it does not contradict the precisely scoped obstruction.

## 9. Recovery record and implications

The owner ledger and the pause overlay are preserved byte for byte:

* `max_affine_gluing_budget.json`: SHA256
  `dad11fbdc16276884f6c265000186e619c56e73d55b5e3adec51c6c1092b2ee4`.
* `max_affine_gluing_pause_20260927.json`: SHA256
  `8204b8f8f14145d7c9871a98ea68c457d9231915f997b87e071426fe095ee837`.

The ledger retains three charged cases and 35.483243705006316 seconds.
The fourth case remains reserved and unreleased in this wave. The raw
NPZ hashes read during recovery are:

* attempt_01: `963d22885b7b743ff436e3595e4d6dfdf710877d5cd23b009b4230ab62bb3621`;
* attempt_02: `077c9f7e9c0c66b4f065f3249ea9f5a6d2e7763406fc36ff5ff669dce6ce3e7f`;
* attempt_03: `6cdf1577e25890da76ed615fe261c141b4954f1479acfbe70d255b5b03b6296a`.

Only stored text, archive headers, and file hashes were read. No arrays
were reevaluated, decomposed, repaired, scanned for a better candidate,
or used as proof of a numerical optimum. The saved actual63 `user_limit`
output had maximum original Q 0.008512935847802695, minimum Gram
eigenvalue -0.003133609315037855, and even a negative reported translation
squared norm. Its positive objective 0.009036192607739111 is neither a
feasible witness nor a separating certificate. The current obstruction is
the independent analytical original-row argument above.

The two recovered plans are resolved as follows at joint-draft level:
the original-cycle route yields the obstruction (1), and a nonzero
actual63 construction within the released fixed-child affine model is
excluded by it. There is no quantitative positive next-state payoff in
that model to extrapolate. The necessary scale rule (19)-(21) instead
explains why the released scales and translations cannot rescue it.

A further construction must change some actual queried-child feature:
the internal positive dependencies, the small atomic return cost d,
the endpoint residual geometry, or the first-coupling correlation (3).
Simply freeing the top scales, top orientation, relative translation,
all values, one optimum or final gradient has now been tested jointly.
No conclusion is drawn about arbitrary different children, nonlinear
deformations of their queried data, or other actual schedules.

The nonempty scalar calibration and the lower-horizon actual31
calibration were used to attack overbroad interpretations. The former
lacks the internal dependencies; the latter has the smaller bridge r_2
and hence does not satisfy the large-r bound in (8). The degenerate
zero-scale face has also been separated explicitly. These checks are
analytical and do not substitute for independent verifier acceptance.

## Appendix A. Recovered actual31 calibration evidence

The following is copied from the previously shared
`max24_released_values_cycle63.md`, Appendix A, including its exact
arithmetic procedure and all four original-row tables. It is included
as defining proof evidence, remains unverified, and was not rerun in this
analytical recovery. Its internal section references refer to that
manuscript; the same atom, maps, and bounds are also defined above.



This appendix contains the full finite interpolation check needed for
the analytical calibration. Its interval tables are copied defining
evidence from the existing exact31 manuscript, without executing their
source or recalculating them. The logical arithmetic enclosure procedure
is stated explicitly below. This is part of the present UNVERIFIED joint
candidate; those inherited tables have not acquired verifier status.
The63 separator in sections4-7 does not need any table in this appendix.

### A1. Atomic normal list, function, and distance

Use exactly the atom in section2. Its residuals are
y_0=(m-1)U+Klen V+eps*(0,0,1),
y_1=z_atom U+Klen V+eps*(0,0,1),
y_2=(Klen-1)V+eps*(0,0,1),y_3=0.
Its normal energies are(m-1,z_atom,(Klen-1)eta,0).
At receiving index0, the vertices0,V,W give respectively
-(m-1),-a,-a-(1-rho)z_atom+eps^2; vertex U gives0.
At receiving index1, vertices0,V,W give
-z_atom,0,-(1-rho)z_atom+eps^2; vertex U gives0.
At receiving index2, vertices0,U both give-(Klen-1)eta,
vertex W gives-(1-rho)(Klen-1)eta+eps^2, and V gives0.
At receiving index3 every normal is0. The elementary estimates in
section6 give e>1/2,z_atom>1 and rho=599/600, so both possible small
positive terms eps^2 are strictly smaller than their subtracted terms.
Thus all atomic normals are nonpositive, and all energies nonnegative.

For any finite vector/position data with these two properties set
Kpoly=conv(0,g_0,...,g_n) and
Fpoly(x)=max_(v in Kpoly)(<x,v>-||v||^2/2).
Completing the square shows that the unique maximizing vector is the
Euclidean projection P_Kpoly(x). The normal inequalities at x_i prove
P_Kpoly(x_i)=g_i. If p=P_Kpoly(x) and q=P_Kpoly(y), the two projection
inequalities give ||p-q||^2<=<p-q,x-y>; hence this projection is
1-Lipschitz. Evaluating each defining maximum at the other's maximizing
vector bounds the differentiability remainder between0 and||x-y||^2.
Thus Fpoly is finite, convex, differentiable, and1-smooth, with gradient
P_Kpoly. At the data points its values are
f_i^proj=<x_i,g_i>-||g_i||^2/2.
Expanding the original Q gives Q_ij=<g_i-g_j,y_j>,
Q_(i,star)=0, and Q_(star,i)=-<g_i,y_i>. This covers both optimum
directions, not only the finite/finite normals.

The minimizer set is Kpoly^polar={u:<u,v><=0 for all v in Kpoly}.
For necessity, use tv in the defining maximum for0<t<=1, divide by t,
and let t decrease to0; sufficiency follows by evaluating the maximum.
The identity p=mU+aV+W is a positive combination of retained gradients.
It persists through every later left embedding. Thus <p,u><=0 for any
minimizer u, and ||p-u||^2>=||p||^2 with equality at u=0. This proves
the exact closest-minimizer distance R at every finite calibrated join.

### A2. The seven-step join

For C_0=0 the exact cross pairing of section2 is
<L_0 u,alpha_0 R_0 v>=-r_0<u,W><p,v>/R^2.
All seed coordinates in the four lists g_i,y_i,p,W are nonnegative.
Therefore each cross inner product between a gradient and a residual
is nonpositive. Subtracting the receiving energy proves every cross
normal. All within-child and star rows are atomic rows or their
alpha_0^2 multiples. Applying A1 realizes the full7 word, including
both endpoint directions, with the retained projection values.

### A4. Exact enclosure procedure specifying the tables

For the copied15 tables use Dint=10^18, and for the31 tables use
Dint=10^24, as in their original recorded calculations. Represent a real
enclosure by two integers[l,u], meaning[l/Dint,u/Dint]. Integers k are
[kDint,kDint].
Addition adds endpoints; negation maps[l,u] to[-u,-l]. Multiplication
takes the min and max of the four integer endpoint products, divides
by Dint, and rounds outwards. Division by an interval avoiding0 takes
the min and max of the four endpoint quotients a*Dint/b and rounds
outwards. Square root maps a nonnegative interval to
[floor(sqrt(lDint)),ceil(sqrt(uDint))], using integer squaring to fix
the endpoints. Each operation encloses the exact result by endpoint
monotonicity or by taking all endpoint extrema.

For the15 rows it suffices to evaluate the exact atom, B(W),B(p),
alpha_0, all7 vectors, then B(w_1),B(p_1),alpha_1 and(AT). For the31
rows one additional square root is needed for the actual15 input:
H_1=(I-C_1^T C_1)^(1/2). Let Dmat=C_1^T C_1. Its operator norm is at
most934/10000<1/10. The scalar binomial series and real symmetric
diagonalization give

 H_1=I-sum_(k>=1)c_k Dmat^k,
 c_1=1/2, c_(k+1)=c_k(2k-1)/(2k+2), 0<c_k<1.

After20 terms the omitted tail has operator norm at most
sum_(k>=21)(1/10)^k=1/(9*10^20). This also bounds every entry.
Evaluate those20 matrix powers with the outward operations and add
this rational radius to every entry. Form the EXACTLY prescribed15
vectors with these H_1 enclosures, then their two Householder frames,
alpha_2 and both directions of(AT). Normalize by the explicit positive
energies and round each upper endpoint upwards to the grid1/10000.
The resulting bounds are the four displayed tables. No spectral
truncation or alternate Gram square root is substituted.

Every denominator is separated from0 by the explicit nonzero axes and
positive energies; the source's complete interval endpoints are in
max_changed_geometry31_exact_replay.json. A1-A2 prove the exact zero
rows without interval tests. At15 the96 nonzero cross rows are strictly
negative, with largest upper bound-2/10000; the remaining32 cross rows
are zero. At31 the384 nonzero cross rows have largest upper bound
-4037/10000; the remaining128 are zero. The512 within-child rows and65
optimum incidences including the diagonal total1089 original31 rows
with the512 cross rows. All are covered by A1-A4.

Thus the calibration invokes an explicit global interpolant with all
original rows and an exact radius, as used in section8. The present
round copies the already recorded finite rational enclosure evidence
and its complete reasoning; it does not claim to have executed or
independently verified the arithmetic replay.
