# Two actual children in one projection body

Status: joint proof draft, **UNVERIFIED**. Eighth launch, cumulative round18.
No solver, parameter sweep, installation or verifier was used. The original
all-schedule exact-profile target is unresolved. Accepted facts
1252b8823bb5a290 and b98bb807d514e80d specify the original interpolation model,
performance W_n, and exact comparison B_n; every new construction below is
derived here. Earlier unverified packages are not premises.

## 1. Full input, function, values, and true distance

A child consists of a nonnegative finite word p of length ell, actual vectors
x_i,g_i for 0<=i<=ell, and the optimum sample x_*=g_*=0. Set

    K=conv(0,g_0,...,g_ell), y_i=x_i-g_i,
    x_(i+1)=x_i-p_i g_i,
    <g_j-g_i,y_i><=0 for every i,j,
    <g_i,y_i>>=0 for every i,
    x_ell=g_ell=w !=0.                                      (1)

Include ALL samples, even duplicated ones or those at zero steps. Nonzero
residuals y_i are permitted. Set

    A=1+sum p_i, R_i=sum_(t=i)^(ell-1) p_t,
    R^2=||x_0||^2, Delta=A^2||w||^2-R^2,
    kappa=A^2||w||^2/R^2.

The values and common function are specified, not freely shifted:

    F_K(x)=max_(g in K)(<x,g>-||g||^2/2),
    f_i=<x_i,g_i>-||g_i||^2/2, f_*=0.                        (2)

The maximizer is the unique Euclidean projection onto K: completing the
square proves this, and its exact optimality condition is (1). Adding the
two projection normals for x,x' proves
||P_K(x)-P_K(x')||^2<=<x-x',P_K(x)-P_K(x')>. Thus it is 1-Lipschitz.
Evaluation of the defining maxima at each other's maximizers bounds the
differentiability remainder by ||x-x'||^2. Consequently F_K is finite,
convex, differentiable and 1-smooth, with gradient P_K and values (2).

The minimizer set is exactly K^polar={z:<z,g><=0 for every g in K}.
The forward implication follows by using epsilon*g in (2) and sending
epsilon down to0; the reverse implication follows directly from (2).
The endpoint and GD identity give

    x_0=w+sum_(i<ell) p_i g_i.                              (3)

Hence <x_0,z><=0 for every minimizer z, so 0 is a closest minimizer and
dist(x_0,argmin F_K)^2=R^2. No arbitrary chosen-optimum normalization is used.
For the original signed interpolation residual of fact1252b8823bb5a290,
substitution of (2) gives the identity

    Q_ij=<g_i-g_j,y_j>.                                    (4)

This includes Q_i*=0 and Q_*i=-<g_i,y_i><=0. Thus checking (1) checks EVERY
original interpolation inequality, including both optimum incidences.

The input contains these vectors and values, not merely Delta or kappa.

## 2. A real cross Gram block and its exact legality conditions

Take two inputs L,R with capacities A,C, initial squared distances R_L^2,
R_R^2, and endpoints w_L,w_R. Choose a bridge 1+r with r>0. Write
e=w_L/||w_L|| and d=x_R,0/R_R. Embed the two spaces in
R direct-sum e^perp direct-sum d^perp by

    J_L u=(<u,e>, u-<u,e>e, 0),
    J_R v=(-<v,d>, 0, v-<v,d>d),
    a_scale=r||w_L||/R_R.                                 (5)

Both J are real isometries. Use J_L x_L,i,J_L g_L,i for the complete left
transcript and a_scale J_R x_R,j,a_scale J_R g_R,j for the complete right
transcript. The bridge is exact because

    J_L w_L-(1+r)J_L w_L=-r J_L w_L=a_scale J_R x_R,0.

All child updates are unchanged. The endpoint is a_scale J_R w_R. The
cross Gram block, on any pair of child vectors, is explicitly

    <J_L u,a_scale J_R v>
       =-r <u,w_L><v,x_R,0>/R_R^2.                         (6)

Real PSD follows from (5), not from an unspecified positive block matrix.
Left values are unchanged; right values are a_scale^2 f_R,j. Let the parent
body be the convex hull of0 and EVERY displayed parent gradient, including
the left bridge gradient J_L w_L and the terminal gradient.

Within-child and optimum normals remain (1), with the right ones scaled
by a_scale^2. The only additional inequalities are, for every i,j,

    -r <g_R,j,x_R,0><w_L,y_L,i>/R_R^2
          <= <g_L,i,y_L,i>,                               (7L)
    -<g_L,i,w_L><x_R,0,y_R,j>
          <= r||w_L||^2 <g_R,j,y_R,j>.                     (7R)

These follow by substituting (6) into the two cross normals; conversely they
are those normals. They include every old gradient against every new residual
and the bridge gradient against all right samples. Thus (7L)-(7R) are exact
necessary and sufficient conditions for this SPECIFIED function and data.
When they hold, (2)-(4) provide the common function, values, all Q inequalities,
and true initial distance R_L, since (3) uses retained left gradients.

The physical parent capacity is P=A+C+r. Its exact ratio is

    kappa_P=P^2 r^2 kappa_L kappa_R/(A^2 C^2).              (8)

For the source primitive bridge rP=AC, equivalently
r=(-A-C+sqrt((A+C)^2+4AC))/2, this is

    kappa_P=kappa_L kappa_R,
    Delta_P=R_L^2[(1+Delta_L/R_L^2)(1+Delta_R/R_R^2)-1].    (9)

The cross term in (9) is an exact consequence of a legal construction ONLY
when (7) holds. No favorable interaction is presumed from two scalar defects.

## 3. The planar core blocks even arbitrary transverse correlations

Here is the previously detected planar core, rederived. Set s=sqrt2,
lambda=1+s, theta=s-1. At its source word (s,2,s), take

    U=1/lambda^2, eta=-(1/2+theta)/s,
    u=(1/lambda,0), v=(eta,sqrt(1/2-eta^2))/lambda,
    w=-u-s v,
    (x_i,g_i)=((lambda u,u),(u,u),(-u,v),(w,w)).             (10)

We have eta=-1+1/(2s), so eta^2<1/2. The residuals are s u,0,-u-v,0.
At sample0 all four vertex normals against0,u,v,w are nonpositive:
<v,u>=eta U<U and <w,u>=(s-3/2)U<U. At sample2,
<v,y>=(1/2-1/(2s))U>0, the u normal is -U/2, and
<w-v,y>=0 by direct substitution. The zero normal is -<v,y><0.
Thus (10) satisfies the full input contract. It has R^2=1,
||w||^2=1/lambda^4, A=lambda^2 and kappa=1.

Consider ANY isometric endpoint-aligned splice with this right child,
regardless of how transverse coordinates are correlated. The actual bridge
gradient necessarily represents -x_R,0/r in right-child units. At right
sample2 its required normal is

    <-x_R,0/r-v,y_2><=0,
    r >= -<x_R,0,y_2>/<v,y_2>=lambda^2=A.                  (11)

The equality follows from
-<x_R,0,y_2>=lambda(1+eta)U and
<v,y_2>=theta(1/2)U/s. The balanced primitive bridge is r=theta A<A.
It therefore fails this ORIGINAL cross-child inequality even at zero defect.
Changing the remaining cross Gram block cannot affect (11).

For completeness the actual modified planar cores use
p0=(16-11s)/3, q0=(8s-10)/3, 0<=t<=1/50,
a=s-p0 t, b=2+q0 t, z=b-1, A=1+2a+b,

    U=1/(a+1)^2,
    eta=-[z^2+(a+1)/2]/[z(a+2)],
    u=(1/(a+1),0), v=(eta,sqrt(1/2-eta^2))/(a+1),
    w=-z u-a v,
    (x_i,g_i)=(((a+1)u,u),(u,u),(-z u,v),(w,w)).             (12)

Squaring rational endpoints gives 1.4142<s<1.4143. It follows that
1.411<a<1.415, 1<=z<1.01, -.654<eta<-.639,
-.1<<u,w>/U<0, -.062<<v,w>/U<0. Also
||w||^2=J U with
J=[z^2(2-a)+a(a^2-2)/2]/(a+2), and .169<J<.177.
These estimates follow by separately bounding the displayed numerators;
in particular .654^2<1/2 proves a real Gram realization. At sample0 the
u-normal tests hold by these correlations. At sample2 the normals against0
and u are (z eta+1/2)U<0 and
[-z+(z-1)eta+1/2]U<=-U/2; the w normal is identically0.
Samples1 and3 have zero residual. Thus these are actual cores, not formal jets.

Writing N=z^2(2-a)+a(a^2-2)/2 and mu=q0-2p0=10s-14>0,
differentiation of A^2 J/(a+1)^2 gives logarithmic derivative

    2mu/A+2p0/(a+1)+N'/N+p0/(a+2),
    N'=2z(2-a)q0-p0[-z^2+(3a^2-2)/2].

The rational bounds .147<p0<.149, .437<q0<.439, .578<N<.603
give N'>.361 and the displayed logarithmic derivative>3/4.
Its initial value is1; integration proves kappa>=1+3t/4.
These defective inputs do not repair the alignment obstruction:
-<x_0,y_2>/<v,y_2>=(a+1)(z+eta)/(-z eta-1/2)>4.97,
whereas the balanced source bridge theta A<2.43. The ratio bound uses
(a+1)(z+eta)>2.411*.346 and -z eta-1/2<1.01*.654-.5.
Thus this entire parameter interval fails the bridge normal, independently
of any free transverse correlation.

## 4. A second actual core retains its nonzero first post-step residual

For arbitrary a>=0, b>1 put c=a, H=a+1, z=b-1, xi=z/H>0, and take
orthogonal vectors U=(1,0), V=(0,sqrt(xi)). The word is (a,b,a), and

    x_0=(H+z)U+H V,       g_0=U,
    x_1=b U+H V,         g_1=U,
    x_2=H V,             g_2=V,
    x_3=V,               g_3=V.                            (13)

The body is conv(0,U,V). At sample0 the V normal is z-(a+b-1)=-a.
At sample1 it is z-(b-1)=0. At sample2 the residual is aV and its U and0
normals are -a||V||^2. At sample3 the residual is0. At samples0 and1,
the zero normals are -(a+b-1) and -(b-1). This lists every vertex normal.
The value formula (2) and true distance proof (3) apply; in particular
y_1=zU+HV is nonzero and is retained everywhere below.

Writing D=1+3xi+xi^2, its exact data are

    A=H(2+xi), R^2=H^2 D, ||w||^2=xi,
    kappa=xi(2+xi)^2/D,
    kappa-1=(1+xi)(xi^2+2xi-1)/D.                          (14)

All four boundary pairings <g_i,w>,<y_i,w>,<g_i,x_0>,<y_i,x_0>
are nonnegative. Therefore ANY two such cores satisfy (7): each left side
is nonpositive and each right side nonnegative. Formula (9) is consequently
an actual, loss-free, simultaneous two-defect aggregation for these inputs.

For the same modified word as (12), H=lambda-p0 t and z=1+q0 t.
Then xi>theta for t>0; (14) proves kappa>1. This is a SECOND defective
core for the SAME physical three-step word, including the face missed by
the x_1=g_1 restriction. In fact xi-theta=(q0+theta p0)t/H>t/5,
and differentiating the middle expression in (14) gives
kappa'(xi)=1+(2xi+3)/D^2>1. Thus kappa>=1+t/5 uniformly on 0<=t<=1/50.
At t=0, kappa=1 exactly. This is a direct algebraic proof, not a reused draft.

## 5. Exact boundary state and a finite obstruction to repeating the join

For samples with e_i=<g_i,y_i>>0 define

    a_i=<g_i,w>/||w||^2, c_i=<g_i,x_0>/R^2,
    B_i=<w,y_i>/e_i, D_i=<x_0,y_i>/e_i,
    k=<x_0,w>/R^2, zeta=||w||^2/R^2.                       (15)

Include all gradient samples in a,c. If a residual has e_i=0, use the original
inequalities (7); division by e_i is not licensed. In the seed (13), e_i=0
only for zero residuals, and this property survives isometric joins.
For two identical children the tests reduce exactly to

    r max_(i,j)(-c_j B_i)<=1,
    max_(i,j)(-a_i D_j)<=r.                                (16)

The parent's retained boundary data are

    a_left=-[k/(r zeta)]a,       a_right=a,
    B_left=-r k B,              B_right=B,
    c_left=c,                   c_right=-r k c,
    D_left=D,                   D_right=-[k/(r zeta)]D,
    k_parent=-r k^2, zeta_parent=r^2 zeta^2.                (17)

These follow by substituting (6), and preserve each sample rather than
assuming extrema are sufficient for an arbitrary Gram completion.

Now repeat the identical-child construction with source primitive bridges:
A_j=lambda^j A_0, r_j=theta A_j, H_0=(a,b,a), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+r_j,H_j).
Use seed (13) with xi>=theta. Its length is3, so H_j has length4*2^j-1.
Set omega=H/A_0=1/(2+xi)<=theta and q=A_0 k_0=omega kappa_0.
The exact formulas give 0<q<1 because
D-xi(2+xi)=1+xi>0. Set u=lambda omega<=1 and v=theta q<1.
In the seed, max a=1, max B=1, max D_i=A_0, and
max c=c_*=(1+xi)/(H D); all four lists are nonnegative. The extrema are
attained: B=1 and D=A_0 at sample1, and c=c_* at sample0.

After the first join their signed extrema are

    a in[-u,1], B in[-v,1], c in[-v c_*,c_*], D_i in[-u A_0,A_0]. (18)

For later formal joins, (17) gives A_j k_j=-q^(2^j) and
k_j/(r_j zeta_j)=-lambda omega^(2^j) for j>=1.
Thus the newly multiplied pieces in (17) have factors between0 and1,
and every extremum in (18) persists at an actual inherited sample.
Consequently, for every j>=1, the exact tests (16) are

    r_j v c_*<=1,      u A_0<=r_j.                         (19)

The second holds for all j>=1 because u<=1 and r_1=A_0. For the first set

    T(xi)=xi(2+xi)^2(1+xi)/(1+3xi+xi^2)^2.

Then r_j v c_*=lambda^(j-2) T(xi). We have 0<T(xi)<1 because
D^2-xi(2+xi)^2(1+xi)=xi^3+3xi^2+2xi+1>0.
Also T is increasing: its positive logarithmic derivative has numerator
2+xi+xi^2+xi^3 after multiplication by xi(1+xi)(2+xi)D.
At xi=theta, T=2-s>theta. Therefore joins j=0,1,2 are legal, producing
actual H_1,H_2,H_3 of lengths7,15,31, with kappa_j=kappa_0^(2^j).
But join j=3 necessarily fails since lambda T(xi)>=lambda(2-s)=s>1.

This failure is a SPECIFIC original inequality. In the proposed H_4 of
length63, left sample25 inherits B=-v (base sample1, then two right-copy
embeddings); right sample32 inherits c=c_* (the right child's first sample).
Its signed interpolation residual is

    Q_(32,25)=e_25[lambda T(xi)-1]>0,                       (20)

where e_25>0. Both children H_3 are legal, including for every t>0 above,
and both carry positive defects. Their next rank-one cross block is real
PSD and satisfies GD, but violates (20). This pinpoints why the formal
product kappa_0^16 is NOT a valid next-level witness.

## 6. Calibration, zero mass, and scope

At t=0 the actual seed and its first three legal joins have kappa=1,
so the construction gives no spurious positive source defect. The illegal
next join is discarded rather than used against an upper certificate.
The exact finite comparison remains B_n, not an assumed Silver equality.
A subsequent objective-tail conversion with the exact source ConPD bridge
has payoff (1+delta)/B_concat with its actual mass B_concat; its derivation
and calibration are included in the next joint continuation package.

The generic input and cross tests in sections1-2 include empty or zero-mass
children: then A=1, all child points and gradients equal w, and y_i=0.
No test is divided by a zero residual or a zero mass. The obstruction in
section5 concerns the explicitly nonempty orthogonal seed with b>1;
it does not extend a nonempty-right dilution claim to empty right blocks.

The modified-seed repeated-word family changes the leading primitive mass:
A_j(t)=lambda^j[lambda^2+(10s-14)t], whereas A_j(0)=lambda^(j+2).
Their ratio is the fixed number1+(10s-14)t/lambda^2>1. This is not the
previously closed common-offset family with only O(n) additional mass.
The actual unbuffered child hypotheses have been proved only through H_3;
(20) forbids extending this particular construction further. No all-h
decomposition, primitive-capacity bound, or asymptotic conclusion follows.

The next operation is to retain both actual children but add explicit
constant-gradient coordinates to pay for their signed boundary correlations.
Its true-distance cost must be carried in the ratio law; it is not a free
translation, a free function-value shift, or a consequence of PSD alone.

# Silver certificate deformation: source reconstruction and three viability screens

Status: JOINT UNVERIFIED ANALYTICAL DRAFT. No formal submission, accepted new
fact, full-class bound for the perturbed word, or resolution of the original
minimax target is claimed. This is max4's one allocated round after completed64,
with completed ceiling65 / attempted ceiling66. TASK.md and guidance
8fd9094550caff70 govern; plan cbe64562a9a92f5d records the work. Latest
guidance56f0c49630564f24 was read and explicitly leaves this assignment unchanged.

The outcome is three exact, narrowly scoped failed screens. The second screen
allows arbitrary terminal-gradient coefficients; the third changes the two
right-child weights independently and passes the first two levels. Neither
survives the original-coordinate test at the third level. The source itself
does give a sharp GD objective certificate for ORIGINAL Silver, but its missing
extension is precisely a primitive/history inequality already present in the
project. No new theorem for the perturbed word follows merely from retrieval.

## 1. Model, accepted inputs, and unchanged physical word

Use fact1252b8823bb5a290's normalized convention: L=1,
x_star=g_star=f_star=0, x_i=x_0-sum_(t<i) h_t g_t, and

    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2 <=0.

All formal vectors (x_0,g_0,...,g_n) remain independent when testing a
certificate. For a nonnegative physical word h, W_n(h) is the supremum of
2(f(x_n)-f(x_star))/(L||x_0-x_star||^2) over the entire smooth-convex class
and every finite dimension. Thus W=2R in the project's notation. Every ordered
row exists; unspecified multipliers below are zero, not deleted constraints.

The other accepted inputs are fact04219554e41d9750, Sections10--11
(primitive and objective identities, including their full-coordinate scope),
and fact5fd396eba75651e7, Section10 (the original benchmark comparison only).
No unverified previous obstruction or numerical optimizer is a premise.

Fix one enclosing integer m and set

    lambda=1+sqrt(2), epsilon=1/(4m), beta=1-epsilon,
    c=lambda(1+epsilon), a=beta/c,
    H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+d_j,H_j), d_j=a c^j, 0<=j<m,
    S_j=sum H_j, A_j=1+S_j, n_m=2^m-1.

Every child uses this SAME epsilon, beta, c, a. In particular testing H_3
below means the actual initial seven-step subword of H_m, not resetting m=3.
The terminal gradient is a formal sample, not another physical step.

## 2. Complete source and hypothesis mapping

### 2.1 Altschuler--Parrilo and the original full interpolation certificate

Source: Jason M. Altschuler and Pablo A. Parrilo, *Acceleration by Stepsize
Hedging II: Silver Stepsize Schedule for Smooth Convex Optimization*,
arXiv:2309.16530v1, Theorem1.1, Definition2.1, Theorem3.4, Lemmas3.5--3.7,
and AppendixA. The exact downloaded HTML reports v1, dated28September2023.
The proof, including the corrections, signs, and AppendixA expansions, was read.
URL: https://arxiv.org/html/2309.16530 .

The source's nonnegative residual is

    P_ij=2(f_i-f_j)+2<g_j,x_j-x_i>-||g_i-g_j||^2 = -2 Q_ji.

Consequently both a transpose and a factor2 are required when transferring
its multipliers. Its theorem is for a convex L-smooth function with a chosen
minimizer, arbitrary initialization and dimension, ORIGINAL Silver word
H^0_(j+1)=(H^0_j,1+lambda^(j-1),H^0_j), and n=2^j-1.
Write A=lambda^j, S=A-1, and

    C_AP=(1+sqrt(4A^2-3))/2, r_AP=1/(2C_AP).

The source identity is

    sum sigma_ij P_ij
      =||x_0||^2-||x_n-C_AP g_n||^2-2C_AP f_n.       (2.1)

It gives W_n(H^0_j)<=1/C_AP, not r_AP. This is a direct GD objective
bound, not a proximal bound. Its multiplier recursion glues the left copy
with weight1 and the right copy with weight lambda^2=1+2lambda, adds
lambda times each actual right step in the two endpoint-to-right rows,
and corrects the three endpoint/optimum incidences. The final optimum
incidences are sigma_(star,i)=h_i for i<n, sigma_(star,n)=C_AP,
sigma_(n,star)=S. Negative correction entries must be aggregated with their
inherited entries; the final multipliers are nonnegative.

Here is a direct reconstruction in the ORIGINAL Q convention, using only
the accepted primitive identity, which also checks the source's full array.
For any word h of sum S and terminal n define

    V_h=2 sum_(i<n) h_i(f_i-f_n)
          +sum_(i<n) h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2-S(S+1)||g_n||^2.

On the original Silver word define the finite multiplier array Lambda^0
from the empty array as follows. For a join, p is the bridge index, z is the
parent terminal, and j ranges over the nonterminal right-child queries.
Let A=1+sum(child), d=A/lambda and let h_j denote its right steps. Embed
both complete child arrays and add

    Lambda^0_parent = Lambda^0_left + lambda^2 Lambda^0_right
       +2lambda [ sum_j h_j (Q_(j,p)+Q_(j,z))
                                      +Q_(z,p)+d Q_(p,z) ].   (2.2)

Here and below a sum of Q terms denotes its coefficient array. All four
added coefficient types are nonnegative. Fact04219554e41d9750, identity(5),
applies because d(2A+d)=A^2. It yields the polynomial identity

                    V_h + sum Lambda^0_ij Q_ij = 0.           (2.3)

The empty identity is0=0. The actual parent capacity is2A+d=lambda A;
induction proves A=lambda^j, so this is exactly the original physical Silver
word at every level. It is an identity on all formal gradients, with zero
PSD remainder, not a restriction to actual or coincident gradients.

To recover (2.1), take sigma_ij=Lambda^0_ji/2 on finite pairs and the
three optimum-incidence types displayed above. The finite part of the
source's Theta+Xi+Delta recursion is exactly (2.2) after this transpose and
factor2; its endpoint coefficients are 2lambda and 2lambda d in Q convention.
The remaining identity follows from

    V_h-2 sum_(i<n) h_i Q_(i,star)
      =||x_0||^2-||x_n||^2-2S f_n-S(S+1)||g_n||^2,             (2.4)
    C_AP^2-C_AP=S(S+2).

Indeed adding -2C_AP Q_(n,star)-2S Q_(star,n) to (2.4) gives the
right-hand side of (2.1). This checks all coefficients without invoking
the source's computer-algebra script. In project normalization the full
PSD identity is the square ||x_n-C_AP g_n||^2/C_AP, with finite multipliers
Lambda^0/C_AP, prefix-to-optimum weights2h_i/C_AP, terminal-to-optimum
weight2, and optimum-to-terminal weight2S/C_AP.

The SAME finite baseline array has the sharper completion

    ||x_0||^2-2D f_n+sum Lambda^0_ij Q_ij
       +2 sum_(i<n) h_i Q_(i,star)+2A Q_(n,star)
                         =||x_n-A g_n||^2,  D=2S+1=2A-1.    (2.5)

For an explicit expansion, substitute (2.3) and (2.4), then use
A^2-A=S(S+1) and D=S+A. Thus every coefficient in (2.5) is nonnegative,
and division by D gives a full original-space objective certificate:

                         W_n(H^0_j)<=1/(2lambda^j-1).        (2.6)

The matching Huber function has slope t=1/D, value x^2/2 for |x|<=t,
and t|x|-t^2/2 otherwise. Start at1, with minimizer0. Since every step is
nonnegative and each prefix sum is at most S, every query lies in the
positive affine piece: x_i=1-t sum_(k<i) h_k and x_n=(S+1)/D>=t.
Its value satisfies2f(x_n)=1/D. This proves tightness of the baseline
completion within this joint reconstruction. It supplies no perturbed-word
certificate. No separate unverified intermediate result is being adopted.

### 2.2 Chikhi: product identity, joint potential, and proximal output

Source: Salah R. Chikhi, *Last-Iterate Performance of Gradient Descent and
Relaxed Proximal Point via s-Composability*, arXiv:2609.13765v1,
Definition1, Proposition1 with proof, Theorem1 with equality construction,
Lemma2 with proof, and Section5/Theorem4 with proof.
URL: https://arxiv.org/html/2609.13765v1 .

Its definition requires a positive schedule and BOTH

    eta=1/(1+S)=product_(i<n)(h_i-1),
    ||x_0-x_star||^2 >= ||x_n-x_star||^2
                    +S(S+1)||g_n||^2+2S(f_n-f_star).          (2.7)

The source obtains Silver's s-composability from Grimmer et al., Lemma6.
No uninspected theorem of that paper is needed here: (2.3)--(2.4) reconstruct
the necessary baseline inequality from the accepted project identity.
For a varying schedule the history expression in Chikhi's Lemma2 is

    sum h_i[2(f_i-f_n)+||g_i||^2+2<g_i,x_0-x_i>]
           -||x_n-x_0||^2-S(S+1)||g_n||^2.

Expanding x_0-x_i=sum_(t<i)h_t g_t cancels every off-diagonal product
against the squared displacement, leaving exactly V_h. Adding the
nonnegative source residuals sum h_i P_(star,i) means subtracting
2sum h_i Q_(i,star), which gives (2.7) by (2.4). Thus an appeal to this
history mechanism for H_m is precisely the already-unpaid primitive
inequality; the new paper does not furnish it for a perturbed word.

For a proper closed convex F with an attained minimum and mu>0, the
source's relaxed proximal iteration is

    z_i=prox_(mu F)(x_i), x_(i+1)=x_i+h_i(z_i-x_i),
    z_n=prox_(mu F)(x_n).

Its exact theorem is sup mu(F(z_n)-F_star)/||x_0-x_star||^2=1/[4(S+1)]
for a positive s-composable schedule. The equality example is F(x)=t|x|
with t=R/[2mu(S+1)], x_0=R, and z_n=R/2. For original Silver this is
1/(4lambda^j). Its terminal point is z_n, not our x_n.

The normalized envelope f=mu F_mu satisfies g_n=x_n-z_n and

                   f(x_n)-f_star=mu(F(z_n)-F_star)+||g_n||^2/2.

Dropping the last term would misidentify the objective. The source's joint
terminal inequality instead gives, with t=||g_n||,

    mu(F(z_n)-F_star)+(S+1)t^2<=R t,
    f(x_n)-f_star <= R t-(S+1/2)t^2 <= R^2/[2(2S+1)].        (2.8)

This is the paid conversion, with W<=1/(2S+1). For an arbitrary smooth
f, the same result follows directly from (2.7) and the original optimum
interpolation inequality; no assumption that a particular smooth f has
already been supplied as an envelope is necessary. Formula(2.5) gives its
explicit original-row certificate whenever (2.3) is available.

The perturbed H_m does not satisfy the definition's product identity.
Set v=a/(c-2), so0<v<1. The physical sum identity, checked by substitution
in A_(j+1)=2A_j+d_j, is

    A_j=v c^j+(1-v)2^j.

Counting each level's multiplicity, or inducting in the physical word, gives

    product_(i<n_m)(h_i-1)=beta^(2^m-1)c^(-m),
    A_m product_(i<n_m)(h_i-1)
      =beta^(2^m-1)[v+(1-v)(2/c)^m] <1.                      (2.9)

This prevents using the named s-composability theorem as stated. It does
NOT disprove the joint potential without the product identity.

### 2.3 Wang--Ma--Yang--Zhou: the older metrics

Source: Bofan Wang, Shiqian Ma, Junfeng Yang and Danqing Zhou,
*Relaxed Proximal Point Algorithm: Tight Complexity Bounds and Acceleration
without Momentum*, arXiv:2410.08890, Lemma5.1 and Theorems5.2--5.3 with the
displayed proofs. URL: https://arxiv.org/html/2410.08890 .

For arbitrary convex L-smooth h and original Silver, Theorem5.2 states the
tight GD objective bound (h(x_n)-h_star)/(L R^2)<=1/(4lambda^j-2), and
gradient bound ||grad h(x_n)||/(L R)<=lambda^(-j). Its finite array in
Lemma5.1 is Lambda^0 transposed and divided by2: its residual is -Q_ji
and its identity(34) has right-hand side V_h/2. The weighted optimum completion
is reproduced explicitly in (2.5); no typography in the extracted proof
is being used as an algebraic premise.

Theorem5.3 applies this to an envelope and bounds
F(z_n)-F_star+(mu/2)||grad F_mu(x_n)||^2 by R^2/[mu(4lambda^j-2)].
The discussion calls the proximal objective bound obtained by dropping
the residual term non-tight. That is the older gap addressed by Chikhi.
It is not a new sharper bound for the GD objective of a perturbed word.

## 3. A paid terminal-coefficient relaxation

For a word of sum S define the generalized history form

    V_(h,K)=2 sum h_i(f_i-f_n)+sum h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2-K||g_n||^2.

Suppose a proposed nonnegative finite array has the exact full identity
V_(h,K)+sum Lambda_ij Q_ij=M(g), where M is a quadratic form in ALL
formal gradients. This equation defines M; it does not presume PSD.
For K>=0 put

    b_K=(1+sqrt(1+4K))/2, F_K=S+b_K.

Expanding (2.4) with K in place of S(S+1) gives

    ||x_0||^2-2F_K f_n+sum Lambda_ij Q_ij
          +2sum h_i Q_(i,star)+2b_K Q_(n,star)
                          =M(g)+||x_n-b_K g_n||^2.           (3.1)

All added coefficients are nonnegative. If M>=0, division by F_K would give

    W_n(h)<=1/F_K=2/[2S+1+sqrt(1+4K)].

Relative to Huber1/(2S+1), the loss is exactly

    chi_K=(2S+1)/(S+b_K).                                   (3.2)

If only a radius-inflated joint inequality with constant r_rad were proved,
the corresponding upper would pay the additional factor r_rad. Neither a
smaller K nor a different radius coefficient is free. The screens below test
the actual M, not a presumed child oracle or an informal potential estimate.

## 4. One full-coordinate coefficient expansion for the screens

Consider (h,1+d,h), with child sum s, A=s+1, T=2A+d, parent sum T-1.
Let p denote its bridge query and z its terminal. Let r_vec=sum_j h_j g_j
over the nonterminal right child. Its two copies of V_(h,K) have factor1
and kappa. Introduce the nonnegative correction array

    E=u sum_j h_j Q_(j,p)+v_w sum_j h_j Q_(j,z)
                                      +p_w Q_(z,p)+z_w Q_(p,z).

The symbol v_w is a multiplier weight, distinct from v=a/(c-2).
For a nonempty right child, all function values cancel in
V_(parent,K_+)-V_left-kappa V_right+E iff

    u+v_w=2(kappa-1),
    u s+p_w-z_w=2(A+d).                                     (4.1)

When the right child is empty, choosing these identities remains a valid
definition of the continuation. Empty sums are zero; no formal endpoint is
identified with a different endpoint. The resulting original quadratic is

    M_def_parent=M_def_left+kappa M_def_right+R,
    R=(u-v_w)||r_vec||^2/2
       +(u d-z_w)<r_vec,g_p>+(p_w-v_w)<r_vec,g_z>
       +(p_w d-z_w)<g_p,g_z>+C_p||g_p||^2+C_z||g_z||^2,       (4.2)
    C_p=K-d-d^2+(u s+p_w)/2-(d+1/2)z_w,
    C_z=-K_++kappa K+v_w s/2+(p_w+z_w)/2.

For a check that this retains remote and mixed terms, the gradient parts of
the four sums in E, before multiplication by u,v_w,p_w,z_w, are

    sum h_j Q_(j,p): d<r_vec,g_p>+||r_vec||^2/2
                       +sum h_j(1-h_j)||g_j||^2/2+s||g_p||^2/2;
    sum h_j Q_(j,z): -||r_vec||^2/2
                       +sum h_j(1-h_j)||g_j||^2/2
                       +s||g_z||^2/2-<r_vec,g_z>;
    Q_(z,p): d<g_z,g_p>+<g_z,r_vec>+(||g_p||^2+||g_z||^2)/2;
    Q_(p,z): -(d+1/2)||g_p||^2+||g_z||^2/2
                                       -<g_p,r_vec>-<g_p,g_z>.

Their value parts immediately give (4.1). Adding the history diagonals gives
(4.2). Thus this is an identity on the original space, with no assumed
coincidence of gradients and no quotient-space PSD test.

## 5. The first actual levels already detect the deformation

For every enclosing m>=3, c<3, so0<d_j<A_j at every level. To see this,
A_j>v c^j=d_j/(c-2) and c-2<1. Also

    t_j=d_j/A_j=(c-2)/[1+(1/v-1)(2/c)^j]

is strictly increasing in j. Put t_star=1/lambda. At the first three levels,

    d_0=a, A_0=1; d_1=beta, A_1=2+a;
    d_2=beta c, A_2=4+a(2+c),
    t_0<t_star, t_1<t_star, t_2>t_star.                      (5.1)

The first inequality follows from a<1/lambda. For the second,

    lambda d_1-A_1=epsilon[-lambda+2/(lambda(1+epsilon))]<0.

For the third, use lambda^3=5lambda+2 and expand only the displayed scalar:

    c(lambda d_2-A_2)
      =epsilon[(lambda+4)-(4lambda+2)epsilon
                                      -(5lambda+2)epsilon^2] >0.       (5.2)

The bracket is positive already for0<epsilon<=1/4: use2<lambda<3 to
bound it below by6-14/4-17/16>0. No schedule recurrence or numerical
evaluation was executed to obtain these textual identities.

## 6. Screen1: equal-weight source transport, canonical terminal coefficient

This law preserves the source's four coefficient ratios, substitutes the
actual A,d, and fixes the scalar gluing factor by exact value flow:

    kappa=(3A+d)/(A-d), k=kappa-1=2(A+d)/(A-d),
    u=v_w=p_w=k, z_w=k d,
    K=A(A-1), K_+=T(T-1).                                  (6.1)

All coefficients are nonnegative. At original Silver, d=A/lambda and
kappa=lambda^2, so this is exactly (2.2), not an unrelated seed.
Substitution in (4.2) leaves

    R=Delta(||g_z||^2-||g_p||^2),
    Delta=kappa A^2-T^2=kappa d^2-A^2,
    Delta/A^2=(1+t)(t^2+2t-1)/(1-t), t=d/A.                (6.2)

At the actual first step t=t_0<t_star, Delta<0. The empty child remainders
are zero, so the formal direction g_0=0,g_1=1 gives M_def_1=Delta<0.
The canonical recursive child certificate therefore fails at its first
perturbed step. Its value-flow and signs alone are not a certificate.
Stop this screen here; Screen2 tests the substantive terminal repair.

## 7. Screen2: arbitrary terminal-gradient coefficients do not repair this law

Retain the finite multiplier recursion(6.1), but now allow ANY real
K_0=0,K_1,...,K_m, consistently in every same-depth child. Use K_j in the
child and K_(j+1) in the parent history form. This includes every weakening
of the canonical terminal coefficient, with its objective cost paid by(3.2)
when K_m>=0. No feasibility of an intermediate child is assumed below.

The actual full M_def_j remains diagonal: its child matrices are diagonal and
(4.2) becomes

    R=(K_j+A_j-kappa_j d_j^2)||g_p||^2
       +[-K_(j+1)+kappa_j K_j
                       +(kappa_j-1)(A_j+d_j)/2]||g_z||^2.   (7.1)

Let T_j^end be the actual terminal diagonal of M_def_j and set
E_def_j=T_j^end+K_j, D_def_j=E_def_j-A_j(A_j-1). These are scalar coefficient
identities, not executed recurrences. From(7.1),

    E_def_0=D_def_0=0,
    E_def_(j+1)=kappa_j E_def_j+(kappa_j-1)(A_j+d_j)/2,
    D_def_(j+1)=kappa_j D_def_j+Delta_j,
    actual bridge diagonal = E_def_j+A_j-kappa_j d_j^2
                            =D_def_j-Delta_j.                  (7.2)

Every K_j has canceled from the bridge diagonal. By(5.1) and(6.2),
Delta_0<0, Delta_1<0, Delta_2>0. Therefore

    D_def_2=kappa_1 Delta_0+Delta_1<0,
    (M_def_3)_(3,3)=D_def_2-Delta_2<0.                              (7.3)

This is the ORIGINAL coordinate g_3, the physical bridge of H_3. Thus the
free-terminal-coefficient repair fails on an elementary principal diagonal;
it is not merely a negative modular remainder that a child might absorb.
For orientation, at the first step K_1=E_def_1 makes the terminal diagonal zero
and leaves the other diagonal positive, so the repair really passes that
first failure before encountering(7.3).

Even inflating only the final radius/objective level cannot repair the fixed
full array at the target horizons. Already in H_4 the two H_3 copies have
negative diagonals at g_3 and g_11, equal to a_neg and kappa_3 a_neg,
where a_neg=D_def_2-Delta_2<0. Choose g_3=1,g_11=-1 and every other gradient
zero. Their actual physical steps both equal1+d_2, so the displacement
sum h_i g_i is zero. With x_0=0, g_n=0, the finishing square in(3.1) is
zero for ANY terminal coefficient b_K, whereas M=(1+kappa_3)a_neg<0.
All later enclosing joins leave this initial H_4 direction untouched.
Adding any multiple of ||x_0||^2 also vanishes on it. This is only an
obstruction to the fixed array(6.1), not to a reoptimized full certificate.

## 8. Screen3: unequal right weights preserving the primitive null vector

Return to K=A(A-1), K_+=T(T-1), but permit the two right weights to differ.
This repair changes the actual finite array. A concrete source-calibrated law is

    u=2lambda,
    v_w=[u d+2(A+d)]/A,
    kappa=1+(u+v_w)/2,
    z_w=2A, p_w=4A+2d-u(A-1)
                  =2lambda+2(d-A/lambda).                  (8.1)

It satisfies both value-flow equations(4.1), and at d=A/lambda recovers
u=v_w=p_w=2lambda, z_w=2A and kappa=lambda^2: exactly the source.
All actual coefficients are nonnegative at EVERY level. The weights
u,v_w,z_w,kappa are positive. At j=0, p_w=4+2d_0>0; at j=1,
A_1<lambda implies p_w>2lambda-2>0; for j>=2, monotonicity in(5.1)
gives d_j>A_j/lambda and hence p_w>2lambda.

The full canonical primitive form vanishes on common gradients: choose
all g_i equal and choose the affine-consistent f_i, so all finite Q's and
V_h are zero. Thus every PSD primitive matrix has the all-ones vector e
in its kernel. In(8.1), direct substitution in(4.2) preserves M_def_j e=0
algebraically at every level, even before asserting PSD.

More explicitly, set delta=t_star-d/A, Y=r_vec-s g_z and Z=g_p-g_z.
The entire correction is

    R=delta[(lambda+1)||Y||^2-2lambda A<Y,Z>
                                +A^2(2+t_star+d/A)||Z||^2].  (8.2)

The bracket is positive definite in Y,Z: its leading diagonal is positive
and its determinant divided by A^2 is

    (lambda+1)(2+t_star+d/A)-lambda^2 >= lambda>0.

Thus(5.1) proves PSD of M_def_1 and M_def_2 by the actual embedded child sums.
Both their signs and their full original remainders pass. At level j=2,
choose ALL left-child gradients, including g_p, equal to1, and ALL
right-child gradients, including g_z, equal to0. The left M_def_2 vanishes on
its common-gradient kernel; the right term vanishes on zero. Formula(4.2)
or(8.2) gives the full original value

                 M_def_3=A_2^2-2A_2 d_2-d_2^2<0.               (8.3)

The vector uses g_0=g_1=g_2=g_3=1 and g_4=...=g_7=0. It is a
formal PSD test, not an asserted interpolable primal trajectory. No
gradient equality was imposed in the certificate's domain.

The failure is not a poor choice of the remaining scalar u. In the more
general four-correction template(4.1), suppose the canonical children are
PSD. Full parent PSD would require M_def_parent e=0. The bridge row of R e is
(A+d)(2A-z_w)/2, so z_w=2A. The right aggregate row requires
u d-v_w A+2(A+d)=0. On the same left-common/right-zero vector the full
value is then A^2-2Ad-d^2, independently of u,v_w,kappa,p_w. Thus no
scalar choice in this Huber-kernel-preserving template repairs this level.
This is a short original-coordinate test, not an all-depth impossibility
argument for unrestricted primitive or objective certificates.

For completeness, merely changing the objective level of this fixed full
array also cannot help. Embed the vector w from(8.3) in the first H_3 of
H_m, setting all later coordinates to zero. Every enclosing correction
vanishes on w, because the enclosing bridge and entire right block vanish;
the negative value is unchanged. Its final gradient is zero. The whole
matrix still has M_def_m e=0. Write D=2S_m+1 and
theta=(sum_(i<n_m)h_i w_i)/D. Set g=w-theta e and x_0=0. Then
M_def_m(g)=M_def_m(w)<0 and x_n-A_m g_n=0. The completion(2.5), divided by D,
is negative in this original direction, and any extra coefficient on
||x_0||^2 vanishes. Changing the full multipliers or the potential shape
is outside this screened law.

## 9. Precisely unpaid interface and benchmark loss

The accepted benchmark is the ORIGINAL all-split recurrence of
fact04219554e41d9750, with U_n^ZJ=1/B_n. Fact5fd396eba75651e7 gives

           B_(2^m-1)/(1+2S_m)<237/250,  m>=1536, tau=1/4.   (9.1)

It is a comparison with the Huber LOWER example; it is not a full upper.
A new full certificate with W_(2^m-1)(H_m)<=chi/(1+2S_m), uniformly with
some fixed chi<250/237, would give the fixed relative improvement
1-237chi/250 on this true unbounded sequence. If the new certificate used
the valid generalized-history route(3.1), its exact required inequality is

    V_(H_m,K_m)+sum Lambda^m_ij Q_ij=M_def_m>=0
    on EVERY formal gradient, with every Lambda^m_ij>=0,
    (2S_m+1)/(S_m+(1+sqrt(1+4K_m))/2)<=chi<250/237.           (9.2)

Neither(9.2) nor a more general objective certificate was obtained. A
radius loss must additionally be multiplied into chi. Proving(9.2) is the
substantive all-function inequality, not a technical continuity estimate.

These screens show exactly what the selected source transport cannot pay:
understeps at the first levels create child slack, but the selected endpoint
and right-aggregate corrections leave an explicit original negative direction
when the next bridge exceeds A/lambda. A future continuation must change
coefficients that act on that direction, for example by changing inherited
finite arrays in their enclosing context or by using a genuinely different
full objective completion. Such a change must prove all signs and the entire
PSD remainder; it cannot invoke the primitive history inequality as a premise.

This does not exclude unrestricted nonnegative multipliers, other source
deformations, collective corrections involving left-interior coordinates,
nonprimitive potentials, or a full-class Huber-like upper for H_m. It is not
an actual lower witness against H_m. The original minimax target remains OPEN.

## 10. Retrieval, records, and resources

Primary PDF and HTML files and their extracted source text are under downloads/.
The source text reader is extract_source_text.py, a standard-library HTMLParser
that preserves MathML alttext; it performs no mathematical evaluation.
The three pdftotext attempts returned exit127, `pdftotext: command not found`.
No installation was attempted. Source HTML retrieval and extraction succeeded.
Matlas was called once for the active Silver/joint-potential mechanism; its
generic constant-step hits were not used. Exact recommended primary papers
were then read. Their statements, definitions, and applicable proofs, rather
than search snippets or numerical tables, supplied the source mapping above.

The primary source's recurrence was never executed. No mathematical program,
numerical evaluation, arithmetic replay, solver, benchmark DP, formal gate,
consult, subagent, or installation was used. The algebra above is textual
reasoning. No external theorem or draft in this report has been promoted to a
fact; no unverified component is an accepted predecessor. This complete joint
draft is a finding, with accepted inputs identified in Section1.

Exactly three continuation laws were screened. The one allocated analytical
round is complete; no further allocation or automatic renewal is inferred.
No publication, finalization, revocation, shutdown, or Goal completion action
was taken.

<!-- BEGIN_SOURCE_BODY DOC-001210 -->
# Actual Newton chronology: a birth chain and a joint covariance charge

Status: UNVERIFIED proof candidate and partial storage argument. The exact
coordinate identities and ordered-kernel result below are complete candidates.
The covariance domination in Section 5 is OPEN. In particular there is no
whole-interface error bound, no legal benchmark-scale construction, and no
resolution of the original exact-profile problem in this file. No optimization,
numerical experiment, installation, or fact submission was used in this round.

Accepted mathematical inputs: ordinary smooth interpolation and scaling in
fact 1252b8823bb5a290, and the benchmark definitions in b98bb807d514e80d.
The total-nonnegativity assertion also uses the explicitly identified and
audited published theorem in Section 3. No earlier unverified max2 result is
a premise: the identities needed for this argument are derived here.

## 1. The actual interface and notation

For an integer m>=128, put

 lambda=1+sqrt(2), c=lambda(1+1/m), a=(1-1/m)/c,
 d_j=1+a c^j, H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,d_j,H_j).

Set n=2^m-1 and q=n-1. The queried steps h_0,...,h_(q-1) are the word
H_m with its final d_0 deleted. The actual final step is b in [0,2]. Define

 s_i=sum_(t<i)h_t, S=s_q,
 p_ij=product_(t<i)(1-h_t/d_j),
 R_i=sum_(j<m)p_ij w_j, Z_i=sum_(j<m)d_j p_ij w_j,
 x_i=u-s_i v+Z_i, g_i=v+R_i, 0<=i<=q.

All these vectors use ONE arbitrary real PSD Gram matrix. The values f_i
form ONE array; the optimum jet is x_star=g_star=f_star=0; ||x_0||=1 is
the distance normalization relative to the chosen actual minimizer. Every
original queried inequality is retained:

 Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2<=0.       (1)

The first d_j occurs at step 2^j-1. Thus p_ij is positive before query
2^j and zero thereafter, and R_q=Z_q=0. The recursion gives

 Z_i-Z_(i+1)=h_i R_i, sum_i h_i p_ij=d_j.             (2)

Here and below unqualified time sums range over 0<=i<q.

For clarity about the original value budget, define

 C_min=f_q-<v,x_q>, eta=-C_min-||v||^2/2,
 delta_i=f_i-<v,x_i>-C_min-||R_i||^2/2.

On an actual legal function, tilting by -<v,x> makes x_q a minimizer;
the smooth optimum inequalities give delta_i>=0. Also eta>=0 follows
from Q_(q,star). Expanding Q_(0,star) gives the exact common budget

 eta>=delta_0+||R_0||^2+<v,R_0>-<R_0,x_0>.          (3)

No height is set to zero and neither eta nor the norm of x_0 is discarded.

## 2. A lossless stochastic change of coordinates

Define polynomials

 phi_k(z)=product_(r<k)(1-d_r z), 0<=k<=m.

Work modulo z phi_m(z), which has the m+1 distinct nodes
0,1/d_0,...,1/d_(m-1). The double zero in the position quotient leaves
exactly this simple-zero gradient quotient. Put

 V_k=v+sum_(j<m)phi_k(1/d_j) w_j, 0<=k<=m.

Then V_0=g_0 and V_m=v. This change from (w_0,...,w_(m-1),v) to
(V_0,...,V_m) is invertible: the evaluation matrix
phi_k(1/d_j), with columns ordered j=0,...,m-1, then the node0, is
upper triangular with strictly positive diagonal.

There are coefficients B_ik determined by B_00=1, B_0k=0 for k>0 and

 B_(i+1),0=(1-h_i/d_0)B_i0,
 B_(i+1),k=(1-h_i/d_k)B_ik+(h_i/d_(k-1))B_i,(k-1), 0<k<m,
 B_(i+1),m=B_im+(h_i/d_(m-1))B_i,(m-1).             (4)

Although an unused factor 1-h_i/d_k can be negative for a dead state,
it always multiplies zero. More precisely, if r_i distinct ruler levels
have occurred before query i, induction gives B_ik=0 for k<r_i.
The next step has level at most r_i, so h_i<=d_k on every occupied
state k<m. If it is the first d_(r_i), the mass at r_i moves entirely
to r_i+1. Consequently all B_ik are nonnegative, every row sums to one,
and after the first d_(m-1) the row is the point mass at state m.

The polynomial identity

 (1-hz)phi_k=(1-h/d_k)phi_k+(h/d_k)phi_(k+1)

for k<m, together with (1-hz)phi_m=phi_m modulo z phi_m, proves

 g_i=sum_(k=0)^m B_ik V_k.                           (5)

Thus B is exactly the marginal law of a time-inhomogeneous pure-birth
chain: from occupied state k<m it stays with probability 1-h_i/d_k
and advances one state with probability h_i/d_k; m is absorbing.
This probability space is auxiliary. No state vector V_k is assumed
to be a gradient at an additional sample of the legal function.

The positions are equally explicit. Let U_k=V_k-v and

 Y_k=sum_(r=k)^(m-1)d_r U_r, Y_m=0.

The conditional expected change of Y at step i is -h_i U_k when the
state is k. Hence sum_k B_ik Y_k changes by -h_i R_i. Initially

 Y_0=sum_k d_k U_k=sum_j d_j w_j=Z_0,

where the middle equality follows by telescoping
d_k phi_k(z)=(phi_k(z)-phi_(k+1)(z))/z at z=1/d_j.
Therefore

 Z_i=sum_k B_ik Y_k, x_i=u-s_i v+sum_k B_ik Y_k.      (6)

In particular the troublesome coupling remains, explicitly,

 <v,Z_0>=sum_(k<m)d_k<v,U_k>.                        (7)

Equations (5)-(6) preserve the original vectors, not just their norms.
Substitution in (1) gives EXACTLY

 Q_ij=f_i-f_j
   +<sum_k B_ik V_k, (s_i-s_j)v+sum_k(B_jk-B_ik)Y_k>
   +(1/2)||sum_k(B_ik-B_jk)V_k||^2.                  (8)

All earlier/earlier, suffix, and optimum conditions remain in this one
array. In particular (5) does not authorize independent state heights,
orthogonality of state vectors, or projected legality.

Two occupation identities retain quantitative physical costs:

 sum_i h_i B_ik=d_k,                                (9)
 sum_i s_(i+1)h_i B_ik=d_k sum_(r<=k)d_r, 0<=k<m.    (10)

To prove (9), sum the flow equation (4). State0 has initial mass1 and
final mass0, so its total outflow is1. Successive transient states have
equal total inflow and outflow, so their total outflows are also1.
For (10), write M_k=sum_i s_(i+1)h_i B_ik and M_(-1)=0.
Summation by parts gives

 sum_i s_(i+1)(B_(i+1),k-B_ik)=-sum_i h_i B_ik=-d_k.

Using (4), M_k/d_k-M_(k-1)/d_(k-1)=d_k, with the second term zero
at k=0. Induction proves (10). The terminal occupation is
sum_i h_i B_im=S-sum_(k<m)d_k; it is nonnegative and keeps the full
constant-gradient suffix in the budget.

## 3. Total nonnegativity for the ACTUAL chronological evaluations

This is a theorem about temporal coefficient matrices, not yet the
weighted energy matrix. It holds for arbitrary strictly increasing positive
levels d_0,...,d_(m-1) with the same ordered first-occurrence rule.

Let P be the matrix with chronological rows i=0,...,q and columns
p_i0,...,p_i,(m-1),1, in that order. Then P is totally nonnegative:
every square minor, with its inherited row and column order, is >=0.
The same is true of B, and of the jump matrix

 A_ij=(h_i/d_j)p_ij, 0<=i<q, 0<=j<m.                (11)

Every column of A sums to1 by (2).

External theorem used, with its local definitions made explicit:
David Galvin and Adrian Pacurar, *Total non-negativity of some combinatorial
matrices*, arXiv:1807.08658v3 (2019), Theorem1.2; published JCTA172 (2020),
105179. For a nondecreasing real sequence a_l, define C_ik by
product_(t<=i)(X-e_t)=sum_k C_ik product_(l<=k)(X-a_l).
Then C is totally nonnegative iff e is restricted growth relative to a:
the cap initially is a_1, an e_t below the cap leaves it unchanged,
an equality advances it to the next a_l, and exceeding it is forbidden.
The proof constructs a planar path matrix; zero pivots at cap hits move
all negative edge weights out of the network without changing its path
matrix. Disjoint-path determinant cancellation then proves the minor signs.

The exact PDF and extracted text were read, including Definition1.1,
Theorem1.2, the pivot lemmas3.7/3.9, and the complete proof of1.2.
Files: downloads/1807.08658v3.pdf and downloads/1807.08658.txt.
Matlas was queried repeatedly for this statement and title but did not
return the paper; the primary arXiv text, not a fabricated Matlas hit,
grounds this citation. No verifier acceptance is claimed for this application.

Here is the complete applicability check. Set

 a_1=-1/d_0<...<a_m=-1/d_(m-1)<a_(m+1)=0,

and continue the a sequence with zeros as far as necessary. Set
e_(i+1)=-1/h_i. A first occurrence of d_j is exactly a hit of the
current cap a_(j+1). Between first occurrences, every level is smaller
and hence its e is strictly below the next cap. After all levels have
appeared, every e is negative and the cap remains0. Thus the theorem's
restricted-growth condition is satisfied, with actual repetitions and
the actual deleted last d_0, not an ordered surrogate schedule.

Let N_k(X)=product_(r<k)(X+1/d_r), so
phi_k(z)=(product_(r<k)d_r)N_k(-z). The exact Newton expansion gives

 B_ik=(product_(t<i)h_t) C_ik/(product_(r<k)d_r),
 0<=k<=m.                                          (12)

The coefficients with k>m vanish upon evaluation at the m+1 nodes
-1/d_0,...,-1/d_(m-1),0. Positive row and column scaling and truncation
preserve all minor signs, proving that B is totally nonnegative.

For completeness the node-evaluation matrix V_kj=phi_k(1/d_j), with
the node0 last, is also totally nonnegative. After positive row scaling,
its transpose is L_jk=product_(r<k)(a_(j+1)-a_(r+1)). It is lower
triangular. In Neville elimination its pivot in row i,column k is

 product_(r=i-k)^(i-1)(a_(i+1)-a_(r+1))>0, i>=k.

Indeed the pivot is the ratio of determinants on consecutive rows
i-k,...,i and initial columns0,...,k, and on rows i-k,...,i-1 and
columns0,...,k-1. These determinants are Vandermonde determinants:
the monic Newton polynomials differ from monomials by a triangular
column change with diagonal1. Cancelling gives the displayed pivot.
All elimination multipliers are positive. Reversing the elimination
expresses L as a product of elementary lower bidiagonal matrices with
nonnegative off-diagonal entries and a positive diagonal matrix.
Each factor is totally nonnegative, as follows directly by expanding
its minors, and products preserve this property by Cauchy--Binet.
Thus V is totally nonnegative. Now P=BV, proving the assertion for P.
Finally A is a row and column positive scaling of P's residual submatrix,
so A has the asserted minor signs and stochastic column sums.

The chronological matrix has full column rank: the queries immediately
before first d_j, followed by any query after first d_(m-1), give a square
upper triangular evaluation submatrix with strictly positive diagonal.
Thus none of these representations has silently removed a modal direction.

## 4. Exact cancellation of ALL shared heights

Define the elapsed-time quadratic form

 J=sum_i [2s_(i+1)h_i<g_(i+1),g_i>
          +s_(i+1)||g_(i+1)-g_i||^2+h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2].

The original interpolation inequalities have the algebraic identity

 ||x_0||^2-2S f_q+2sum_i h_i Q_(i,star)
       +2sum_i s_(i+1)Q_(i+1,i)=||x_q||^2+J.        (13)

To check it, GD gives
2sum_i h_i Q_(i,star)=2sum_i h_i f_i-||x_0||^2
 +||x_q||^2+sum_i h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2.
Also Q_(i+1,i)=f_(i+1)-f_i+h_i<g_(i+1),g_i>
 +||g_(i+1)-g_i||^2/2. Summation by parts with s_0=0 and s_q=S
cancels the displayed common values and proves (13). All multipliers
are nonnegative. This is a sufficient certificate combination and does
not claim that its selected constraints are sufficient for legal data.

## 5. A Brownian-bridge covariance with the full diagonal cost

Put D_i=g_i-g_(i+1)=sum_j A_ij w_j, where A is (11). Define increasing
clock knots and nonnegative penalties

 beta_i=s_(i+1)(s_i+1), 0<=i<q,
 beta_q=S(S+1), beta_(-1)=0,
 c_i=(h_i-1)s_(i+1).

All h_i>1, so c_i>=0, and beta_0<...<beta_q. Expanding the squared
differences in J gives

 J=sum_(i=0)^q(beta_i-beta_(i-1))||g_i||^2
                          -sum_(i<q)c_i||D_i||^2. (14)

For example the coefficient of ||g_i||^2 at an interior node is
s_i(h_(i-1)+h_i)+h_i; that of ||g_q||^2 is S h_(q-1).
These are exactly the differences of beta in (14).

Since g_i=v+sum_(r>=i)D_r, changing the order of finite sums yields

 J=beta_q||v||^2+2<v,sum_i beta_i D_i>
        +sum_(i,l<q)beta_min(i,l)<D_i,D_l>
        -sum_i c_i||D_i||^2.

Define

 Kbr_(i,l)=beta_min(i,l)-beta_i beta_l/beta_q.

Completing the ONE constant-mode square proves the exact joint identity

 J=beta_q||v+(sum_i beta_i D_i)/beta_q||^2
       +sum_(i,l<q)Kbr_(i,l)<D_i,D_l>
       -sum_i c_i||D_i||^2.                         (15)

The kernel Kbr is a real covariance kernel. For a direct elementary
construction, take independent centered random variables xi_r of variances
beta_r-beta_(r-1), 0<=r<=q, let Z_i=sum_(r<=i)xi_r, and set
Zbr_i=Z_i-(beta_i/beta_q)Z_q. Direct expansion gives
E[Zbr_i Zbr_l]=Kbr_(i,l). Consequently Kbr is PSD. This use of
probability creates no randomness in the legal instance or its shared Gram.

The remaining sufficient claim is the EXPLICIT known-matrix domination

 A^T Kbr A >= A^T diag(c_i) A                        (16)

in the real PSD order, for the actual nearcritical ruler words. It is
equivalent to J>=0 on this temporal quotient, because the constant square
in (15) can be minimized by a freely chosen formal vector v. Every cost
is present, including all repeated small steps, the large-step penalties,
and the final constant-gradient suffix through beta_q.

Thus (16), IF PROVED, combines with (13) and ||x_0||=1 to give
2f_q<=1/S, and the ordinary smooth descent inequality then gives
2F(x_q-bv)<=1/S for every b in [0,2]. The previous draft's exact-B
comparison would have to be included in a complete eventual submission;
its conditional target constant is lambda/exp(1)<1. Neither (16) nor
that resulting whole-interface bound is asserted established here.

Equation (16) is a fixed, concrete covariance inequality on stochastic
Newton columns. It has no unknown interpolation multipliers or heights.
It remains a hard restricted weighted Hardy inequality, not a routine
check and not an equivalent restatement of the original all-h minimax goal.

## 6. Why total nonnegativity does NOT settle the energy sign

Here is an exact counterexample to that GENERIC implication. It is not a
counterexample to (16) for the actual m-dependent parameters.

Take levels t=3/2 and u=4 and the ruler word (t,u,t). On its first two
steps, choose the single residual column at rate1/u, namely

 g_0=1, g_1=5/8, g_2=0, v=0.

The post-annihilation third step contributes zero. The ordered Newton
matrix and birth matrix are totally nonnegative by the same construction
as above. Nevertheless the first step contributes

 2t^2(5/8)+t(3/8)^2+t(1-t)=291/128,

and the second contributes

 (t+u)(5/8)^2+u(1-u)(5/8)^2=-325/128.

Therefore J=-17/64<0. The hypotheses used to prove the temporal minor
signs all hold, but weighted-energy positivity fails. These scalar data
are a formal certificate test, not an asserted legal interpolation array.

Accordingly, a planar network for A cannot by itself be called a proof of
(16). A successful path argument must ALSO absorb the explicit costs c_i
using the actual relation between ruler levels and chronological mass.

## 7. Current proof state

Complete draft structure: lossless finite birth chain; invariant occupation
budgets (9)-(10); audited total nonnegativity of the actual temporal and
jump matrices; common-height cancellation (13); and the exact conditioned
covariance formula (15), with all cross terms and all penalties retained.

Still open: the uniform quantitative domination (16), or an actual legal
positive-height object escaping its intended bound. Two direct attempts
did not prove it: local scalar square completion discards required past
covariance; comparison to a continuous positive Laplace kernel has no
proved uniform error bound on signed modal combinations. The new ordered
kernel result does not fill this quantitative gap, as Section6 demonstrates.

No whole-interface charge or benchmark-scale legal construction has been
completed. The original problem remains unresolved, and no new fact entered
the graph. The fourth launch's second round does not authorize another round,
numerical budget, or verifier submission.
