<!-- BEGIN_SOURCE_BODY DOC-001211 -->
# Exact variable-word coordinates for the original full certificate space

Status: COMPLETE JOINT RESEARCH DRAFT, UNVERIFIED. This is max2's ONE
analytical round after completed43, under TASK.md and master guidance
1c516691cc4c3011. No numerical evaluator, solver, machine algebra,
fact_submit, consult, or subordinate agent is used. This report supplies
an equality chart and an exact subthreshold starting point, not an
improving seed or a result on the original all-word minimax Goal.

The accepted correctness inputs are facts04219554e41d9750, Section10,
and1252b8823bb5a290, first proof paragraph, for the original coefficient
identity and interpolation orientation. The threshold is the one in
accepted4c92d3fc4eb6153e. Findings c6572c6316ca1876 and
c556fffde4a2c43e are discovery/context only. All new algebra, coverage,
degeneracy analysis, and starting-point estimates used here are proved
together in this evidence, including the reproduced derivation in
Appendix A. No unverified finding is used as a predecessor fact.

## 1. Exact object and the two divergence equations

For an integer r>=1, let h=(h_0,...,h_(r-1)) be an original nonnegative
physical word, s=sum_i h_i, A=1+s, n=r+1, and e the all-ones vector in
R^n. All n original formal gradients remain separate variables. Define

    T_ii=1, T_it=h_t if t<i, T_it=0 if t>i,
    z=T e, so z_i=1+sum_(t<i)h_t and z_r=A,
    Z_diag=diag(z),
    omega=(-2h_0,...,-2h_(r-1),2s)^T,
    D=diag(h_i(2-h_i) for i<r,-s(s+2)).               (1)

For every real n-by-n array Lambda with zero diagonal, set

    L=diag(Lambda e)-Lambda,
    M=D+(L T+T^T L^T)/2.

The equality space of interest is exactly

    L^T e=omega,                 M e=0.             (2)

The original certificate additionally requires Lambda_ij>=0 for EVERY
i!=j and M>=0 on ALL of R^n. At this stage the off-diagonal entries
are allowed to be arbitrary real numbers so that coverage of the whole
equality space, before inequalities, can be established.

For any zero-diagonal real matrix X define two divergences

    d(X)=X e-X^T e,
    d_z(X)=X z-Z_diag X^T e=d(X Z_diag).                      (3)

Both have coordinate sum zero. The weighted matrix in (3) is X Z_diag,
whose (i,j) entry is X_ij z_j; it is NOT Z_diag X. No division by z_i is
involved.

The elementary identity driving the construction is

    2D e+T^T omega=-Z_diag omega.                         (4)

Here is every coordinate of its verification. For i<r put
p_i=sum_(t<i)h_t, so z_i=1+p_i. Then

    (T^T omega)_i=-2h_i+h_i sum_(k=i+1)^r omega_k
                 =-2h_i+2h_i(p_i+h_i).

Adding 2h_i(2-h_i) gives 2h_i(1+p_i)=-z_i omega_i.
At i=r the left side is -2s(s+2)+2s=-2s(s+1), also
-z_r omega_r. Thus (4) holds even at zero steps.

If d(Lambda)=L^T e=omega, then

    2M e=L z-Z_diag omega=-d_z(Lambda).                   (5)

Indeed L z=Z_diag Lambda e-Lambda z, so
Z_diag d(Lambda)-L z=Lambda z-Z_diag Lambda^T e. It follows that

    (2) is equivalent to d(Lambda)=omega,
                         d_z(Lambda)=0.             (6)

No equation has been dropped: the equivalence holds coordinate by
coordinate on all n vertices. The two zero-sum identities are the
only redundant equations on the nondegenerate domains proved below.

Nor does (2) impose an additional restriction on an original PSD
certificate. Under value flow one has

    e^T D e=-(s^2+sum_i h_i^2),
    z^T omega=s^2+sum_i h_i^2,
    e^T M e=0.                                      (7)

For the second equality use
2 sum_i h_i sum_(t<i)h_t=s^2-sum_i h_i^2. If a symmetric
matrix M is PSD and e^T M e=0, then for every real vector v the
nonnegative quadratic (e+t v)^T M(e+t v) has zero constant term;
its linear coefficient 2e^T M v must be zero. Hence M e=0.
Consequently retaining (2), all original signs, and full PSD is exactly
equivalent to the original full certificate conditions at the same h.

## 2. General tree chart: explicit formulas and both directions

Fix ANY undirected spanning tree on {0,...,r}. Give each of its r
edges an orientation u_l -> v_l. Delete that edge and let S_l be
the component containing u_l. Write

    Delta_l=z_(u_l)-z_(v_l).

The chart domain is

    U_tree={h: Delta_l!=0 for every tree edge l}.     (8)

The original positive-word domain (0,infinity)^r is contained in
U_tree for every tree, since z_0<...<z_r. The formulas also apply to
any nonnegative boundary point lying in U_tree. More generally their
algebra is valid for real h in (8), though signed physical steps are
outside the original problem.

For EACH ordered off-diagonal pair whose unordered pair is not a tree
edge, introduce an INDEPENDENT real coordinate N_ij. There are

    n(n-1)-2r=r(r-1)

such coordinates. Define N to have these entries, zero diagonal, and
zero on both directions of each tree edge. For each tree cut compute

    alpha_l=sum_(i in S_l) omega_i
             -sum_(i in S_l,j not in S_l)(N_ij-N_ji),
    beta_l=-sum_(i in S_l,j not in S_l)
                                (N_ij z_j-N_ji z_i). (9)

Terms with tree pairs in these sums are zero by the definition of N.
The chart Phi_tree(h,N) is the following COMPLETE array:

    Lambda_ij=N_ij                         off the tree,
    Lambda_ii=0,
    Lambda_(u_l,v_l)=(z_(u_l) alpha_l-beta_l)/Delta_l,
    Lambda_(v_l,u_l)=(z_(v_l) alpha_l-beta_l)/Delta_l. (10)

Only the explicitly nonzero Delta_l are denominators. In particular
there is no denominator h_i-1, h_i-2, 1-product(1-h_i), determinant
of an unspecified matrix, or fitted completion.

Proof of the construction and coverage. For an edge oriented u->v
with coefficients a=Lambda_uv and b=Lambda_vu, its contributions to
the two divergences in (6) are, respectively,

    (a-b)(e_u-e_v),
    (z_v a-z_u b)(e_u-e_v).                         (11)

Summing either divergence over S_l cancels all edges internal to
S_l or to its complement. The ONLY tree edge crossing S_l is edge l.
Thus (6) requires exactly

    a-b=alpha_l,        z_v a-z_u b=beta_l.           (12)

Solving this displayed two-by-two system with Delta_l!=0 gives (10).
Conversely those formulas satisfy BOTH equations in (12) exactly.

It remains to prove that all these cut equations force every vertex
equation, rather than only some aggregation. Let q be either residual
vector d(Lambda)-omega or d_z(Lambda). Its coordinate sum is zero.
If every tree cut sum of q is zero, choose a root for the tree.
Every subtree cut has zero sum: it is either S_l or its complement,
and q has total sum zero. A nonroot leaf therefore has q_i=0.
Inducting upward, the subtree sum at a nonroot vertex and all its
child subtree sums being zero imply q_i=0. The root then follows
from total sum zero. This proves q=0, and therefore (6) and (2).

Now start with ANY original real Lambda satisfying (2) at h in
U_tree. Read off all its off-tree ordered entries to form N. Its tree
coefficients satisfy (12) by summing the actual vertex equations.
The determinant of (12) is z_v-z_u=-Delta_l, which is nonzero, so
each pair is exactly (10). Thus the inverse is simply

    (h,Lambda) -> (h,(Lambda_ij on all off-tree ordered pairs)). (13)

Equations (10) and (13) are inverse in both directions. They cover
the WHOLE equality space, not just arrays reached from an initial
curve or through a restricted type of correction. For each fixed h
in U_tree the equality space is an affine space of dimension r(r-1).
The equality map has rank 2r. When h also varies, (10) is a smooth
rational chart with r+r(r-1)=r^2 independent real coordinates.
For r=5 this means 20 independent coefficient coordinates and five
independent physical steps. All statements about dimension concern
the equality space; the sign/PSD feasible subset is still constrained.

The tree is a basis for equality elimination ONLY. Non-tree entries
are not set to zero in Lambda; only the auxiliary matrix N sets the
dependent tree positions to zero. Opposite non-tree directions are
independent. There is no reflection condition or repeated first row.

## 3. The requested length-five chart in original index order

Use the path order (0,1,2,5,4,3), oriented in that order. Its data are

    l   (u_l,v_l)    S_l                   Delta_l
    0   (0,1)        {0}                   -h_0
    1   (1,2)        {0,1}                 -h_1
    2   (2,5)        {0,1,2}               -(h_2+h_3+h_4)
    3   (5,4)        {0,1,2,5}              h_4
    4   (4,3)        {0,1,2,5,4}            h_3.         (14)

The first term of alpha_l in (9) is, in the same order,

    -2h_0, -2(h_0+h_1), -2(h_0+h_1+h_2),
                  2(h_3+h_4), 2h_3.                  (15)

Together (1), (9), (10), (14), and (15) are finite explicit formulas
for all ten dependent ordered entries in terms of h and the twenty
independent ordered entries. In particular no generic LinearSolve
operation is needed.

For clarity the ten output locations are, successively,
(01,10), (12,21), (25,52), (54,45), (43,34). In each pair the first
entry is (z_u alpha-beta)/Delta and the second is
(z_v alpha-beta)/Delta, with its corresponding row in (14).

On nonnegative physical words the exact nonsingular domain of THIS
path is h_0,h_1,h_3,h_4>0 and h_2>=0. On the specified positive-word
domain all five h_i>0, all denominators are nonzero. A box such as
h_i>=1/4 is an optional SEARCH restriction. It would bound their
absolute values below by1/4 (and the middle one by3/4), but it is
not a lossless reduction of the original nonnegative-step problem.
No upper capacity or step box has been proved or imposed here.

## 4. Every degeneracy of the tree completion, and boundary coverage

First keep an arbitrary fixed tree and h, allowing Delta_l=0.
The cut equations (12) remain necessary and sufficient: the subtree
proof never divided by Delta. If Delta_l=0, put c_l=z_u=z_v. Then
the exact condition on the independent data at this edge is

    beta_l=c_l alpha_l.                              (16)

If (16) fails, these particular off-tree coordinates admit NO
completion on this tree. If it holds, the entire solution at this
edge is

    Lambda_vu=t_l,       Lambda_uv=t_l+alpha_l,
    t_l any real number.                             (17)

Different degenerate edges introduce independent t_l. At every
nondegenerate edge use (10). These rules and (16) characterize ALL
solutions at the fixed h and fixed off-tree data, including every
simultaneous degeneracy. Sign conditions on (17), when required, are
t_l>=0 and t_l+alpha_l>=0, not automatic consequences of (16).

Here is the actual kernel, not just a determinant diagnosis. With h
and all off-tree entries fixed, a homogeneous change must have zero
net flow on each tree cut, hence delta Lambda_uv=delta Lambda_vu.
Its second cut equation is then -Delta_l delta Lambda_uv=0.
Therefore the kernel has exactly one basis direction per equal-
potential tree edge, adding the same number to its two ordered
coefficients. There are no other kernel directions. The map from
the 2r tree coefficients to the pair of zero-sum divergence vectors
has rank 2r-m when exactly m tree differences vanish.

An explicit diagnostic illustrates why dividing through zero or
declaring every off-tree coordinate free at that point is wrong.
In the requested path take h=(0,1,1,1,1), so z=(1,1,2,3,4,5).
Take N_02=1 and every other off-tree entry zero. At edge(0,1),
alpha_0=-1 and beta_0=-2, whereas c_0 alpha_0=-1. Thus no
completion exists for these off-tree data. Independently, the
homogeneous change delta Lambda_01=delta Lambda_10=t preserves
both divergence equations because z_0=z_1. These are an actual
compatibility obstruction and actual kernel for THIS singular
coordinate choice, not an obstruction to another chart or to the
original certificate cone.

For every nonzero nonnegative word there is an explicit alternative
nonsingular tree retaining all original vertices, even when some
physical steps vanish. Since s>0, z_0=1 and z_r=1+s are distinct.
Use the double-star tree

    {0,r};
    {0,i} for 1<=i<r with z_i>1;
    {r,i} for 1<=i<r with z_i=1.                      (18)

Every vertex other than the two centers is a leaf, and the centers
are joined. Thus (18) is connected with r edges and no cycle. Its
potential differences are all nonzero: -s on (0,r), 1-z_i on
(0,i), and s on (r,i). The general formula (10) applies without
identifying any two formal gradients. This constructs a covering
family of charts for every h>=0 with s>0. On overlaps, changing
charts is simply reconstructing the full Lambda by (10) and taking
the other chart's off-tree entries by (13); coverage is exact.

The remaining stratum is the allzero word h=0. Here T=I, z=e,
D=0, omega=0, and d_z(Lambda)=d(Lambda). The two systems coincide:
the only equality is balanced flow d(Lambda)=0. All equations (16)
hold automatically because beta_l=alpha_l for every edge. Take all
r(r-1) off-tree coordinates and r additional t_l freely, and apply
(17). This gives exactly the whole equality space of dimension r^2
(25 when r=5), whereas the nonzero-word fibre has dimension r(r-1).
The equality rank is r at h=0 and 2r at each nonzero nonnegative h,
the latter by (18) and Section2. This rank jump explains why a single
chart with only r(r-1) coefficient coordinates cannot cover the
origin fibre as an independent-coordinate bijection. It is not a
reason to delete a zero-step query.

For completeness, when h=0 and Lambda>=0 is balanced, M=sym(L)
is already the undirected Laplacian with weights
(Lambda_ij+Lambda_ji)/2, so is PSD: its quadratic form is the sum
of these weights times (g_i-g_j)^2 over i<j. This special boundary
observation is not a PSD shortcut at nonzero words. If r=0, the
empty word has no off-diagonal coefficients, one formal gradient,
and the one-by-one matrices Lambda=M=0; it is handled separately.

These exhaust degeneracies of the stated tree formulas and all
nonnegative-word strata. No exceptional divisions arise at steps1
or2, repeated positive step values, or a singular PSD remainder.

## 5. All signs and the FULL PSD condition remain

On any nonsingular chart impose

    N_ij>=0 on EVERY independent ordered pair,
    (z_u alpha_l-beta_l)/Delta_l>=0,
    (z_v alpha_l-beta_l)/Delta_l>=0 on EVERY tree edge. (19)

There are exactly n(n-1) original sign inequalities. Depending on
the sign of Delta, multiplying one through by Delta reverses its
sense; retaining the displayed quotient avoids that ambiguity.
No sign of a final tree entry is promised for arbitrary N.

The full symmetric matrix is always formed by the original formula
in Section1. Let B be its leading r-by-r principal block and let
one_r be the all-ones vector in R^r. Because M e=0 identically,

    M = [[B, -B one_r],
         [-one_r^T B, one_r^T B one_r]]
      = J B J^T,       J=[[I_r],[-one_r^T]].         (20)

This follows by solving each of the first r row sums for its final
entry and the final row sum for the final diagonal. Consequently,
for EVERY original formal-gradient coordinate vector g,

    g^T M g=(g_(0:r-1)-g_r one_r)^T
                   B(g_(0:r-1)-g_r one_r).          (21)

Thus M>=0 on R^(r+1) if and only if B>=0 on R^r. One direction
also follows by taking g=(y,0); the other follows from (21).
The variables have not been identified: (21) evaluates the original
form for arbitrary independent g_0,...,g_r. Furthermore B>0 if and
only if M is positive definite on e-perp and ker(M)=span{e}.
Indeed the difference vector in (21) vanishes exactly on span{e}.

For completeness the exact Cholesky equivalence, INCLUDING singular
boundary matrices, is

    M>=0  <=>  B>=0  <=>  B=C C^T
    for some real lower triangular r-by-r C with C_jj>=0. (22)

A finite construction proving the last implication is as follows.
Process columns j=0,...,r-1. Set

    p_j=B_jj-sum_(k<j) C_jk^2,
    a_ij=B_ij-sum_(k<j) C_ik C_jk  for i>j.

If p_j>0 take C_jj=sqrt(p_j) and C_ij=a_ij/C_jj. If p_j=0,
require a_ij=0 for all i>j and take the whole remaining part of
column j, including its diagonal, equal to zero. If p_j<0, or if
p_j=0 with some nonzero a_ij, reject PSD. Set C_ij=0 for i<j.

To prove this algorithm covers all PSD matrices, suppose the active
trailing residual S is PSD. If its leading pivot p>0, for any y the
minimum of (x,y)^T S(x,y) over x is
y^T(S_tail-a a^T/p)y; hence that Schur residual is PSD. If p=0,
nonnegativity of (t,e_i)^T S(t,e_i) for every real t forces the
corresponding a_i=0; the trailing principal submatrix is PSD.
This proves the assertion inductively. Conversely completing the
recursion gives B=C C^T entry by entry and therefore PSD. The same
two tests prove that a rejection rules out PSD. No divisions by
zero are concealed. Strictly positive diagonal factors describe
only B>0 and must not be presented as coverage of the whole cone.

At fixed h the entries of B are affine in the independent N entries,
so the exact fibre of feasible certificates is given by the linear
sign inequalities (19) and B>=0. When h varies the displayed
functions are rational; no convexity or improved convergence of
the joint search is claimed. Introducing a factor C and imposing
B=C C^T is an optional equivalent description, with all zero-pivot
cases allowed. It introduces additional variables/equalities and
does not replace the actual matrix formula by a freely chosen PSD
remainder. Nothing here sets M=0 or imposes its rank.

## 6. Exact starting coordinates and inverse-coordinate readback

All formulas in this section specify ONE starting point. They place
no relations on the five physical steps or twenty coefficient
coordinates in the general chart. To reproduce the known dense
point from scratch, define

    a=sqrt(2), lambda=1+a, rho=a-1,
    b=(-a+sqrt(10+8a))/2, p=a+b,
    t=99/100, delta=1/100,
    X=t a, Y=t b, S=X+Y, C0=1+S,
    d=rho C0-delta,
    h_*=(X,Y,1+d,X,Y), A_*=2C0+d,
    c=(C0+d)/(C0-d), k=1+2c,
    tau=delta(2a C0-delta)/(C0-d), eta=2tau,
    u=2S(1+X)/Y, v=2S C0/Y,
    epsilon=1/10000000.                              (23)

C0 is a child capacity, not the matrix C in (22) or the benchmark
sequence. Appendix A uses the letter C for this same child capacity;
it never uses that letter for a Cholesky factor.

The twenty independent starting coordinates are especially short:

    N_32=N_35=2cX+epsilon,
    N_42=2cY+epsilon,
    every other off-tree ordered N_ij=epsilon.       (24)

The full list of twenty locations in original lexicographic order is

    02,03,04,05; 13,14,15; 20,23,24;
    30,31,32,35; 40,41,42; 50,51,53.

In particular there are seventeen entries equal to epsilon. Denote
this independent array by N_*. These coordinates together with h_*
specify the starting point without any unknown completion,
optimization, or numerical fitting.

Here is an exact readback of every dependent coefficient. At this
point

    z=(1,1+X,C0,C0+1+d,A_*-Y,A_*)^T,
    Zsum=sum_i z_i=2+C0+3A_*-2Y,
    K=6I-ee^T,
    F_l=sum_(i in S_l)(K z)_i,
    w_l=F_l/Delta_l,       gamma_l=1-w_l.             (25)

The five Delta_l are (-X,-Y,-(C0+d),Y,X). Written without a cut
operation, the w_l are

    w_0=(Zsum-6)/X,
    w_1=[2Zsum-6(2+X)]/Y,
    w_2=[3Zsum-6(2+X+C0)]/(C0+d),
    w_3=[6(2+X+C0+A_*)-4Zsum]/Y,
    w_4=[Zsum-6(C0+1+d)]/X.                          (26)

Using (24), the alpha and beta from (9) are EXACTLY

    l  alpha_l                    beta_l^0
    0  -2X                         0
    1  -2S                         0
    2   2c(d-1)                    2c C0 S
    3   2kS-2cY                    2c(C0 S+A_* X)
    4   2kX                        2cX(C0+A_*),

    beta_l=beta_l^0-epsilon Delta_l gamma_l.          (27)

To verify (27) directly, remove epsilon first. The only non-tree
entries are then 32 and35 of value2cX and42 of value2cY. Taking
their ordinary and z-weighted cut divergences gives the displayed
table, using c(C0-d)=C0+d and k=1+2c. Reintroducing epsilon on
each ordered non-tree pair is symmetric, so changes no alpha_l.
Its contribution to beta_l is
epsilon sum_(i in S_l,j not in S_l,off tree)(z_i-z_j).
The all-pairs sum is F_l, and the only excluded tree pair crossing
this cut contributes Delta_l. Thus the contribution is
epsilon(F_l-Delta_l)=-epsilon Delta_l gamma_l, as claimed.

Inserting (27) into (10) returns

    Lambda_01=2+epsilon gamma_0,
    Lambda_10=2(1+X)+epsilon gamma_0,
    Lambda_12=u+epsilon gamma_1,
    Lambda_21=v+epsilon gamma_1,
    Lambda_25=2cd+eta+epsilon gamma_2,
    Lambda_52=2c+eta+epsilon gamma_2,
    Lambda_54=kv+epsilon gamma_3,
    Lambda_45=ku+2cY+epsilon gamma_3,
    Lambda_43=2k(1+X)+epsilon gamma_4,
    Lambda_34=2k+epsilon gamma_4.                     (28)

For the first two pairs the baseline weighted difference is zero
because u C0=v(1+X). For pair(2,5), let
Delta_bridge=d A_*-C0^2=-delta(2a C0-delta). Then
(C0+d)eta=-2c Delta_bridge, and

    A_*(2cd+eta)-C0(2c+eta)=2c C0 S.

For the last two pairs use k(C0+d)=c(C0+A_*), giving respectively
z_4 kv-A_*(ku+2cY)=2c(C0 S+A_* X) and
z_3[2k(1+X)]-z_4(2k)=2cX(C0+A_*). Their ordinary differences
are the alpha values in (27). Thus all five baseline pairs solve
(12), and the -epsilon Delta gamma change in beta adds exactly
epsilon gamma to each member of the pair. This verifies every
entry in (28), not merely equality of a scalar objective.

The remaining twenty entries are exactly (24). Reading those
entries back out of this full array by (13) therefore recovers
precisely the twenty input coordinates, and the physical h is
unchanged. Conversely (10) reconstructs all thirty entries. This
is the requested inverse-coordinate readback.

Appendix A below proves, jointly with this chart, the exact margins

    epsilon<=Lambda_ij<97 for ALL i!=j,
    M e=0,
    (1/900)P <= M < (7/2)P,  P=I-ee^T/6,
    B>=(1/5400) I_5,                                (29)

with strict inequalities on e-perp for the full spectral bounds.
The kernel is exactly span{e}; no other original gradient has been
removed. The quotient condition number is less than3150. The
positive Cholesky algorithm in Section5 therefore applies to B:
it gives an exact finite radical expression for a factor, without
running it. Every squared pivot is at least1/5400, because at each
stage minimizing a quadratic bounded below by (1/5400)||x||^2
leaves a Schur complement bounded below by (1/5400)I. Each pivot
is also below7/2. These are bounds, not evaluated eigenvalues or
reported solver output.

The actual capacity remains

    A_*=a+(99/100)[(1+a)(a+b)+1],
    19/2<A_*<48763/5000<49/5<Gamma5_c107.             (30)

Thus (29) is a strict starting margin, not a threshold-crossing
endpoint. The accepted conditional lift4c92d3fc4eb6153e cannot be
invoked to obtain original-target progress from this point. Neither
its readback nor the chart is a proof that the earlier local-search
failure has been diagnosed or cured.

## 7. Finite implementation specification; no execution

The following is pseudocode for the requested original-index path.
It is supplied as text only; it has NOT been run or numerically
evaluated during this round.

    input h[0..4], twenty real coordinates N at the listed locations
    make a 6-by-6 zero matrix N and insert those twenty coordinates
    z[0] = 1
    for i = 0..4: z[i+1] = z[i] + h[i]
    omega = (-2h[0],...,-2h[4],2*sum(h))
    Lambda = N
    for (u,v,S) in the five rows of (14):
        Delta = z[u]-z[v]
        alpha = sum(omega[i], i in S)
                 -sum(N[i,j]-N[j,i], i in S, j outside S)
        beta = -sum(N[i,j]*z[j]-N[j,i]*z[i],
                                              i in S, j outside S)
        if Delta == 0:
            return OUTSIDE_THIS_NONSINGULAR_CHART, with the exact
            compatibility condition and free variable from (16)-(17)
            do not output a completed certificate from this routine
        otherwise:
            Lambda[u,v] = (z[u]*alpha-beta)/Delta
            Lambda[v,u] = (z[v]*alpha-beta)/Delta
    form T,D,L and the FULL M from (1) and Section1
    retain all thirty inequalities Lambda[i,j] >= 0 for i != j
    take B = leading 5-by-5 block of the actual M
    retain B >= 0, equivalently the full PSD condition by (20)-(22)
    output h,Lambda,M,B and the original capacity A=1+sum(h)

All sums contain finitely many explicitly bounded terms (at most
nine crossing unordered pairs in any length-five cut). There is
no unspecified inverse or auxiliary unknown. A straightforward
general-tree implementation costs at most O(r^3) arithmetic
operations using all cut sums and ordinary dense matrix products;
at r=5 the arrays have fixed sizes. This complexity estimate is a
count of the displayed operations, not a performed computation.

For a boundary word, either use the complete formulas (16)-(17)
with their additional inputs and compatibility conditions, or
select the tree (18) FIRST and supply its own independent coordinates.
An incompatible off-tree assignment cannot be repaired merely by
renaming its entries as coordinates of another tree. The transition
map described in Section4 applies to a known full equality array.

For a future search one can use h and N directly, or translate
the twenty coordinates as N=N_*+xi with independent xi. One must
not continue imposing h=(X,Y,1+d,X,Y), equal paired coordinates,
or the symmetric epsilon filling during that general search:
these were formulas for the SINGLE starting point only. In exact
arithmetic value flow and M e=0 hold identically for every point
of the stated chart. Floating-point evaluation can still leave
rounding residuals, particularly near small denominators; no
numerical error or convergence theorem is asserted. The original
sign and full PSD tests remain mandatory.

The full PSD matrix may be reconstructed from B using (20) for
an algebraic representation, but its defining B must always be
computed from the actual original D,L,T. Assigning an arbitrary
B while keeping unrelated multipliers is not this chart.

## 8. Retrieval, status, and limits

The query-memory skill recovered the strict point c6572c6316ca1876,
the actual local-search obstacle eb35e3d52cf6db82, and guidance
1c516691cc4c3011. Fact search did not reveal an already accepted
chart; the relevant accepted coefficient identity was read in

## Appendix A. Joint reproduction of the strict-point proof

The following derivation is included IN FULL as part of this same
evidence. It was rechecked symbolically for this round; it is not
a citation to the unverified prior finding. In this appendix the
child capacity C equals C0 of (23), L_flow=L, D_prim=D,
M_prim=M, and the concrete t is99/100. The auxiliary interval
99/100<=t<1 is used solely to prove the displayed point's bounds.
Its epsilon=(1-t)/100000 is exactly the epsilon in (23) at t=99/100.
Appendix equation numbers are local and separate from (1)-(30).

### A.1. Original notation and the concrete word

Normalize smoothness to1. All six formal gradients g_0,...,g_5 remain
independent variables. For a five-step word h, put s=sum h_i, A=1+s,
and x_i=x_0-sum_(j<i)h_j g_j. The ordered residual and primitive
potential, in the accepted original normalization, are

    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2,
    V_h=2sum_(i<5)h_i(f_i-f_5)
          +sum_(i<5)h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2-s(s+1)||g_5||^2.

Every actual smooth convex trajectory has Q_ij<=0. Set

    T_ii=1, T_it=h_t for t<i, T_it=0 for t>i,
    L_flow=diag(Lambda e)-Lambda,
    omega=(-2h_0,-2h_1,-2h_2,-2h_3,-2h_4,2s)^T,
    D_prim=diag(h_i(2-h_i) for i<5,-s(s+2)),
    M_prim=D_prim+sym(L_flow T),  sym(B)=(B+B^T)/2.

The accepted coefficient identity is

    L_flow^T e=omega  ==>  V_h+sum_(i!=j)Lambda_ij Q_ij
                          =<M_prim,Gram(g_0,...,g_5)>.    (1)

Thus the required object is in the original coordinates, with no
rescaling of Lambda, physical steps, or M_prim. The project retains
W=2R. The ordinary benchmark symbols retain their accepted meanings;
in particular C below is a child capacity, not the sequence C_n.

Define exact algebraic numbers

    a=sqrt(2), lambda=1+a, rho=a-1,
    b=(-a+sqrt(10+8a))/2,  p=a+b.

They satisfy a^2=2, rho=1/lambda, and

    b^2+a b=2(1+a),     p b=2lambda.                     (2)

For the joint derivation allow 99/100<=t<1, and set

    delta=1-t, X=t a, Y=t b, C=1+X+Y,
    d=rho C-delta,
    h=(X,Y,1+d,X,Y),
    A=2C+d=lambda C-delta=a+t(lambda p+1).              (3)

The concrete output means precisely t=99/100, delta=1/100 in every
formula below. There are no parameters to be fitted or solved for.

Here are elementary strict bounds used throughout:

    7/5<a<71/50,  8/5<b<161/100,  3<p<303/100,
    397/100<C<403/100,
    1<X<71/50, 1<Y<161/100,
    3/2<d<17/10,   5/2<1+d<27/10,
    39/100<d/C<21/50,
    (29/50)C<C-d<(61/100)C<3.                          (4)

For completeness, F(u)=u^2+a u-2(1+a) is increasing for u>0.
F(8/5)=14/25-(2/5)a<0. Also
F(161/100)=5921/10000-(39/100)a>383/10000>0.
This proves the bounds on b and then p>3 and p<303/100.
The bounds on C,X,Y follow from t>=99/100. Since
rho>2/5, rho<21/50, delta<=1/100 and C>397/100,

    d>(2/5)(397/100)-1/100>3/2,
    d<(21/50)(403/100)<17/10,
    d/C>2/5-1/397>39/100.

The remaining bounds in(4) follow. Every denominator below is positive.

### A.2. Joint derivation of the full baseline array

First consider the two-step child (X,Y). Put S=X+Y=C-1 and

    u=2S(1+X)/Y=2(p/b)(1+t a),
    v=2SC/Y=2(p/b)C,
    w0=2-X^2=2delta(1+t),
    w1=(S/Y)[2-Y^2+(2-Y)X]
       =2(p/b)delta(1+lambda t).                       (5)

The full child array, in its three original coordinates, is

             0          1          2
    row0     0          2          0
    row1     2(1+X)     0          u
    row2     0          v          0.

Its divergence is (-2X,-2Y,2S), since v-u=2S. Direct substitution in
the original matrix formula gives all its entries as

    [[w0,-w0,0],[-w0,w0+w1,-w1],[0,-w1,w1]].           (6)

For example M_12=[v(Y-1)-u]/2=-w1 and M_22=-S(S+2)+v=w1;
M_00=w0, M_01=-w0 and M_02=0 follow from the first row of the array.
The middle diagonal is
Y(2-Y)+2(1+X)+u(1-Y)=w0+w1: here uY=2S(1+X) and
w1=u-S^2 show the equality by direct expansion.
To obtain the second equality for w1 in(5), expand its bracket as
2+2t a-t^2 b(a+b)=2[1+t a-lambda t^2]
=2(1-t)(1+lambda t), using(2).

Next define

    c=(C+d)/(C-d),  k=1+2c=(3C+d)/(C-d),
    Delta=d(2C+d)-C^2=-delta(2a C-delta),
    tau=-Delta/(C-d)=delta(2a C-delta)/(C-d),
    eta=2tau.                                        (7)

All c,k,tau,eta are positive. Concatenate two copies of(X,Y) with
bridge step1+d. The bridge gradient is g_2 and terminal gradient g_5.
For this paragraph write P=2,Z=5, beta_3=X,beta_4=Y, and let

    E=sum_(j=3,4)beta_j Q_(j,P)+Q_(Z,P)+d Q_(P,Z)
                                      +sum_(j=3,4)beta_j Q_(j,Z).

The value part of E is

    2sum_j beta_j f_j+(d-C)f_P+(2-C-d)f_Z,

and its gradient part after the actual GD substitutions is

    sum_j beta_j(1-beta_j)||g_j||^2
       +[(C-d)/2-d^2]||g_P||^2+(C+d)||g_Z||^2/2.       (8)

Here is the mixed-entry check for(8). In each Q_(j,P), the position
difference is (1+d)g_P+sum_(3<=l<j)beta_l g_l. In Q_(j,Z), it is
-sum_(j<=l<=4)beta_l g_l. For the pair of tail indices3,4 the two
weighted position contributions cancel. Bridge-to-tail terms from
sum beta_j Q_(j,P) equal d sum beta_j<g_j,g_P>, canceled by the
position part of d Q_(P,Z). Tail-to-terminal terms from
sum beta_j Q_(j,Z) equal -sum beta_j<g_j,g_Z>, canceled by Q_(Z,P).
The bridge-to-terminal contributions of Q_(Z,P) and d Q_(P,Z) are
d<g_P,g_Z> and -d<g_P,g_Z>. These exhaust all mixed pairs.
Collecting diagonal terms gives(8).

It now follows coefficient by coefficient that

    V_h=V_left+k V_right-2c E
                         +c Delta(||g_Z||^2-||g_P||^2). (9)

Indeed c(C-d)=C+d matches the value coefficients. For the bridge
diagonal use c Delta=k d^2-C^2; for the terminal diagonal the required
identity is
-(2C+d-1)(2C+d)+k(C-1)C+c(C+d)=c Delta.
Both follow by inserting k=(3C+d)/(C-d). The tail diagonals are
-2c beta_j(1-beta_j), exactly as in(8). Thus no mixed coefficient is
being suppressed in(9).

Add eta to BOTH original coefficients(P,Z) and(Z,P). Set

    U_L=g_0-g_1, V_L=g_1-g_2,
    U_R=g_3-g_4, V_R=g_4-g_5,
    zeta=g_2-g_5, J=X U_R+(X+Y)V_R.

The exact identity

    Q_(P,Z)+Q_(Z,P)
      =-d||zeta||^2-(C+d)<zeta,g_Z>-<zeta,J>

follows from x_P-x_Z=(1+d)g_P+Xg_3+Yg_4. Since
tau=-c Delta/(C+d), adding eta times this identity to the last term
in(9) gives tau[(C-d)||zeta||^2-2<zeta,J>].

Consequently the baseline full array Lambda^0 is

          0          1       2         3          4          5
    row0  0          2       0         0          0          0
    row1  2(1+X)     0       u         0          0          0
    row2  0          v       0         0          0          2cd+eta
    row3  0          0       2cX       0          2k         2cX
    row4  0          0       2cY       2k(1+X)    0          ku+2cY
    row5  0          0       2c+eta    0          kv         0.       (10)

Its exact full remainder is

    M^0[g]=w0||U_L||^2+w1||V_L||^2
          +k(w0||U_R||^2+w1||V_R||^2)
          +tau[(C-d)||zeta||^2-2<zeta,J>].             (11)

This also gives M^0 e=0 directly. For an independent full flow check,
the first two divergences of(10) are -2X,-2Y. The other four are

    v-u+2c(d-C)=-2(1+d),
    4cX-2kX=-2X,
    4cY+2kX+k(u-v)=-2Y,
    k(v-u)+2c(2-C-d)=2(2C+d-1)=2s.                   (12)

Thus every one of the six original flow equations is satisfied.

### A.3. A quantitative full baseline margin

Complete the square in(11). Define

    R=X^2/w0+(X+Y)^2/w1=Rbar/delta,
    Rbar=t^2[1/(1+t)+lambda/(1+lambda t)],
    q=tau/(C-d), sigma=k-qR,
    E0=zeta-J/(C-d).

The identity for R follows from a^2=2 and pb=2lambda. Both summands
in Rbar are increasing for t>=0, by differentiating t^2/(1+t) and
lambda t^2/(1+lambda t). Therefore

    Rbar<=1/2+lambda/(1+lambda)=lambda/2.              (13)

For our whole parameter interval sigma>5/2. To see this, (4) gives

    k>(3+39/100)/(1-39/100)=339/61>11/2,
    qR=(2a C-delta)Rbar/(C-d)^2
       <(7/2)C/[(29/50)^2 C^2]
       =8750/(841C)<17500/5887<3,                    (14)

where a lambda=2+a<7/2 and C>7/2 were used. Hence k-qR>5/2.

The exact sum of squares is

    M^0[g]=w0||U_L||^2+w1||V_L||^2
          +sigma(w0||U_R||^2+w1||V_R||^2)
          +q||(X+Y)sqrt(w0/w1)U_R-Xsqrt(w1/w0)V_R||^2
          +tau(C-d)||E0||^2.                         (15)

Expanding the fourth term uses
R(w0||U_R||^2+w1||V_R||^2)-||J||^2
=||(X+Y)sqrt(w0/w1)U_R-Xsqrt(w1/w0)V_R||^2.
Thus(15) is an identity on the entire original space.

We now turn it into an explicit Euclidean margin. From(4)-(5),(14),

    w0>3delta, w1>3delta,
    sigma w0>(15/2)delta, sigma w1>(15/2)delta,
    tau(C-d)=delta(2a C-delta)>11delta.               (16)

Also

    alpha=X/(C-d)<7100/11513<2/3,
    beta=(X+Y)/(C-d)<15150/11513<4/3.

Since zeta=E0+alpha U_R+beta V_R, Cauchy--Schwarz gives

    ||zeta||^2<=3||E0||^2+(4/3)||U_R||^2+(16/3)||V_R||^2.

Define the unweighted tree energy

    E_tree=||U_L||^2+||V_L||^2+||zeta||^2
                                +||V_R||^2+||U_R||^2.

It is at most
||U_L||^2+||V_L||^2+(7/3)||U_R||^2+(19/3)||V_R||^2+3||E0||^2.
Each of these coefficients is dominated by its corresponding
coefficient in(16) after division by delta. Dropping the fourth
nonnegative square in(15) consequently gives

    M^0[g]>=delta E_tree.                            (17)

The tree is the path with vertex order v=(0,1,2,5,4,3). If g is a
scalar vector with sum g_i=0, then

    ||g||^2=(1/6)sum_(i<j)(g_i-g_j)^2.

Bound each difference by Cauchy--Schwarz along its path. The total
coefficients of the five successive squared edge differences are
15,24,27,24,15. For example the middle one is
sum_(i=0,1,2)sum_(j=3,4,5)(j-i)=27; the other four follow from
the same finite sums. Therefore ||g||^2<=(9/2)E_tree, and

    g^T M^0 g >= (2delta/9)||g||^2   for g perpendicular to e. (18)

This argument is also valid coordinate by coordinate for vector
gradients. No equality between two original formal gradients is used.

### A.4. Exact tree compensation that fills every missing coefficient

This is the complete symmetric filling and compensation step. Let e_i be the original coordinate
vectors, retain v=(0,1,2,5,4,3), and put

    z=T e=(1,1+X,C,C+1+d,A-Y,A)^T,
    Z=sum_i z_i=2+C+3A-2Y,
    K=6I-ee^T,
    b_l=e_(v_l)-e_(v_(l+1))             for l=0,...,4,
    S_l={v_0,...,v_l},
    F_l=sum_(i in S_l)(Kz)_i,
    w_l^tree=F_l/(z_(v_l)-z_(v_(l+1))),
    gamma_l=1-w_l^tree.                              (19)

The edge differences in their stated order are

    -X, -Y, -(C+d), Y, X.

They are all nonzero, with absolute value greater than1. To eliminate
any implicit linear solve, the five weights are explicitly

    w_0^tree=(Z-6)/X,
    w_1^tree=[2Z-6(2+X)]/Y,
    w_2^tree=[3Z-6(2+X+C)]/(C+d),
    w_3^tree=[6(2+X+C+A)-4Z]/Y,
    w_4^tree=[Z-6(C+1+d)]/X.                         (20)

Define the symmetric matrix

    H=K-sum_(l=0)^4 w_l^tree b_l b_l^T.              (21)

We claim H e=H z=0, as exact identities. The first is immediate
from the displayed matrices. For the second, set F_(-1)=F_5=0.
At vertex v_l, the vector sum_l F_l b_l has entry F_l-F_(l-1)
=(Kz)_(v_l); at the last vertex this remains true because sum_i(Kz)_i=0.
But (b_l b_l^T)z=(z_(v_l)-z_(v_(l+1)))b_l, so(19) proves H z=0.
This is a full coordinate check; it does not infer preservation of
the kernel merely from conservation of total flow.

Choose the exact positive fill size

    epsilon=delta/100000.                            (22)

For unordered tree pairs {v_l,v_(l+1)}, add epsilon gamma_l to BOTH
ordered coefficients. For every other distinct pair, add epsilon.
Equivalently, with all entries in original index order, the final
array is the following complete specification:

         0                     1                    2                    3                    4                    5
 row0    0                     2+eps*gamma0          eps                  eps                  eps                  eps
 row1    2(1+X)+eps*gamma0      0                    u+eps*gamma1         eps                  eps                  eps
 row2    eps                   v+eps*gamma1          0                    eps                  eps                  2cd+eta+eps*gamma2
 row3    eps                   eps                  2cX+eps              0                    2k+eps*gamma4         2cX+eps
 row4    eps                   eps                  2cY+eps              2k(1+X)+eps*gamma4    0                    ku+2cY+eps*gamma3
 row5    eps                   eps                  2c+eta+eps*gamma2     eps                  kv+eps*gamma3         0.           (23)

Here eps is epsilon and gamma0,...,gamma4 mean gamma_0,...,gamma_4
from(19)-(20). In particular the diagonal is exactly zero.

Let L^0=diag(Lambda^0 e)-Lambda^0. The signed symmetric change in(23)
has Laplacian epsilon H, so the resulting ORIGINAL quantities are

    L_flow=L^0+epsilon H,
    M_prim=M^0+epsilon sym(H T).                     (24)

Since H^T e=H e=0, (12) proves L_flow^T e=omega exactly. Moreover

    sym(H T)e=(H z+T^T H e)/2=0.                     (25)

Thus the full common-gradient kernel equation is also exact. Equations
(11),(20)-(24) give the entire M_prim, including every mixed entry.

It would not be valid to add arbitrary positive entries without this
compensation. For example a nonzero symmetric nonnegative addition
with Laplacian N and N z=0 would satisfy
z^T N z=sum_(i<j)Nweight_ij(z_i-z_j)^2=0. Here every z_i is distinct,
so every added weight would have to vanish. The signed compensation
on already positive tree edges is essential. The final coefficients,
as opposed to their increments, are all positive by the next section.

### A.5. All signs and the full perturbed spectral margin

First bound every tree weight in(19). Because K is the complete-graph
Laplacian,

    F_l=sum_(i in S_l,j not in S_l)(z_i-z_j).

Every difference has absolute value at most s=A-1<9. There are at
most9 crossing unordered pairs, since |S_l|(6-|S_l|)<=9. Hence

    |F_l|<81,  |w_l^tree|<81,  |gamma_l|<82.          (26)

Every baseline coefficient on a tree pair, in both directions, is
at least2. To check the bridge pair, c>1 and d>3/2 imply2cd+eta>3,
and2c+eta>2. On the two child pairs this follows directly from(5)
and(10). Since epsilon<=1/10000000, each final tree coefficient is

    Lambda_ij >=2-82epsilon >epsilon.               (27)

Every non-tree pair has a nonnegative baseline coefficient and is
increased by epsilon. Thus ALL30 final ordered coefficients obey
Lambda_ij>=epsilon. This includes edges that were positive in only
one direction in the baseline; neither direction is deleted.

For explicit upper ranges, c<71/29<5/2, k<6 and tau<5delta, so
eta<10delta<=1/10. From p/b<303/160 one obtains

    u<10, v<16,
    2cd+eta<10, 2c+eta<6,
    2cX<8, 2cY<9, 2k(1+X)<30,
    ku+2cY<69, kv<96.

Together with(26) these prove, uniformly on the stated t interval,

    epsilon<=Lambda_ij<97 for i!=j,
    |Lambda_ij-Lambda^0_ij|<82epsilon.               (28)

For instance v<16 follows from
2(303/160)(403/100)=244218/16000<16, and
tau<2a delta/(2-a)<(142/29)delta<5delta.
These are proved ranges, not the coordinate boxes of any earlier
numerical search. The original array has not been rescaled to fit
such boxes.

Next control the ENTIRE matrix perturbation. All operator norms below
are Euclidean. K has norm6, each b_l b_l^T has norm2, and(26) gives

    ||H||<6+5*81*2=816.

There are six diagonal entries1 and fifteen strictly lower triangular
entries in T; every physical step is less than3. Consequently

    ||T||<=||T||_F<sqrt(6+15*9)=sqrt141<12,
    ||sym(H T)||<=||H|| ||T||<9792<10000.             (29)

For every g perpendicular to e, (18),(22),(24),(29) give

    g^T M_prim g > (2delta/9-delta/10)||g||^2
                   =(11delta/90)||g||^2
                   >(delta/9)||g||^2.              (30)

Equation(25) and M^0 e=0 then imply positivity on the FULL R^6,
with kernel exactly span{e}. In particular, no omitted off-diagonal
entry, hidden gradient identification, or principal-block shortcut is
needed for(30).

A conservative upper bound also quantifies conditioning in this
coordinate system. In(11), w0<4delta and w1<14delta. The norms of
the four child squared-difference matrices are2, and k<6, giving
an upper norm bound252delta for their sum. The vector defining J
is (0,0,0,X,Y,-X-Y), whose norm is less than4:
X<3/2, Y<2, X+Y<31/10 give its squared norm less than16.
The vector defining zeta has norm sqrt2. Since C-d<3 and tau<5delta,
the norm of the last part of(11) is less than

    tau[2(C-d)+2sqrt2*4]<18tau<90delta.

It follows that ||M^0||<342delta. Adding(29) costs less than delta/10,
so, conservatively, ||M_prim||<350delta. With P=I-ee^T/6 this proves

    (delta/9)P <= M_prim <=350delta P,               (31)

where both inequalities are strict on e-perp. The condition number
on that complement is therefore less than3150. This is a bound for
this matrix in the stated Euclidean coordinates, not a claim about
the condition number of an entire QCQP or a nonlinear solver.

Let B be the leading five-by-five principal block of M_prim. For
y in R^5 and g=(y,0),
||Pg||^2=||y||^2-(sum y_i)^2/6>=||y||^2/6. Hence

    B >= (delta/54)I_5.                             (32)

At t=99/100, equations(22),(28),(31),(32) give exactly the advertised
margins epsilon=1/10000000, full eigenvalue bound1/900 on e-perp,
upper bound7/2 there, and leading-block bound1/5400.

### A.6. Capacity and exact limits of the result

The concrete capacity is exactly

    A=a+(99/100)[(1+a)(a+b)+1].                      (33)

Using a>7/5, lambda>12/5 and p>3 gives
A>7/5+(99/100)(41/5)=4759/500>19/2. On the other hand

    A<lambda(1+p)<(121/50)(403/100)
                        =48763/5000<49/5,
    Gamma5_c107-49/5=78807/806000>0.                 (34)

Thus the sufficient hypothesis of accepted4c92d3fc4eb6153e is NOT
satisfied. Nothing here asserts an improving seed or a result on
the original asymptotic minimax target.

# Strict simplex witnesses for primitive failure

Status: complete candidate, NOT verifier-accepted. No new fact_submit or
numerical search is allocated. The proof uses the interpolation necessity
in accepted fact 1252b8823bb5a290. The public projection/saturation findings
20c6d21ee60a4cb0 and 4ad60ca9f3ec3e2e were leads, not premises.
All new steps, including the independent-gradient perturbation, are below.

## 1. Exact statement

For a finite nonnegative physical schedule h=(h_0,...,h_(n-1)), put
S=sum h_i and define, on any actual convex differentiable 1-smooth GD run,

    V_h=2 sum_(i<n) h_i(f_i-f_n)
        +sum_(i<n) h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2
        -S(S+1)||g_n||^2.                            (1)

Call h primitive when V_h>=0 for every such finite-dimensional run with
a minimizer. Let m be the number of strictly positive steps of h.

The following conditions are equivalent:

* h is primitive;
* for every finite-dimensional compact convex polytope C containing0,
  the function F_C(x)=(||x||^2-dist(x,C)^2)/2 and its GD run satisfy
  ||x_n+S P_C(x_n)||<=||x_0||;
* the same inequality holds for every full-dimensional simplex in
  R^(m+1) with vertices 0,v_0,...,v_m.

More strongly, if h fails primitivity, a negative witness can be chosen
on a simplex of the last form with its m+1 distinct compressed query
gradients exactly v_0,...,v_m. Every projection normal inequality at
one of these query vertices is STRICT against each different vertex,
including zero. Duplicates introduced by zero steps are simply restored.
Thus there are exactly m+2 vertices, dimension m+1, and no limiting
interpolant. The original step values and order are unchanged.

The construction preserves a strictly negative intrinsic energy. It
does NOT preserve the normalized objective error, or the initial distance.
Section7 gives a fixed triangle where the absolute negative energy stays
constant while even the objective normalized by the closest-minimizer
distance tends to zero.

## 2. Projection functions and every normal inequality

Let C be a nonempty compact convex set containing0. The unique maximizer
of <x,v>-||v||^2/2 over v in C is its nearest point p=P_C(x), by completing
the square. Therefore

    F_C(x)=max_(v in C)(<x,v>-||v||^2/2)
          =(||x||^2-dist(x,C)^2)/2.                   (2)

The nearest-point condition is

    <x-p,v-p><=0 for EVERY v in C.                   (3)

Necessity follows by differentiating squared distance along the segment
from p to v; sufficiency follows by expanding ||x-v||^2. Applied twice,
(3) gives ||P_C(x)-P_C(y)||^2<=<P_C(x)-P_C(y),x-y>, so P_C is
1-Lipschitz. Comparing the maxima in (2) gives

    0<=F_C(x+d)-F_C(x)-<P_C(x),d>
      <=<P_C(x+d)-P_C(x),d><=||d||^2.

Consequently F_C is convex, differentiable, and 1-smooth, with gradient
P_C. It is nonnegative and F_C(0)=0. Its minimizer set is precisely the
polar cone C^polar={z:<z,v><=0 for every v in C}: use (3) with p=0.

For proposed query data X_i,G_i, define

    Y_i=X_i-G_i,  F_i=<G_i,X_i>-||G_i||^2/2.

On C=conv{0,G_0,...,G_m}, the COMPLETE projection conditions are

    <Y_i,G_j-G_i><=0 for ALL i,j,
    <Y_i,-G_i><=0 for ALL i.                         (4)

Convex combinations then give (3) for every retained point. No adjacent
or chronological subset replaces (4).

## 3. A finite strict perturbation with independent gradients

Compress zero steps from the supplied negative witness. A zero step repeats
the point, gradient, and function value. Its coefficients in (1) vanish,
and S and the final jet are unchanged, so V_h is unchanged. If m=0 then
V_h=0, and there was no negative witness. Hence assume m>=1 and the
compressed steps are all positive; write their actual energy as -v_def<0.

Construct an auxiliary quadratic on R^(m+1): let

    theta_l=(l+1)/(2(m+2)), 0<=l<=m,
    A_aux=diag(theta_0,...,theta_m),
    psi(z)=<z,A_aux z>/2,  z_0=(1,...,1).

Run the same compressed schedule and denote its jets by z_i,w_i,psi_i.
If P_i(z)=product_(t<i)(1-h_t z), its coordinate gradients are
w_(i,l)=theta_l P_i(theta_l). These m+1 gradients are linearly independent.
Indeed the P_i have distinct degrees 0,...,m and nonzero leading
coefficients. A linear combination vanishing at the m+1 distinct theta_l
is a polynomial of degree at most m with m+1 roots, hence is zero; the
distinct-degree basis then makes every coefficient zero.

Every two sample positions z_i,z_j are distinct, since P_i-P_j is a
nonzero polynomial of degree at most m and cannot vanish at all theta_l.
For the signed residual convention from accepted fact 1252b8823bb5a290,

    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2,

direct substitution for this auxiliary quadratic gives

    Q_ij^aux=-<z_i-z_j,A_aux(I-A_aux)(z_i-z_j)>/2<0    (i!=j). (5)

All theta_l lie strictly between0 and1. Let E_aux be the finite energy
of this explicitly given quadratic run. Choose

    epsilon=v_def/[2(v_def+|E_aux|)] in(0,1/2].       (6)

Take the fixed product function

    f_mix(x,z)=f_original(x)+epsilon psi(z/sqrt(epsilon)).

Start it at (x_0,sqrt(epsilon)z_0). Its sampled points and gradients are
(x_i,sqrt(epsilon)z_i) and (g_i,sqrt(epsilon)w_i), with values
f_i+epsilon psi_i. It is convex differentiable 1-smooth, and all GD
equations are exact. Energy and every Q are additive across these
coordinates. Hence its energy is

    V_mix=-v_def+epsilon E_aux<=-v_def/2<0,           (7)

its gradients are independent, and all finite distinct-sample Q_ij
are strictly negative by (5). Rename these mixed data x_i,g_i,f_i.

## 4. One origin/value shift makes an actual simplex trajectory

Set

    f_* = min_i(f_i-||g_i||^2/2)-1,
    r_i = f_*-f_i+<g_i,x_i>-||g_i||^2/2.

Let A_gram=(<g_i,g_j>)_(0<=i,j<=m), which is positive definite.
Define z_*=sum_j (A_gram^(-1)r)_j g_j. Then <g_i,z_*>=r_i.
Set X_i=x_i-z_* and F_i=f_i-f_*. The GD equations are unchanged,
and the defining equalities give

    F_i=<g_i,X_i>-||g_i||^2/2.                        (8)

For all finite ordered pairs, the Q residual is unchanged and is
strictly negative. Substituting (8) into it gives

    Q_ij=<X_j-g_j,g_i-g_j><0 for i!=j.               (9)

Moreover <X_i-g_i,g_i>=F_i-||g_i||^2/2>=1. These are ALL the
normal inequalities (4), including strict inequalities against zero.
Consequently the fixed polytope C=conv{0,g_0,...,g_m} satisfies
P_C(X_i)=g_i and F_C(X_i)=F_i at every compressed query.
Thus it is an actual globally legal projection-function realization;
we did not just assign independent values and gradients.

The g_i are linearly independent, so these m+2 points are all vertices
of a simplex of dimension m+1. Orthogonally project all X_i onto
W=span{g_0,...,g_m}. Gradients, values, GD equations, and all inequalities
(9) are unchanged. The removed component is constant along the run.
This puts the entire realization in dimension m+1. All initial points
are nonzero, since <g_0,X_0>=F_0+||g_0||^2/2>0.

Translation of the points and a common shift of the values leave (1)
unchanged; orthogonal compression does too. Therefore its final energy
is exactly V_mix<0. Restoring the original zero steps repeats the
corresponding actual jets and keeps the same polytope, all physical
steps, and the energy. This proves the claimed finite sign reduction.

## 5. The norm identity, including the discarded-slack term

For arbitrary original legal data with minimizer0 and value0 define

    I_i=<g_i,x_i>-f_i-||g_i||^2/2>=0,
    J=sum_(i<n)h_i I_i.

The GD squared-distance telescope gives

    V_h=||x_0||^2-||x_n+Sg_n||^2+2(S I_n-J).         (10)

Indeed ||x_0||^2-||x_n||^2
=2sum h_i<g_i,x_i>-sum h_i^2||g_i||^2; substitute
<g_i,x_i>=f_i+||g_i||^2/2+I_i and expand the final square.

For projection functions, (2) makes every I_i exactly zero. Therefore
the proposed physical-step formula is exactly

    V_h=||x_0||^2-||x_n+S P_C(x_n)||^2.             (11)

It is generally false without the extra slack in (10). Combining (11)
with sections2--4 proves all equivalences in section1. No missing
function values were dropped: they determine r, z_*, and the resulting
positions relative to the simplex.

## 6. A precise full Gram game and its scope

For the compressed length m define the lower-triangular matrix T_h by
(T_h)_ii=1, (T_h)_ij=h_j for j<i, and zero for j>i. Let A_gram be
the positive definite gradient Gram matrix and b_i=<g_i,X_0>.
The complete normal inequalities are precisely

    b_j-b_i <= (T_h A_gram)_ij-(T_h A_gram)_ii   for all i,j,
    b_i >= (T_h A_gram)_ii                      for all i. (12)

The strict simplex construction makes all nontrivial inequalities strict.
Conversely any positive definite A_gram and b satisfying (12) have a
realization: choose independent vectors with this Gram matrix, and set
X_0=sum_j(A_gram^(-1)b)_j g_j. Equations (12) then prove all projection
conditions for the specified simplex and prescribed GD trajectory.

Put d_i=h_i for i<m, d_m=-S. Then sum_i d_i=0 and

    V_h=2 b^T d-d^T A_gram d,
    ||X_0||^2=b^T A_gram^(-1)b.                       (13)

These formulas retain every queried vertex. They are a lossless
finite-dimensional game for primitive validity, not yet a capacity bound.
In particular, no conclusion S+1<=(m+1)^(log_2(1+sqrt(2))) is inferred.

Adding a common constant to every b_i preserves the finite differences
and the primitive energy, and can enforce the origin inequalities. It
changes the initial norm in (13); it therefore cannot be used freely in
an objective-normalized lower bound.

## 7. An exact triangle, and a quantitative normalization failure

Take the one-step schedule h=(3/2), the triangle

    v_0=(1,0),  v_1=(-2/5,1/10), C=conv{0,v_0,v_1},
    X_0=(2,267/20), X_1=(1/2,267/20).

The two query normals are Y_0=(1,267/20) and Y_1=(9/10,53/4).
ALL nontrivial projection comparisons are

    <Y_0,v_1-v_0>=<Y_1,v_0-v_1>=-13/200,
    <Y_0,-v_0>=-1,  <Y_1,-v_1>=-193/200.

Thus these are strict vertex projections, and their complete values are
F_C(X_0)=3/2, F_C(X_1)=21/20. The norm game gives

    V_h=72889/400-72904/400=-3/80<0.                  (14)

For every T>=0 put u=(1,14), X_i(T)=X_i+T u. Since
<u,v_0>=<u,v_1>=1, every finite normal difference above is unchanged,
and the origin comparisons only become more negative. Hence the SAME
fixed triangle realizes the same gradient trajectory for every T,

    F_C(X_i(T))=F_C(X_i)+T,
    V_h(T)=-3/80.

Its minimizer cone is {z:z_1<=0, z_2<=4z_1}. Both coordinates of
X_0(T) are positive, while both coordinates of a cone point are
nonpositive. Expanding squared distances proves that zero is the
closest minimizer to X_0(T). Therefore the genuine normalized terminal
objective is

    [21/10+2T]/[(2+T)^2+(267/20+14T)^2] ->0.          (15)

This is not merely an arbitrary poor choice of minimizer. It proves that
an absolute negative primitive margin alone cannot lower-bound the
normalized objective even on one fixed simplex and one fixed schedule.
It does not upper-bound the worst case for that schedule: other instances
may give a much larger value.

The mechanism holds on every independent-gradient simplex. Let u be
the unique vector in the gradient span with <u,g_i>=1 for all i.
Translation by T u preserves all finite normal inequalities and shifts
all sample values by T, while improving the origin comparisons.
Since sum d_i=0, it leaves V_h unchanged. The closest-minimizer distance
is Theta(T): for each cone point z, <g_i,z><=0, so
||X_0+Tu-z||>=(T+<g_i,X_0>)/||g_i|| for sufficiently large T;
the upper bound O(T) follows by choosing z=0. Thus (15)'s dilution
is a general geometric feature, not a special coordinate artifact.

## 8. A failed capacity-witness simplification

A natural attempt to make the simplex game tractable is to require all
nonzero visited vertices to have one common radius. This is NOT a
universal class of primitive counterexamples, even at length1.

Suppose 0<=h_i<2 and every nonzero sampled projection gradient has norm R.
If both consecutive gradients are nonzero, projection cocoercivity gives

    ||g_i-g_(i+1)||^2
      <=h_i<g_i,g_i-g_(i+1)>
      =(h_i/2)||g_i-g_(i+1)||^2.

They must therefore be equal. If a zero gradient is ever reached, that
point is a minimizer and all later gradients are zero. Before that time
the gradient is constant. If no zero is reached, x_n+Sg_n=x_0 and V_h=0.
If a zero is reached, each h_i<2 step decreases squared distance to zero:
the vertex0 inequality gives <x_i,g_i>>=||g_i||^2, hence

    ||x_(i+1)||^2<=||x_i||^2-h_i(2-h_i)||g_i||^2.

Thus (11) is nonnegative again. In particular equal-radius vertex walks,
including regular-simplex vertex walks, cannot reject h=(3/2), although
the explicit strict triangle (14) does reject it. Allowing projections
onto nonvertex faces is outside this failed simplification.

The exact general simplex reduction succeeds. The equal-radius capacity
ansatz fails, and the unrestricted capacity inequality remains open.
