# A growing shifted-circle chain: actual coupled dynamics and the unpaid input flux

Status: JOINT UNVERIFIED ANALYTICAL DRAFT. The growing policy passes the
scale screen. Its complete retained terminal payoff is NOT bounded sharply
enough here to prove either a benchmark witness or a complete obstruction.
A specific nonnegative input flux remains unpaid. No formal gate was
allocated; no assertion below has been promoted to a fact.

Project gd-exact-profile-20260926; worker max3; one round after baseline59,
completed ceiling60 / attempted ceiling61. TASK.md and guidance
7b221a98307db859 govern. Frozen-policy finding: eec80a72df26e757.
No construction repair was used. Previous drafts4c4a21a484564bdc and
ced9f5595b446c1d are context, not accepted premises. All ingredients below
are derived jointly. The only accepted mathematical input is the original
benchmark/ray conclusion of fact04219554e41d9750.

## 1. Frozen policy and the actual problem left open

For each integer m>=16 put

    lambda=1+sqrt(2), tau=1/4, k=floor(sqrt(m)), J=m-k,
    beta=1/k, eta_word=1-1/(4m), c=lambda*(1+1/(4m)),
    a_word=eta_word/c,
    H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,b_j,H_j), b_j=1+a_word*c^j,
    S_j=sum H_j, n_m=2^m-1.

All children retain the SAME enclosing m and eta_word,c,a_word. Circular
beta differs from eta_word. Select precisely the first occurrences of
levels J,J+1,...,m-1: k physical steps at zero-based positions
2^(J+i-1)-1 for i=1,...,k. No other step is changed or removed. The
unchanged word is exactly

    (H_J,b_J,H_J,b_(J+1),H_(J+1),b_(J+2),...,
                                      H_(m-2),b_(m-1),H_(m-1)).       (1.1)

Thus ordinary block masses are s_1=S_J, s_i=S_(J+i-2) for 2<=i<=k,
and s_(k+1)=S_(m-1). Selected step i is b_(J+i-1). These steps exceed2
for all sufficiently large m, the range used for all eventual conclusions.

The original benchmark, never recomputed here, is

    C_0=B_0=1,
    C_n=max_(0<=r<n) Omega(C_r,C_(n-r-1)),
    B_n=max_(0<=r<n) G(C_r,B_(n-r-1)),
    Omega(x,y)=(x+y+sqrt((x+y)^2+4xy))/2,
    G(x,y)=2x+y/2+sqrt(y^2+8xy)/2, U_n^ZJ=1/B_n.

Every integer split r is included. Fact04219554e41d9750 gives

    0<Z_1=lim_m B_(2^m-1)/lambda^m<infinity.                       (1.2)

For the static convex1-smooth F below with minimum0, write
E_m=2F(x_(n_m))/dist(x_0,argmin F)^2. Multiplying F by any advertised
L>0 gives x^+=x-(h/L)grad(LF)=x-h grad F and the same payoff. This is
a particular lower witness for W_n(h), not an identity for its supremum.
The construction gives the EXACT decomposition

    B_(n_m) E_m = T_m+B_(n_m) V_m/R_m^2,                         (1.3)
    T_m -> T_infty:=Z_1*exp(-17/4)/(2lambda)>0.                   (1.4)

V_m retains ALL k circular values at the actual terminal iterate; R_m
is the true closest initial distance. Equation(1.4) is a scale screen,
not the requested retained-history result. The unpaid term is identified
in Sections7--10, including all ordinary steps and large overshoots.

## 2. Preliminary scale screen

Sections3--6 jointly prove, uniformly over these k blocks,

    C_beta=beta+sqrt(1+2beta),
    M_i=C_beta*s_i/2+O(k^2 exp(2k)), R_m^2->1,
    D_(k+1)->(lambda-2)exp(-2), sigma_1~2/(C_beta*S_J).           (2.1)

The overlap smoothness bound remains at most1 for every k, so dimension
does not dilute the normalization. The final Huber term consequently
has the positive benchmark-scale limit(1.4), with no loss exponential
in k. These statements are proved below, not assumed as premises.

The retained caps and radius alone also do not force a deficit. For the
SAME function, the comparison point z=(0,-1,0,...,0) has norm1 and

    Phi_1(z_1,z_2)=sigma_1-(1+beta^2)*sigma_1^2/2,

while every later circular component is zero. Therefore

    B_(n_m)*Phi_1(z_1,z_2)/R_m^2
                  ~2Z_1*exp(-1/4)*lambda^k ->infinity.          (2.2)

This is solely a cap/radius screen. The point z is NOT asserted to be
on the actual trajectory. It shows why a geometric cap bound cannot
reject this policy as trivially diluted. The actual terminal V_m still
has to be paid; neither(2.2) nor(1.4) is a retained-history solution.

## 3. Circular envelope and every local physical step

For 0<beta<=1/4 set

    p(q)=beta+sqrt((1+beta)^2-(q+beta)^2), 0<=q<=1,
    v(q)=(p(q),-q), K=conv({0} union {v(q):0<=q<=1}).

The disk of center(beta,beta), radius1+beta contains0 because
2beta^2<=(1+beta)^2. Its radial vector(p(q)-beta,-q-beta) supports
K at v(q). The endpoints are(C_beta,0),(beta,-1), and the horizontal
segment from0 to(C_beta,0) is in K. Hence

    0<=p<=C_beta, 0<=q<=1, p>=beta q,
    (p,0) in K whenever(p,-q) in K.                              (3.1)

For compact convex C containing0 define
E_C(r)=max_(g in C){<g,r>-||g||^2/2}. Completing the square gives
the unique maximizer Pi_C(r), the Euclidean projection. Its inequalities
at r,s give ||Pi_C(r)-Pi_C(s)||^2<=<Pi_C(r)-Pi_C(s),r-s>.
Comparing maximization formulas at r,r+d bounds the increment after
subtracting <Pi_C(r),d> by ||d||^2. Thus E_C is differentiable, convex,
nonnegative and1-smooth with gradient Pi_C. Its zero set is the polar
cone: a positive support value gives a positive objective at a small
positive multiple of the supporting vector. The cone of K is generated
by its endpoints, so

    E_(sigma K)(U,V)=0 iff U<=0 and beta U-V<=0.                  (3.2)

Define Hub_delta(t) to be0 for t<=0, t^2/2 for 0<=t<=delta, and
delta*t-delta^2/2 for t>=delta; its derivative is clip(t,0,delta).

Take an ordinary block a_1,...,a_N in its physical order, alpha_j=a_j/2.
Construct r_j=(u_j,-y_j) backwards from r_N=(beta,-1). For alpha_j>0
choose the unique q_(j-1) in[0,1) satisfying

    (q_(j-1)+beta)/(p(q_(j-1))-beta)
       =max{beta/(C_beta-beta),
                  (y_j+(1-alpha_j)beta)/(u_j-(1-alpha_j)beta)},
    r_(j-1)=r_j+alpha_j*v(q_(j-1)).                              (3.3)

For alpha_j=0 keep the state and its projection. The denominator is
positive because u_j>=beta and alpha_j>0; the left ratio increases
from beta/(C_beta-beta) to infinity. We have u_j>=beta,y_j>=1 and
q_(j-1)<=q_j: at an interior current point the ratio is
(y_j+beta)/(u_j-beta), and subtracting alpha_j beta from its numerator
while adding it to its denominator can only decrease it. At q_j=1
the upper ratio is infinite. At q_j=0 the normal-cone condition
y_j<=beta(u_j-C_beta)/(C_beta-beta) keeps the next point capped.

For q_(j-1)>0, equation(3.3) places r_(j-1)-v(q_(j-1)) on its
outward circular normal; y_(j-1)>=1>=q_(j-1) fixes the sign. For
q_(j-1)=0 it gives y_(j-1)<=beta(u_(j-1)-C_beta)/(C_beta-beta),
the endpoint cone generated by the circular normal and(0,1).
The criterion <r-g,w-g><=0 for every w in K proves

    Pi_K(r_j)=v(q_j),
    u_0=beta+sum alpha_j*p(q_(j-1)),
    y_0=1+sum alpha_j*q_(j-1), u_0>=beta*y_0.                    (3.4)

This is a symbolic finite definition, not an executed recurrence.
For incoming ell>=0,D>0 put

    M=u_0+y_0/beta, sigma=D/M, ell_out=sigma*y_0,
    A=ell+ell_out/beta,
    Phi(X,Y)=E_(sigma K)(X-A,Y-ell_out).                          (3.5)

The local states(A+sigma*u_j,ell_out-sigma*y_j) start at(ell+D,0)
and follow exactly -(a_j/2)grad Phi. Inputs exceed ell and outputs
are in[0,ell_out). The final gradient is sigma(beta,-1). A selected
b>2 has T=b/2-1,D_out=sigma*T and exact post-checkpoint state

    (X^+,Y^+)=(A-beta*D_out,ell_out+D_out).                      (3.6)

The component is zero at this instant by(3.2); later motion may
reactivate it. It is zero whenever X<=ell,Y>=0. Its output derivative
vanishes for EVERY X when Y>=ell_out: replacing(p,-q) by(p,0) in
its maximization increases the objective by q(Y-ell_out)+q^2/2
for q>0. This exact isolation pays the active-coordinate induction.

## 4. Static function, all global updates, smoothness and exact radius

Apply Section3 to every block in(1.1), obtaining u_i,y_i,M_i. Set
ell_1=0,D_1=1 and

    T_i=b_(J+i-1)/2-1, sigma_i=D_i/M_i,
    ell_(i+1)=sigma_i*y_i, A_i=ell_i+ell_(i+1)/beta,
    D_(i+1)=sigma_i*T_i,
    Phi_i(X,Y)=E_(sigma_i K)(X-A_i,Y-ell_(i+1)).

Fix, before GD, the function on R^(k+1)

    delta=D_(k+1)/(1+S_(m-1)),
    F(x)=1/2 sum_(i=1)^k Phi_i(x_i,x_(i+1))
                 +1/2 Hub_delta(x_(k+1)-ell_(k+1)), x_0=e_1.     (4.1)

The map stacking all coordinate pairs and the last scalar counts every
coordinate at most twice; its squared operator norm is at most2. Each
unscaled summand is convex1-smooth on its own coordinates. Thus the
factor1/2 makes F globally convex1-smooth. F>=0 and F(0)=0.

At ANY state define

    (P_i,-Q_i)=Pi_(sigma_i K)(x_i-A_i,x_(i+1)-ell_(i+1)),
    P_(k+1)=clip(x_(k+1)-ell_(k+1),0,delta), Q_0=0.

The full physical update, retaining all earlier components, is

    x_j^+=x_j-(h/2)(P_j-Q_(j-1)), 1<=j<=k+1.                   (4.2)

At the start of block i its input is ell_i+D_i and later coordinates
are zero. The local states have x_i>=ell_i, so Q_(i-1)=0 by isolation.
The next component has input at most ell_(i+1) and output0 and hence
vanishes, as do later components. Components before i-1 involve neither
active coordinate. Thus(4.2) realizes EVERY ordinary step in Section3
and its checkpoint(3.6). Earlier components still follow(4.2) and may
have nonzero values and gradients. They are never reset to zero.

During the final suffix, if s' is the mass already read, the last excess
is exactly D_(k+1)-s'*delta/2>=delta: the preceding output derivative
Q_k is zero in this region and the scalar derivative is delta, proving
the assertion by induction. Consequently

    2F(x_n)=sum_(i=1)^k Phi_i(x_(n,i),x_(n,i+1))
                       +D_(k+1)^2/[2(1+S_(m-1))].             (4.3)

From(3.2) and nonnegativity the ENTIRE minimum set is

    C_min={x: x_i<=A_i, x_(i+1)>=beta(x_i-ell_i), 1<=i<=k;
                                                x_(k+1)<=ell_(k+1)}.

The point x_*=(A_1,ell_2,0,...,0) belongs to it. Equations(3.4) give
1-A_1=sigma_1*u_1, ell_2=beta*A_1=sigma_1*y_1, and
A_1<=1/(1+beta^2). A feasible point with x_1<0 has squared distance
at least1 from e_1. For 0<=x_1<=A_1 that square is at least
(1-x_1)^2+beta^2*x_1^2, decreasing on this interval and minimized
at A_1. The displayed x_* attains it. Thus the TRUE closest radius is

    R_m^2=(1-A_1)^2+ell_2^2=(u_1^2+y_1^2)/M_1^2.               (4.4)

All original directed interpolation rows hold for this static function:
the1-smooth descent bound for V(z)=F(z)-<grad F(y),z>, minimized at y,
gives F(x)-F(y)-<grad F(y),x-y>>=||grad F(x)-grad F(y)||^2/2.
Both orientations, minimizers and terminal points are included. Actual
Euclidean Gram matrices are positive semidefinite by definition.

## 5. Uniform local estimates when beta=1/k

Let w_j=(y_j+beta)/(q_j+beta). At an uncapped point the circular normal
says u_j-beta=(p(q_j)-beta)w_j. A cross product in the backward step
gives

    w_(j-1)=w_j[1+
       (p(q_(j-1))*q_j-q_(j-1)*p(q_j))
                     /(beta*(p(q_(j-1))+q_(j-1)))].             (5.1)

When q_(j-1)=0 this identity follows from y_(j-1)=y_j; if both q are
zero it is equality. Its numerator is the integral from q_(j-1) to q_j
of p(q_(j-1))-q_(j-1)*p'(t). Dividing by p(q_(j-1))+q_(j-1)
bounds the integrand by max(1,-p'(t)). Since w_N=1, multiplication
and 1+z<=exp(z) give

    w_0<=exp(I_beta/beta),
    I_beta=integral_0^1 max(1,-p'(t))dt
             =sqrt(2)(1+beta)-beta<2.

The integral is evaluated by splitting where -p'(t)=1, namely
t=(1+beta)/sqrt(2)-beta. Therefore

    y_0<=2exp(2k)=:Y_k.                                         (5.2)

At any uncapped point u_j<=beta+2k(Y_k+beta). Uncapped backward
steps form a contiguous portion, whose total u increment is at most
2k(Y_k+beta); their p is at least beta. Capped steps have p=C_beta.
It follows from(3.4) that

    u_0=beta+C_beta*s/2-E_gap,
    0<=E_gap<=4k^2(Y_k+beta),
    |M-C_beta*s/2|<=20k^2 exp(2k)=:K_k.                         (5.3)

The last bound pays beta,y_0/beta,E_gap; the loose constant20 works
for k>=4. A useful exact lower bound comes from the concave function
p(q)+q/beta. It is at least the smaller endpoint value C_beta, since
beta+1/beta>=C_beta for beta<=1/4. Thus

    M>=beta+1/beta+C_beta*s/2>=C_beta*(1+s/2).                   (5.4)

The mass recurrence S_(j+1)=2S_j+b_j has the exact solution

    d_m=c-2, v_mass=a_word/d_m in(0,1),
    1+S_j=v_mass*c^j+(1-v_mass)*2^j,
    b_j=d_m*S_j+c-1-d_m*(1-v_mass)*2^j.                        (5.5)

The inequality v_mass<1 follows from c(c-2)>1>eta_word. We have
v_mass->1 and c->lambda. Uniformly for J<=j<=m-1, S_j/(v_mass*c^j)
has error O(c^(-J)+(2/c)^J) from1. Furthermore

    k*K_k/S_J->0,
    S_J/lambda^J->exp(1/4),
    S_(m-1)/lambda^(m-1)->exp(1/4).                             (5.6)

The numerator in the first limit has logarithm O(k+log k), while the
denominator has logarithm J log(lambda)+O(1), and k/m->0. The other
limits follow from 0<=x-log(1+x)<=x^2/2 for x>=0 and j/m->1.
These are symbolic comparisons; no recurrence or numeric evaluation
was executed.

## 6. Amplitudes, cap ratios, and completion of the scale screen

The first transfer is T_1/M_1; later transfers use the one-level-shifted
gap in(1.1). Equations(5.3)--(5.6) give

    D_(k+1)=[d_m/C_beta]*[c*d_m/C_beta]^(k-1)*(1+o(1)).         (6.1)

The errors are summable: each normalization relative error is
O(K_k/S_J) and each mass or subtraction-of1 error is
O(c^(-J)+(2/c)^J); k times their sum tends to zero. Taking logarithms
proves the product assertion. Since c*d_m=1+O(1/m), its(k-1)st power
tends to1. Also log(beta+sqrt(1+2beta))/beta->2, so C_beta^k->exp(2).
Consequently

    D_(k+1)->(lambda-2)exp(-2),
    R_m^2->1, sigma_1~2/(C_beta*S_J).                           (6.2)

The radius conclusion uses u_1~C_beta*S_J/2,y_1<=Y_k and
M_1~C_beta*S_J/2. The next ordinary block after selected checkpoint i
is exactly H_(J+i-1), so uniformly for i<k,

    sigma_(i+1)/sigma_i=T_i/M_(i+1)->lambda-2.                  (6.3)

Eventually all these ratios are between1/3 and1/2. Hence

    sum_i sigma_i^2<=2sigma_1^2,
    B_(n_m)*sigma_1^2/R_m^2->0,                                (6.4)

the second quantity being O(lambda^(-m+2k)). Combining(6.2), the suffix
mass limit, and(1.2) proves(1.4). At the comparison point in Section2,
x_1<=A_1 gives the exact edge expression
Phi_1=Hub_((1+beta^2)sigma_1)(beta*x_1-x_2)/(1+beta^2), derived
also in Section7. At(0,-1) this equals the value in Section2 for large
m, while later inputs lie below their thresholds with nonnegative
outputs. This proves(2.2).

For reference T_infty<1. The j=0,b=1 enclosure of accepted
fact04219554e41d9750 gives Z_1<5, since B_1=G(1,1)=4 and
(2+3/100)^2<5. As exp(17/4)>16 and 2lambda>4, T_infty<5/64.
Its deficit from1 must be supplied by retained values for a benchmark
witness from this policy. The scale screen does not supply them.

## 7. The entire actual retired system and its two constraint residuals

Index the final suffix by t=0,...,N, N=2^(m-1)-1, with steps h_t
equal to the ordered H_(m-1). Write x(t) for its first k coordinates.
The incoming vector x(0) is the ACTUAL vector obtained from every
earlier update(4.2). After subsequent checkpoints, retired coordinates
may have changed in both directions. We do NOT set all of them to
their individual post-checkpoint values A_i-beta D_(i+1) at the root.

Throughout the suffix Q_k=0, and the fixed retired function is

    G_ret(x)=1/2 sum_(i<k) E_(sigma_i K)
                       (x_i-A_i,x_(i+1)-ell_(i+1))
                         +1/2 Hub_(C_beta*sigma_k)(x_k-A_k).    (7.1)

The last circular component is horizontal because its output is above
ell_(k+1). Its value is retained exactly, not dropped. The full retired
update is

    x_i(t+1)=x_i(t)-(h_t/2)[P_i(t)-Q_(i-1)(t)], 1<=i<=k,        (7.2)

where P_k is the derivative of the Huber in(7.1), and V_m=2G_ret(x(N)).
For i<k define

    r_i=x_i-A_i, z_i=beta(x_i-ell_i)-x_(i+1),
    U_i=P_i-beta Q_i>=0.

Also r_k=x_k-A_k,U_k=P_k,Q_0=Q_k=0. U_i is a horizontal cone
coefficient, distinct from the original benchmark U_n^ZJ. Since
beta A_i=beta ell_i+ell_(i+1), the local envelope is exactly

    Phi_i=max {U*r_i+Q*z_i-[(U+beta Q)^2+Q^2]/2:
            0<=Q<=sigma_i,
            0<=U<=sigma_i*p(Q/sigma_i)-beta Q}.                (7.3)

Indeed, the horizontal sections of K run from p=beta q to p=p(q).
The lower segment joins0 to(beta,-1), the upper boundary is concave,
and their convex hull contains every intervening point. Concavity also
bounds the convex hull above. Thus(7.3) is an identity.

When r_i<=0, maximizing first over U gives U_i=0, followed by

    Q_i=clip(z_i/(1+beta^2),0,sigma_i),
    Phi_i=Hub_((1+beta^2)sigma_i)(z_i)/(1+beta^2).               (7.4)

For general r_i, U_i can be positive. The exact residual updates are

    r_i^+=r_i-(h/2)(U_i+beta Q_i-Q_(i-1)),
    z_i^+=z_i-(h/2)[(1+beta^2)Q_i-beta Q_(i-1)-beta Q_(i+1)
                                               +beta U_i-U_(i+1)]. (7.5)

These include both neighboring contributions. Summing independent
two-Huber tail laws would delete genuine terms of(7.5).

## 8. Whole-hierarchy comparison with all input excursions charged

Put kappa=1+beta^2,theta=(1-beta)/(1+beta), and for i<k define

    qhat_i=clip(z_i/kappa,0,sigma_i), qhat_0=qhat_k=0,
    barsigma_i=sigma_i for 1<=i<k, barsigma_0=barsigma_k=0,
    e_i=beta*(barsigma_(i-1)+barsigma_(i+1)),
    gamma_i=kappa*sigma_i-e_i.

Here e_i,gamma_i are fixed cap combinations. From(6.3) and beta->0,
eventually gamma_i>0 and, uniformly for i<k,

    d_m*e_i<=gamma_i.                                         (8.1)

Every exact maximizer in(7.3) satisfies

    Q_i<=qhat_i,
    kappa(qhat_i-Q_i)-beta U_i<=theta U_i.                     (8.2)

For the complete curved-regime check, the function
A(q)=sigma_i*p(q/sigma_i)-beta q decreases continuously from
C_beta*sigma_i to0. At fixed U its feasible Q interval is[0,qmax(U)],
where A(qmax(U))=U and qmax(0)=sigma_i. The maximizer therefore has
Q=clip((z-beta U)/kappa,0,qmax(U)), proving Q<=qhat. If the upper
cap is inactive, kappa(qhat-Q)<=beta U. If active, Q=qmax(U).
Concavity and endpoint values imply p(q)+q>=1+beta, hence
U=A(Q)>=(1+beta)(sigma_i-Q). It follows that
kappa(qhat-Q)-beta U<=kappa(sigma_i-Q)-beta U<=theta U.
Lower and upper cap boundaries are included.

Insert(8.2) into(7.5). All neighbor differences that are dropped are
NONPOSITIVE, since Q_j<=qhat_j. The resulting unconditional step bound is

    z_i^+<=z_i-(h/2)[kappa qhat_i-beta qhat_(i-1)-beta qhat_(i+1)]
                                  +(h/2)[theta U_i+U_(i+1)].  (8.3)

Define the nonnegative charge from the ACTUAL suffix trajectory

    L_i=(1/2)sum_(t=0)^(N-1) h_t[theta U_i(t)+U_(i+1)(t)].     (8.4)

For any starting vector, the final residual obeys

    z_i(N)<=max{kappa*sigma_i+(c-1)e_i/2,
                              z_i(0)-gamma_i*S_(m-1)/2}+L_i.  (8.5)

If z_i(N)<=kappa*sigma_i this is immediate. Otherwise take its LAST
entry into z_i>kappa*sigma_i. If it was above the threshold at all
samples, qhat_i=sigma_i throughout, and summing(8.3) with the neighbor
caps gives the second term plus L_i. Otherwise the last entry uses an
actual step b_j,0<=j<m-1, whose preceding z_i is at most kappa*sigma_i.
Dropping only its nonpositive own term bounds the new residual by
kappa*sigma_i+b_j e_i/2 plus that step's charge. Immediately after
this occurrence is a complete H_j within the remaining suffix: this
right-subtree property follows inductively from the word definition.
At all samples after the last entry, qhat_i=sigma_i. The following
H_j therefore loses at least gamma_i*S_j/2 apart from its charges;
all further steps lose a nonnegative amount apart from their charges.
The uncharged bound is kappa*sigma_i+(b_j e_i-gamma_i S_j)/2.
Using(5.5), b_j<=d_m S_j+c-1, and(8.1) proves the first term.
Adding at most L_i pays EVERY source, including the crossing and all
large steps. This is an argument on the ordered hierarchy, not a
mass-only replacement of its nonlinear dynamics.

For this policy's ACTUAL incoming vector the second term in(8.5) is
nonpositive for all sufficiently large m. Let S_pre=S_(m-1)+b_(m-1),
the complete mass before the suffix. Global updates(4.2) and caps give

    delta_(i1)-C_beta*sigma_i*S_pre/2<=x_i(0)
                    <=delta_(i1)+sigma_(i-1)*S_pre/2,

where sigma_0=0 and delta_(i1) is1 for i=1 and0 otherwise. Therefore

    z_i(0)<=beta*delta_(i1)
                   +(beta*sigma_(i-1)+C_beta*sigma_(i+1))*S_pre/2. (8.6)

Only the nonpositive -beta ell_i was dropped. Uniformly in i, the
coefficient divided by sigma_i tends to lambda-2,
S_pre/S_(m-1)->lambda-1, and gamma_i/sigma_i->1. Their product
tends to (lambda-2)(lambda-1)=2-sqrt(2)<1. For i=1 the extra ratio
beta/(sigma_1*S_(m-1)) tends to0 by(6.2) and J=m-k. This proves
the incoming budget. As kappa+(c-1)e_i/(2sigma_i)->1 uniformly,
(8.5) yields eventually

    (z_i(N))_+<=2sigma_i+L_i.                                 (8.7)

There is no assumption that earlier components were zero at the root.
The exact maximization formula also gives

    Phi_i<=C_beta*sigma_i*(r_i)_++sigma_i*(z_i)_+, i<k,
    Phi_k<=C_beta*sigma_k*(r_k)_+.

Define one remaining nonnegative quantity on the ACTUAL trajectory:

    X_m=C_beta*sum_(i=1)^k sigma_i*(r_i(N))_+
           +(1/2)sum_(t=0)^(N-1) h_t
                         sum_(i=1)^(k-1) sigma_i
                                      [theta U_i(t)+U_(i+1)(t)]. (8.8)

Summing(8.7) and using(6.4) proves the unconditional comparison

    0<=V_m<=4sigma_1^2+X_m,
    B_(n_m)E_m<=T_m+o(1)+B_(n_m)X_m/R_m^2.                    (8.9)

Every old value is included. X_m has NOT been proved small. It is a
sufficient interface, not an equivalent formula for V_m: accumulated
nonnegative charges can overcount activity dissipated later. Proving
B_(n_m)X_m/R_m^2->0 would imply the complete scoped obstruction
B_(n_m)E_m->T_infty<1. No such estimate is asserted here.

## 9. The conditional slab result and a counterexample to blanket invariance

If every suffix sample satisfied x_i(t)<=A_i for all i<=k, then
U_i(t)=0 by(7.3)--(7.4), and every terminal(r_i)_+=0. Thus X_m=0
and(8.9) proves a full asymptotic obstruction. This is CONDITIONAL,
not a property proved for the actual trajectory. It already concerns
the coupled edge system, with no independence assumption.

The entire slab {x_i<=A_i for 1<=i<=k} is NOT invariant for this
same static function and the available large steps. For all large m,
consider this suffix state in the original R^(k+1):

    x_1=A_1, x_2=ell_2-kappa*sigma_1, x_3=ell_3,
    x_4=...=x_k=0, x_(k+1)=ell_(k+1)+D_(k+1).

All retired inputs are at most their A_i. The first component has
z_1=kappa*sigma_1 and(P_1,Q_1)=(beta*sigma_1,sigma_1).
The second has input<A_2 and output ell_3, so P_2=Q_2=0. Later
circular components vanish: the third input is its threshold ell_3
and its output0; subsequent inputs are0 with nonnegative outputs.
The terminal scalar does not affect coordinate2. Therefore

    x_2^+=ell_2-kappa*sigma_1+h*sigma_1/2>A_2
       whenever h>2kappa+2ell_3/(beta*sigma_1).                 (9.1)

The unchanged suffix contains h=b_(m-2). Meanwhile
ell_3/(beta*sigma_1)=T_1*y_2/(beta*M_2)=O(k exp(2k)) by
(5.2),(5.4),(5.5), whereas b_(m-2) grows like lambda^(m-2).
Thus(9.1) holds eventually. This uses the SAME function and a physical
step in the suffix; it is not a construction repair.

The falsifying state is NOT asserted to be the actual state at that
step. It refutes blanket slab invariance only. A trajectory-specific
barrier or a bound on the excursion flux could still hold. The example
identifies why a two-checkpoint absorption argument cannot be applied
independently to every pair.

## 10. Exact energy accounting and the remaining interfaces

An exact identity also keeps the signed effects that the sufficient
positive-source bound(8.8) may overcount. Let z_*=(A_1,ell_2,0,...,0)
in R^k, which minimizes G_ret, and define at each suffix sample

    d_i=P_i-Q_(i-1),
    N_grad=sum_i P_i^2+sum_(i<k)Q_i^2,
    C_cross=sum_(i=2)^k P_i Q_(i-1),
    slack=sum_(i<k)[-<(P_i,-Q_i),
                        (z_*(i)-A_i,z_*(i+1)-ell_(i+1))>]
                                     -P_k*(z_*(k)-A_k)>=0.

The slack sign follows from the polar-cone condition at a minimizer;
it is not an assumed sign for individual coordinates. The maximization
identity gives <grad G_ret(x),x-z_*>=G_ret(x)+N_grad/4+slack/2.
Since sum_i d_i^2=N_grad-2C_cross, expansion of(7.2) gives for EVERY
physical step, with no restriction on its size,

    ||x-z_*||^2-||x^+-z_*||^2
       =2h G_ret(x)+h(2-h)N_grad/4
                         +h^2 C_cross/2+h*slack.              (10.1)

C_cross and slack are nonnegative. The N_grad coefficient is negative
when h>2 and MUST be paid. Dropping it would not be a descent proof.

For the exact terminal value, write
D_E(v,u)=E(v)-E(u)-<grad E(u),v-u>>=0 for the Bregman remainder of
a differentiable convex E. For i<k take the fixed envelope E_(sigma_i K)
at its two centered coordinates, and for i=k take Hub_(C_beta*sigma_k)
at r_k. Let B_i(t) denote the remainder from sample t to t+1. Direct
first-order expansion of(7.1) gives the equality

    V_m=2G_ret(x(0))
              -(1/2)sum_t h_t sum_i d_i(t)^2
                                      +sum_t sum_i B_i(t).    (10.2)

All overlaps enter through d_i, and all overshoots remain in the actual
Bregman terms. Neither sum has been compared sharply enough here.

A sufficient retained contribution for a diagnostic lower witness at
the original benchmark is, for example,

    liminf_m B_(n_m)*V_m/R_m^2>=1-T_infty.                     (10.3)

Its exact V_m is specified by the actual static trajectory or by(10.2).
A strict version would give a strict margin for THIS diagnostic family.
A strict upper bound for the same term below1-T_infty would instead
obstruct this policy. The explicit sufficient unpaid obstruction
inequality supplied by the new comparison is

    B_(n_m)*X_m/R_m^2 ->0.                                   (10.4)

Attempt one: obtain(10.4) from slab invariance. Section9 refutes general
invariance; actual-trajectory invariance remains unproved. Attempt two:
use distance descent. Equation(10.1) exposes the unpaid h(2-h) term.
Its favorable coupled cross term is exact, but no comparison paying
all large-step negative terms from it has been established.

No snapshot, independent pair sum, or terminal-only product is offered
as the result. Equations(1.3),(8.8)--(8.9),(10.2)--(10.4) state exactly
what is paid and what is not. This round does NOT complete a retained
terminal bound for the chosen policy.

Even a successful(10.3) only treats H_(m,1/4) at horizons2^m-1.
The original all-word/all-horizon minimax question would still require
a lower bound for W_n(h) uniform over EVERY nonnegative word h of
length n, at the original B_n scale, or another valid route to the
original target. No such extension follows from this one policy.

## 11. Retrieval, resources, and handoff

Query-memory recalled the shifted-circle construction, complete
two-checkpoint draft, scoped scalar/tree failures, and current guidance.
Fact searches found no accepted growing circular-prefix result.
Fact5fd396eba75651e7 concerns different spiral families and is not a
premise. Only the original-ray conclusion of04219554e41d9750 is used;
its statement and relevant proof were read.

Matlas retrieval for overlapping projection envelopes and long-step
retained Huber transport returned three non-applicable results:
2011.14269 Lemma3.6 is a continuous-flow perturbation bound;
2212.04459 Lemma3 is a proximal descent inequality;2012.15458 Lemma2.1
concerns a different Moreau composition. None was imported.

The exact primary source was downloaded and extracted:
Kaizhao Liu and Yuhan Ye, Silver Rate Is (Almost) Optimal for Gradient
Descent: The Strongly Convex Case, arXiv:2609.26873v1,
https://arxiv.org/html/2609.26873v1. Read the preceding-component
updates, FactA.1, and the circular construction and checkpoint proof.
Its finite-condition-number contraction and relative-function
normalization do not apply here. The circular envelope was reconstructed
directly without a quadratic term, with overlap smoothness and closest
radius derived anew. No external theorem is used as a black box.

Skills: query-memory, obtain-immediate-conclusions, search-math-results,
direct-proving, construct-counterexamples. TASK and current guidance
explicitly allocate no formal gate, so fact_submit was not called.
All new assertions remain one joint unverified proof attempt.

Mathematical programs0; numerical evaluations0; arithmetic replays0;
executed recurrences0; solvers0; benchmark DP0; formal submissions0;
new accepted facts0; installations0; consults0; subagents0; repairs0.
Only administrative file I/O, text recall, source retrieval, HTML text
extraction, and integrity checks were performed. No old grant was reopened.

An early output helper failed with `Unable to store "recall0". Only
plain serializable objects can be stored.` An absent structured result
was guarded before the read-only requests were repeated. Broad recall
output was truncated; necessary sections were reread in bounded chunks.
The first report patch failed with `invalid hunk at line 240` because
one added line lacked its patch prefix. It created no report; the report
was then written in smaller valid patches and read back.

The original target and this policy's retained-terminal comparison are
STILL OPEN. The one assigned round closes with a precise unpaid input
flux, not a complete obstruction or witness. No automatic renewal,
publication, finalization, revocation, shutdown, or Goalcompletion was
performed. Expected completed count on return60; the official post-return
counter has not been observed. No work is in flight.

# One shared array for all chronological three-block tests

Status: self-contained candidate, **not verifier accepted**. This continuation
uses no optimization, new launch, installation, or fact_submit. The accepted
dependency is `1252b8823bb5a290`, for smooth-convex interpolation and the
original finite full-objective formulation. Every new statement below is
proved within this document. In particular an earlier unverified cut identity
or recursive identity is not a correctness premise.

The requested decision at q=1/M_m is NOT obtained: no feasible common array,
and no infeasibility proof for the entire collection, is asserted. What is
obtained is an exact attained legal-primal interpretation of the collection,
its common two-jump matrix, a quantitative optimum-source budget, and a
quantitative rejection of one explicitly constructed scalable common array
family by two cuts already in the collection.

## 1. Exact collection and notation

Let n>=2, h=(h_0,...,h_(n-1)) have nonnegative finite entries, and put
S=sum h_i, M=1+S, P_i=sum_(t<i)h_t, including P_n=S. Normalize L=1 and
x_star=g_star=f_star=0. The original independent formal vectors are
(x_0,g_0,...,g_n); x_i=x_0-sum_(t<i)h_t g_t. For distinct sample indices
in I={star,0,...,n}, write

 Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2.

Choose ONE finite nonnegative array lambda_ij with finite-node flow

 sum_j lambda_ij-sum_j lambda_ji=2*1_(i=n).                 (1)

Its value-free quadratic polynomial is

 C_lambda=q||x_0||^2-2f_n+sum_(i!=j)lambda_ij Q_ij.        (2)

For every 1<=k<l<=n, require (2) to be nonnegative whenever

 g_i=u for i<k, g_i=v for k<=i<l, g_i=w for i>=l,         (3)

with x_0,u,v,w independent arbitrary formal vectors. The same array is used
for every (k,l). This is the collection A_2. One-block and two-block tests
are included by equality of block values. All the earlier queried-j/tail
tests are included: for j>0 use k=j,l=j+1,u=0; for j=0 use a two-block
specialization. No position moment, optimum edge, or initial coordinate is
discarded in (2).

## 2. One common matrix, with all singular cases

Introduce chronological jumps d_k=g_k-g_(k-1), 1<=k<=n, and b=g_0. Then
g_i=b+sum_(k<=i)d_k. This is an invertible linear coordinate change. Write
the matrix of (2) in coordinates (x_0,b,d_1,...,d_n) as

              [ C       R ]
 A_lambda =   [ R^T     T ],                              (4)

where C is 2 by 2, r_k is column k of R, and T is n by n. A restriction
(3) is EXACTLY the principal block of (4) on x_0,b,d_k,d_l. Thus the entire
collection is equivalent to

 C>=0;
 r_k in Range(C) for every k;
 Z_kk>=0 and Z_kl^2<=Z_kk Z_ll for every k<l,              (5)
 Z_kl=T_kl-r_k^T C^+ r_l.

Here C^+ inverts positive eigenvalues and vanishes on Ker(C). To prove the
singular statement, diagonalize C. A nonzero component of any r_k in its
kernel makes the corresponding quadratic unbounded below in a free base
variable. Once every such component vanishes, complete the squares in the
positive base coordinates. The remaining two-variable quadratic has matrix
[[Z_kk,Z_kl],[Z_kl,Z_ll]], whose PSD criterion is exactly (5). This proves
both implications, including zero diagonal entries and C=0.

There are n(n-1)/2 coupled pair tests of ONE residual matrix Z. This is not
independent per-partition feasibility. It is also not full PSD of Z.

Here is an explicit matrix-only distinction. In any three jump coordinates
take diagonal entries 1 and all off-diagonal entries -3/4, with every other
jump diagonal 1 and other off-diagonals zero; take C=I_2 and R=0. Every
two-jump principal matrix is positive definite, but the vector with these
three jumps equal to 1 has quadratic value 3-9/2=-3/2. This exhibits the
three-jump correlation absent from A_2. It does NOT assert that this matrix
is realized by a nonnegative interpolation array for a particular h.

## 3. Quantitative budget for actual optimum-source edges

Set mu_i=lambda_(i,star), nu_i=lambda_(star,i), and nu=sum_i nu_i.
Summing (1) gives sum_i mu_i=2+nu. On the constant-gradient subspace
g_i=b, direct expansion gives

 C = [ q                -(2+nu)/2 ]
     [ -(2+nu)/2        1+2S+sum_i(1+P_i)nu_i ].          (6)

For the lower-right coefficient, finite edges contribute
sum_i P_i(out_i^finite-in_i^finite)
=2S-sum_i P_i mu_i+sum_i P_i nu_i. The finite-to-star position terms
add back sum_i P_i mu_i; the optimum-edge norm terms add 1+nu. The x_0
coupling is -(sum_i mu_i)<x_0,b>. These are exactly (6).

At the proposed q=1/M, positivity of C is equivalent to

 sum_i (S-P_i)nu_i + (M/4)nu^2 <= S.                     (7)

Indeed its determinant is
1-1/M-sum_i(S-P_i)nu_i/M-nu^2/4, and its first diagonal is positive.
In particular nu<=2sqrt(S/M), with a stricter penalty on early
optimum-source mass. This uses the actual optimum edges, not a choice to
delete them. The budget alone does not prove infeasibility, since nu=0
remains allowed.

## 4. The legal primal cone represented by the collection

Let V_(k,l) be the subspace of real coordinate lists
(X,G_0,...,G_n) in which G is constant on the three blocks (3), while X is
free. Define the cone

 K_2=sum_(1<=k<l<=n) { positive semidefinite matrices with range in V_(k,l) }.
                                                                    (8)

Equivalently, K_2 consists of finite sums vv^T where each scalar gradient
list in v has at most two chronological jumps. This follows by diagonalizing
each positive semidefinite summand. Conversely each such vv^T lies in one
of the summands, after inserting an unused cut if necessary.

Thus G belongs to K_2 if and only if it has a Gram realization in a finite
Euclidean space with an orthonormal coordinate system in which every
coordinate of (g_0,...,g_n) changes at most twice. Different coordinates may
use different cuts. The x_0 coordinate in each of these directions is free.
This is substantially more flexible than a whole vector gradient with only
two jumps: many cut patterns coexist in a single trajectory.

The cone is closed. If G_r converges and G_r=sum_a G_(r,a) is a decomposition
over the finitely many summands of (8), each summand is PSD and has trace at
most trace(G_r). In fixed finite dimension every PSD matrix has norm bounded
by its trace. Pass to a common subsequence on which all summands converge;
their ranges remain in their closed subspaces and their limits are PSD.
Their sum is the limit of G_r and belongs to K_2.

The cone is full dimensional in the space of symmetric (n+2)-matrices. In
jump coordinates every pair of coordinates occurs in one allowed block
{x_0,b,d_k,d_l}. Therefore the linear map from the direct sum of the
symmetric block spaces onto the ambient symmetric space is surjective.
It has a linear right inverse. Starting from identity matrices on all these
blocks, sufficiently small ambient perturbations can be lifted by this
right inverse while every block remains positive definite. The sum of the
embedded identities is consequently an interior point of K_2.

For n>=3 this is a PROPER subcone of the full PSD cone. Choose v with three
nonzero jumps. If vv^T=sum_a G_a with all G_a PSD, then every vector orthogonal
to v has zero quadratic value in each G_a, so Range(G_a) is contained in
span(v). Every nonzero summand is therefore a positive multiple of vv^T.
Such a summand cannot be supported on an allowed two-jump subspace. Hence
vv^T is not in K_2. This is a cone statement, not a claim that the particular
v is a legal GD transcript.

Finally, by (8), a matrix has nonnegative pairing with every G in K_2 if and
only if every restriction (3) is PSD. Thus (5) is exactly the dual-cone
condition K_2^*, with the original array flows imposed separately.

## 5. Exact attained equality on the actual positive-step family

Assume now h_i>1 for every i<n, as holds for every critical H_m below. Let
w_2(h) be the maximum of 2f_n over G in K_2, G_(x0,x0)<=1, and every original
ordered constraint Q_ij<=0 including the optimum. Then

 w_2(h) = min { q : some common lambda>=0 satisfies (1) and A_2 at q }.
                                                                    (9)

Both extrema are attained. The left side is an actual legal smooth-convex
GD performance over the coordinate class described above, not an aggregate
of independently chosen functions. In particular w_2(h)<=W_n(h).

Here is a proof including strict feasibility rather than an assumed generic
strong-duality theorem.

First construct a strict interpolation point within K_2. Put

 kappa=min(1,min_i(h_i-1))/4 >0,
 r=kappa/[2(kappa+1)], a_i=r^i,
 g_i=sqrt(a_i)e_i, f_i=(kappa+1/2)a_i,
 x_0=(kappa+2)sum_(i=0)^n sqrt(a_i)e_i+e_extra,             (10)

where the displayed coordinate vectors are orthonormal. For i<j, write
delta_i=f_i-a_i/2=kappa a_i. Direct substitution of orthogonality into the
two finite interpolation constraints shows they are strict exactly when

                   a_j < delta_i-delta_j < (h_i-1)a_i.   (11)

For the lower inequality use a_j/a_i<=r and
kappa(1-r)-r=kappa/2>0. For the upper use
delta_i-delta_j<kappa a_i<(h_i-1)a_i. The optimum residuals are
Q_(i,star)=-a_i and Q_(star,i)=-kappa a_i. Thus ALL ordered residuals are
strict. The extra coordinate makes the full Gram matrix positive definite.
Each gradient coordinate in (10) is supported on one query, hence has at
most two jumps; the extra coordinate has zero gradients. The Gram matrix
lies in K_2. Scale all vectors and the square roots of all values by one
small positive number to make the radius inequality strict as well.

This point may be on the boundary of K_2. Add a sufficiently small positive
multiple of the interior point constructed in Section 4 to its Gram matrix,
holding the values fixed. Finitely many strict linear inequalities remain
strict for small enough perturbations, including the radius. A point of a
convex cone plus an interior point lies in its interior: translate a small
ball in the cone by the cone point. This gives full conic strict feasibility.

The primal feasible set is compact. Every feasible Gram matrix is a genuine
finite set of Euclidean vectors. Adding Q_(i,star) and Q_(star,i) gives
||g_i||^2<=<g_i,x_i>, hence ||g_i||<=||x_i||. Inductively
||x_i||<=product_(t<i)(1+h_t), and all Gram entries are bounded by
Cauchy--Schwarz. Also ||g_i||^2/2<=f_i<=||x_i||^2/2. The cone is closed by
Section 4; all constraints are closed. Compactness proves primal attainment.
The interpolation assertion in accepted fact1252b8823bb5a290 supplies a legal
finite-dimensional smooth convex function for each feasible full transcript.
The maximum is positive: take x_0=1, every g_i=t=1/(2S+1), and
f_i=t-(P_i+1/2)t^2. Finite-pair and finite-to-star residuals vanish, while
Q_(star,i)=-t+(P_i+1)t^2<0. This constant-gradient Gram matrix is in K_2 and
2f_n=t>0. Homogeneous scaling consequently forces an optimal transcript to
have ||x_0||=1, so the maximum agrees with the normalized objective ratio.

For explicit separation, form the open convex set of tuples (s,t,c) for
which some G in K_2 and real f obey s_ij>Q_ij, t>G_(x0,x0)-1, c<2f_n.
The point (0,0,w_2) is outside. Separate it, obtaining nonnegative
coefficients (lambda,rho,eta) on (s,t,-c). If eta=0, substitution of the
strict feasible point makes the limiting separation inequality strictly
negative, contrary to separation. Hence eta>0; normalize eta=1. Because
the values f_i are unrestricted in the separation inequality, their
coefficients vanish and give exactly (1). Scaling G in K_2 implies that
the matrix of C_lambda with q=rho lies in K_2^*. Setting G=f=0 gives
rho<=w_2. The matrix condition and lambda>=0 give the reverse weak-duality
inequality w_2<=rho. Thus equality holds and the common array is attained.
This is the same elementary separation mechanism as the accepted full-cone
proof, applied here only after proving its needed hypotheses for K_2.

Consequently, on H_m, failure of the entire common-array relaxation at
q=1/M_m would have an actual legal witness in this coordinate class with
2f_n>1/M_m. This conclusion relies on (9), not on regarding any formal
negative vector of a particular certificate as a legal trajectory.

## 6. An explicit scalable common array that fails a retained two-cut test

This section screens one concrete construction; it does NOT disprove common
array feasibility in (9).

For a schedule a of sum A-1, define the intrinsic polynomial

 V_a=2sum_i a_i(f_i-f_end)+sum_i a_i(1-a_i)||g_i||^2
                                      -(A-1)A||g_end||^2.

Take two identical step lists of capacity A, separated by a physical bridge
1+r, and put t=r/A, T=(2+t)A. Let p be the bridge gradient, z the final
gradient, and H the weighted sum of gradients at the right-child steps.
Set W=H+z, and use right-child indices j in

 G=sum_j a_j Q_(j,p)+Q_(z,p),
 J=sum_j a_j Q_(j,z),
 k=(2+t)^2, u=2(2+t), v=2A,
 jcoef=2+6t+2t^2, delta=2-(1+t)^2.

The following identity holds for EVERY r>=0, including supercritical r:

 V_join=V_left+k V_right-u G-v Q_(p,z)-jcoef J
                                      +delta||W-Ap||^2.  (12)

For a direct coefficient derivation, put C_r=G+rQ_(p,z)+J. Expanding the
actual positions gives

 C_r,value=2sum a_j f_j+(r-A)f_p+(2-A-r)f_z,
 C_r,gradient=sum a_j(1-a_j)||g_j||^2
                  +[(A-r)/2-r^2]||p||^2+(A+r)||z||^2/2,
 V_right-2J=||W||^2-A^2||z||^2,
 Q_(p,z)=f_p-f_z-(r+1/2)||p||^2+||z||^2/2-<p,W>.

The cross terms in the second line cancel using
2sum_j a_j<g_j,sum_(i<j)a_i g_i>=||H||^2-sum_j a_j^2||g_j||^2.
These four displayed expansions verify (12) in all values and gradient
coefficients, by substituting the definitions of k,u,v,jcoef,delta. No
positivity of delta is used in this polynomial identity.

Start with the empty array at a leaf. Recursively retain the left array,
multiply the right array by k, and add uG+vQ_(p,z)+jcoef J. All actual
coefficients are nonnegative for r>=0. If R_left and R_right are the two
value-free intrinsic remainders, the joined remainder is exactly

 R_join=R_left+kR_right+delta||W-Ap||^2.                  (13)

Now fix m>=128 and, for this WHOLE horizon, set

 sigma=1+sqrt(2), c=sigma(1+1/m), a=(1-1/m)/c,
 H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a c^j,H_j),
 r_j=a c^j, M_j=1+sum H_j,
 t_j=r_j/M_j, delta_j=2-(1+t_j)^2.                       (14)

The parameters are not recomputed inside the recursion. Counting gives

 M_j=A_mass c^j+(1-A_mass)2^j,
 A_mass=a/(c-2),
 t_j=(c-2)/[1+B(2/c)^j],
 B=(1-A_mass)/A_mass
   =[(sigma^2+2)m+sigma^2]/[m(m-1)].                    (15)

For m>=128, 0<B<8/m, 2/c<5/6 and 0<c-2<1/2. The first estimate follows
from sigma^2=3+2sqrt(2)<35/6 and m>83; the others follow from
12/5<sigma<17/7 and m>=128. For j=m-2 or m-1, we have j>=12 and
(2/c)^j<(5/6)^12<1/8. Using 1/(1+x)>=1-x in (15) gives

 t_j>(c-2)-(c-2)/m
     =(sigma-2)+[sigma-(c-2)]/m
     >(sigma-2)+1/m.

Therefore delta_j<-2/m for these two levels.

Use the actual three-block cuts

                   k=2^(m-1), l=3*2^(m-2).             (16)

Write U=M_(m-1), V=M_(m-2), r=r_(m-1), s=r_(m-2).
Set all gradients on the three blocks to respectively

        u0=-(V+s)/(U+r) v0,       v0,       0,           (17)

where v0 is any nonzero scalar or vector. The full step displacement is
D=sum h_i g_i=(U+r)u0+(V+s)v0=0, and g_n=0. All node squares in (13)
vanish except those at the root and its right child: every smaller node
has constant gradients. Thus

 R_Hm=M_m^2 { delta_(m-1)(V+s)^2/(U+r)^2
                             +delta_(m-2)V^2/U^2 }||v0||^2
      < -8 M_m^2/(25m) ||v0||^2.                       (18)

For the last inequality use both negative deltas and U/V=2+t_(m-2)<5/2.

The obstruction survives the whole following objective conversion family.
For any 0<alpha<=1/S with S=M_m-1, multiply the intrinsic array by alpha,
add lambda_(i,star)=2alpha h_i for i<n, add
lambda_(n,star)=2beta with beta=1-alpha S>=0, and take ANY q. Every
coefficient is nonnegative and (1) holds. Direct expansion gives

 C_lambda=q||x_0||^2-2<x_0,alpha D+beta g_n>
          +alpha||D||^2+2beta<D,g_n>
          +(alpha S M_m+beta)||g_n||^2+alpha R_Hm.       (19)

To check (19), expand 2sum h_i Q_(i,star)-V_h as
||D||^2-2<x_0,D>+2S f_n+S M_m||g_n||^2, and then add 2beta Q_(n,star).
The values cancel because alpha S+beta=1. On (17), set x_0=0; then
(19)=alpha R_Hm<0 independently of q. In particular this array does NOT
pass A_2 at q=1/M_m. Its failure is quantitative and occurs in the already
requested collection, with actual moments and full original flows.

The vector (17) is not a legal adversary: it is a negative test direction
for this particular coefficient family. Only a proof excluding EVERY
nonnegative common array, together with (9), would produce the claimed
legal lower obstruction. No such exclusion has been established here.

## 7. Remaining decision and next interface

The task's central dichotomy remains open. Section 5 converts it exactly to
an attained legal performance problem on a specified proper Gram cone;
Section 6 rejects an explicit scalable candidate at every q. Neither gives
the common feasible-array formula or the collection-wide lower bound at
1/M_m that the assignment requests.

Adding all four-interval partitions would test every three-jump principal
block in the common matrix (4). It adds the determinant/correlation excluded
by the explicit example after (5), without yet reverting to arbitrary
singleton PEP. This names the new constraint precisely; it is not a claim
that those extra partitions are sufficient for the quantitative target.
The exact all-schedule F/G comparison and the original target are unchanged.

<!-- BEGIN_SOURCE_BODY DOC-001216 -->
# Actual H3 payment and the limit of a U-only join

JOINT UNVERIFIED. Worker max2, invocation80, actual ended baseline79.
Project gd-exact-profile-20260926. Guidance a45f2ea425c1505b.
ONE allocated invocation; completed ceiling80/attempted81; controller cap1.
No formal submission is authorized. Every new assertion below is part of
this one candidate derivation, not a fact in the accepted graph.

## Verdict and scope

The actual same-m H3 admits a candidate nonnegative identity proving

    U_d(T_H3 x) <= U_(d+2S3)(x)

for every nonempty compact convex D0 containing0, every finite dimension,
every start x, and every d>=1. Both actual H2 certificates are retained.
The new ingredient is the ACTUAL auxiliary projection at the input of the
right H2, with its actual trailing mass. A primitive-left/bridge identity
has a positive explicit coefficient, bounded below by3C(C+k)/46 in this
H3 application, where C=1+S2 and k=a*c^2. This pays the old negative
primitive term without assuming primitive positivity of H3.

A natural attempt to propagate the same join rule with only U laws for
both children is FALSE. Two children consisting of the single step3/2
both satisfy the universal U law for all d>=1. Their join with bridge9/4
passes the new scalar bridge condition, yet on the ONE fixed interval
D0=[-1,1] it violates U for every1<=d<175/154. At d=1 its ratio is575/512.
This is a legal counterexample to that concrete reusable U-only join,
not to the actual perturbed H4 or to every possible richer invariant.

The positive reusable law still requires a primitive left reserve. It
therefore closes H3 but does not propagate through arbitrary higher
symmetric depths, whose left children need not be primitive. No uniform
all-depth loss1+C/m or full original-function-class bound is obtained.
The original unrestricted minimax problem remains OPEN. No fact_id was
created. Exactly two substantive mechanisms were used.

## 1. Fixed parameters, bodies, and accepted scope

Fix ONE integer m>=1536, throughout all children. Define

    epsilon=1/(4m), beta=1-epsilon, lambda=1+sqrt(2),
    c=lambda(1+epsilon), a=beta/c,
    H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a*c^j,H_j),
    S_j=sum H_j, C_j=1+S_j.

The physical H2 is (A,B,A), where A=1+a and B=1+beta. Write

    s=S2=2A+B, C=1+s, k=a*c^2=beta*c,
    h=C+k, S=S3=s+(1+k)+s=2C+k-1.

Thus h is the MASS of the prefix H2 followed by its root bridge, not an
extra physical step. The seven physical steps are
(A,B,A,1+k,A,B,A). Neither m nor the leaf parameters reset.

For one fixed nonempty compact convex set D0 in a finite-dimensional real
Euclidean space, containing0, put

    P(v)=argmin_(g in D0)||v-g||^2,
    F(v)=max_(g in D0)(<v,g>-||g||^2/2),
    U_d(v)=max_(g in D0)(2<v,g>-d||g||^2), d>0.

A physical word uses x_(i+1)=x_i-h_i g_i and g_i=P(x_i).
All auxiliary projections in this report also use this SAME D0.
No approximation to the physical trajectory is used.

The accepted interpolation orientation is fact1252b8823bb5a290:

    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2 <=0.       (1.1)

Its necessity proof was reread. The complete applicable primitive and
dominant composition definitions in accepted04219554e41d9750, Sections
10-11, were reread. Its exact-root join is not invoked at an off-root
bridge. The identities needed here, including the old H2 draft, are
proved jointly below. Accepted931b9653ce0f7201 still requires a FULL-class
upper before its conditional negative conclusion applies; no such upper
is inferred here. This is the gradient-projection diagnostic, not the
residual-gradient affine-height representation. W=2R remains unchanged.

For clarity, the scalar s here is S2; the constant sqrt(2)-1 is denoted u.
The original problem is the asymptotic unrestricted comparison of W_n^star
with the original ALL-split Zhang--Jiang benchmark, not this finite H3 law.

## 2. Elementary projection facts and exact original rows

Projection optimality is

    <v-P(v),g-P(v)> <=0 for all g in D0.                 (2.1)

Necessity follows by minimizing squared distance on each segment from
P(v) to g. Sufficiency follows by expanding ||v-g||^2. The minimizer is
unique by strict convexity of squared distance. Adding the two conditions
for inputs v,w gives ||P(v)-P(w)||^2<=<v-w,P(v)-P(w)>; hence P is
1-Lipschitz. Completion of the square identifies P(v) as the unique
maximizer in F. The two maximizing inequalities bound the first-order
remainder of F by ||t||||P(v+t)-P(v)||, proving grad F=P. Thus F is
convex, differentiable and1-smooth.

Its minimum is0. The minimizers are exactly

    K0={z:<z,g><=0 for every g in D0}.

If z is in K0, all candidates in F(z) are nonpositive and g=0 attains0.
If some <z,g> is positive, sufficiently small positive multiples of g
belong to D0 and give a positive value. For every z_star in K0 and M>0,

    U_M(x)<=||x-z_star||^2/M,                           (2.2)

by completing the square in2<x-z_star,g>-M||g||^2 and using
<z_star,g><=0. No origin-radius replacement is made.

At physical or auxiliary samples of this same F, direct substitution of
f_i=<x_i,g_i>-||g_i||^2/2 into (1.1) gives

    Q_ij=<x_j-g_j,g_i-g_j>.                            (2.3)

Every row below is therefore an actual same-body projection normal.
Auxiliary samples introduce no physical gradient-descent step.

For a word b of mass t, input v, endpoint z, define its primitive energy

    V_b=2sum_(i<N)b_i(f_i-f_N)
        +sum_(i<N)b_i(1-b_i)||g_i||^2-t(t+1)||g_N||^2.

Telescoping ||x_i||^2-||x_(i+1)||^2 proves

    V_b=||v||^2-||z+tP(z)||^2.                         (2.4)

Indeed the value and nonterminal gradient terms sum to
||v||^2-||z||^2-2t f_N; substitution of f_N gives (2.4).

## 3. H2 and off-root identities retained jointly

For an actual step x_v=x_u-l g_u, the two ordered rows are

 Q_uv=f_u-f_v+(1/2-l)||g_u||^2-<g_u,g_v>+||g_v||^2/2,
 Q_vu=f_v-f_u+||g_u||^2/2+(l-1)<g_u,g_v>+||g_v||^2/2.

Coefficient collection gives

 V_(l)=-2Q_uv-2(1+l)Q_vu+(2-l^2)||g_u-g_v||^2,          (3.1)
 ||g_u||^2-||g_v||^2
   =(2/l-1)||g_u-g_v||^2-(2/l)(Q_uv+Q_vu), l>0.         (3.2)

These identities keep both formal gradients.

For general left and right words with capacities P_L and P_R, insert the
actual bridge1+t, 0<=t<P_R. Let p be its query and z the parent endpoint;
j ranges over the nonterminal right queries with steps b_j. Define

 K=(2P_L+P_R+t)/(P_R-t),
 Delta=P_L P_R-t(P_L+P_R+t),
 R=(P_L+t)Delta/(P_R-t),
 E=sum_j b_j Q_(j,p)+Q_(z,p)+t Q_(p,z)+sum_j b_j Q_(j,z).

On the actual history, without any primitive assumption, one has

    V_parent=V_left+K V_right-(K-1)E
                         +R(||g_p||^2-||g_z||^2).       (3.3)

Here is a coefficient check of the off-root term. Expansion of E gives
value coefficients

    2sum_j b_j f_j+(t-P_R)f_p+(2-P_R-t)f_z

and gradient terms

 sum_j b_j(1-b_j)||g_j||^2
 +[(P_R-t)/2-t^2]||g_p||^2+(P_R+t)||g_z||^2/2.

All mixed terms cancel: with G=sum b_j g_j, the two sums contribute
respectively t<G,g_p>+||G||^2/2 and-||G||^2/2-<G,g_z>, in addition to
half their displayed diagonals; Q_(z,p) contributes t<g_z,g_p>+<G,g_z>,
and tQ_(p,z) contributes-t<g_p,G>-t<g_p,g_z>. The remaining value
coefficients match because(K-1)(P_R-t)=2(P_L+t). The unmatched gradient
coefficients are P_L^2-Kt^2=R and K P_R^2-(P_L+P_R+t)^2=-R. Factoring
after multiplication by P_R-t verifies these two identities. This proves
(3.3) without an exact-root substitution or a changed trajectory.

For H2 only, put C1=1+A=2+a and

 K1=(3C1+beta)/(C1-beta),
 Delta1=C1^2-beta(2C1+beta), R1=(C1+beta)Delta1/(C1-beta),
 deltaA=2-A^2.

Let u=sqrt(2)-1. Then a=u(1-epsilon)/(1+epsilon), 2u+u^2=1, and

 u C1-beta=epsilon(1+epsilon-2u^2)/(1+epsilon)>0,
 Delta1=(u C1-beta)(lambda C1+beta)>0.                (3.4)

Also1<A<sqrt(2),1<B<2,C1>beta, and K1>1. Apply (3.3) to the two
leaves, then (3.2) to bridge B and the final leaf A. Together with (3.1)
this gives the ENTIRE H2 identity, on its four original gradients,

 V_H2=-sum_(i!=j)Lambda_ij Q_ij
       +e0||g0-g1||^2+e1||g1-g2||^2+e2||g2-g3||^2,   (3.5)

 e0=deltaA, e1=R1(2/B-1)=R1 epsilon/B,
 e2=K1 deltaA+R1(2/A-1),

 Lambda_01=2,
 Lambda_10=2(1+A),
 Lambda_12=2R1/B,
 Lambda_21=(K1-1)A+2R1/B,
 Lambda_13=(K1-1)beta,
 Lambda_31=K1-1,
 Lambda_23=2K1+(K1-1)A+2R1/A,
 Lambda_32=2K1(1+A)+2R1/A.                           (3.6)

All other multipliers are zero; none is negative. The value coefficients
are(2A,2B,2A,-2s): for the bridge they give
-2A+(K1-1)(C1-beta)=2B, and at the last nonterminal point they give
-2(K1-1)A+2K1 A=2A. The square remainder is a positive weighted path
Laplacian on all four gradients. No formal coordinates are identified.

There is also the conservative bound

    e_i>=epsilon^2/2, i=0,1,2.                       (3.7)

To check it, deltaA=[2u epsilon/(1+epsilon)](sqrt(2)+A)>epsilon/2;
u>1/4, epsilon<1, and sqrt(2)+A>2 suffice. Formula (3.4), u<1/2,
and C1>2 give uC1-beta>epsilon/4 and lambda C1+beta>4. Hence
Delta1>epsilon and R1>epsilon. Since B<2, (3.7) follows. These facts
are derived here, not assumed from the earlier unverified H2 report.

For the actual H3 root use C=1+s and k=a*c^2. Equation (3.3) reads

 K2=(3C+k)/(C-k), Delta2=C^2-k(2C+k),
 R2=(C+k)Delta2/(C-k),
 V_H3=V_L+K2 V_R-(K2-1)E2+R2(||g3||^2-||g7||^2).     (3.8)

E2 is the displayed E with p=3,z=7 and j=4,5,6. Its physical right
steps are A,B,A. Every row and the two H2 histories remain actual.

The sign is genuinely R2<0. Indeed
C=5-epsilon+2u(1-epsilon)/(1+epsilon), k=lambda(1-epsilon^2),
and lambda=5u+2u^2 give

 k-uC=epsilon[u+4u^2/(1+epsilon)-lambda epsilon]>0.

Here u>2/5, lambda<5/2 and epsilon<1/8 ensure the last bracket is
positive. Thus Delta2=(uC-k)(lambda C+k)<0. Primitive positivity is
not a premise of the H3 proof.

## 4. Every actual maximization remainder

For any actual word of mass t, input v, endpoint z and trailing d>=1,
write

 M=d+2t, q=P(z/d), p=P(v/M),
 bbar=[v-z+(d+t)q]/M,
 Rem(v,M,bbar)=M||p-bbar||^2+2<v-Mp,p-bbar>.           (4.1)

The point bbar belongs to D0: its physical gradient weights are the
actual nonnegative steps, and its q weight is d+t; their sum is M.
Each term in Rem is nonnegative by (2.1). Comparing the maximum at p
with the feasible point bbar, then expanding the squares, gives EXACTLY

 U_M(v)-U_d(z)
   =[||v||^2-||z+tq||^2]/M+Rem(v,M,bbar).             (4.2)

For instance v-M bbar=z-(d+t)q, which makes the expansion immediate:
the numerator after subtracting M U_d(z) is
||v||^2-||z||^2-2t<z,q>-t^2||q||^2.

Put g=P(z). Comparing the optimizers of U_d(z) at two different d values
gives, for1<=d1<d2 and q_i=P(z/d_i),

 d1(||q1||^2-||q2||^2)
 <=2<z,q1-q2><=d2(||q1||^2-||q2||^2).

It follows that the squared-norm and inner-product differences are
nonnegative. Consequently the exact radial term

 Lrad(t,z,d)=2t<z,g-q>+t^2(||g||^2-||q||^2)>=0         (4.3)

satisfies

 U_M(v)-U_d(z)=[V_word+Lrad(t,z,d)]/M+Rem(v,M,bbar).  (4.4)

This proves the H2 U law from (3.5), for ALL starts and d>=1. It also
retains the actual remainders, instead of assigning them values from a
different trajectory.

All remainders are themselves sums of original augmented normal rows
and one square. In (4.1), let bbar=sum theta_i g_i with theta_i>=0 and
sum theta_i=1, including the actual auxiliary q sample. The initial
auxiliary sample has input v/M and gradient p. Formula (2.3) gives

 Rem=M||p-bbar||^2-2M sum_i theta_i Q_(i,p).           (4.5)

Here Q_(i,p)=<v/M-p,g_i-p>. Thus no separate unproved positivity
principle is concealed in the notation Rem.

## 5. Mechanism1: the primitive-left / actual-auxiliary bridge identity

This is the new payment. Keep a GENERAL left word of mass s_L and
capacity C_L=1+s_L, with actual input x and endpoint v. Let a_p=P(v).
Apply the ACTUAL bridge1+k, k>=0, to obtain

 w=v-(1+k)a_p, h=C_L+k=s_L+1+k.

Let D>0 be an independent trailing parameter, and define

 M=D+2h, r=P(w/D), p0=P(x/M),
 zP=[x-w+(D+h)r]/M,
 Np=<v-a_p,a_p-r>, Nr=<w-D r,r-a_p>,
 Theta=h[C_L D-k(C_L+D+k)]/(D+k).                  (5.1)

Both Np and Nr are nonnegative. In augmented row notation,
Np=-Q_(r,p), Nr=-D Q_(p,r), where the r sample has input w/D and the
p sample is the physical bridge query v. Every point is actual.
The mixture zP uses all left physical gradients, the bridge gradient
with weight1+k, and r with weightD+h. These weights sum to M.

Without any assumed signs for the primitive energy V_L, one has

 U_M(x)-U_D(w)
   = [V_L+Theta||a_p-r||^2
        +2h(1+C_L/(D+k))Np+2h C_L/(D+k)Nr]/M
       +Rem(x,M,zP).                                (5.2)

Here is a full derivation. Put X=v+s_L a_p and e=a_p-r. Then

 w+h r=X-h e,
 ||x||^2-||w+h r||^2=V_L+2h<X,e>-h^2||e||^2.        (5.3)

The actual projection normals give the exact relations

 Np+Nr=(D+k)<a_p,e>-D||e||^2,
 <X,e>=C_L<a_p,e>+Np
       =C_L D||e||^2/(D+k)
          +(1+C_L/(D+k))Np+C_L Nr/(D+k).            (5.4)

Substitute (5.4) in (5.3) and then apply (4.2) to the prefix mass h
with trailing D. The square coefficient is
h[2C_L D/(D+k)-h]=Theta, proving (5.2).

Therefore, if

    V_L>=0 and C_L D>=k(C_L+D+k),                   (5.5)

then U_D(w)<=U_(D+2h)(x). This is a reusable quantified lemma with
independent C_L,D and actual physical bridge k. The numerical capacity
condition is not a substitute for the displayed actual left reserve.
A right word of mass s_R may be appended if its actual U law holds at
trailing d; use D=d+2s_R. No equality C_L=1+s_R is required by (5.2).

## 6. Application to both actual H2 children: nonnegative H3 identity

Now specialize to the actual H3. Write

 x=x0, v=x3, a_p=g3, w=x4, y=x7, b=g7,
 D=d+2s, M=d+2S=D+2h,
 q=P(y/d), r=P(w/D), p0=P(x/M),
 zR=[w-y+(d+s)q]/D,
 zP=[x-w+(D+h)r]/M,
 RR=Rem(w,D,zR), RP=Rem(x,M,zP),
 LR=Lrad(s,y,d).

The initial maximizer of the right child is EXACTLY r, the final
auxiliary maximizer in (5.2). There is no independent choice or reset.
Let V_L and V_R be the actual H2 energies (3.5), using respectively
indices0,1,2,3 and4,5,6,7.

The scalar coefficient is strictly positive for every d>=1. Since
beta>9/10, c<5/2, one has a>1/3, C=4+2a+beta>5, and
k=lambda(1-epsilon^2)<5/2<C/2. Also D>=2C-1. Hence

 C D-k(C+D+k)=(C-k)D-k(C+k)
   > (C/2)(2C-1)-3C^2/4=C(C-2)/4>0.                (6.1)

There is a uniform-in-d lower bound for the actual square coefficient:
D>9C/5 and k<C/2 imply k/(D+k)<5/23, so

 Theta/h=C-k-2Ck/(D+k)>C/2-10C/23=3C/46,
 Theta>3C(C+k)/46.                                 (6.2)

Summing (5.2) and the actual right-child identity (4.4) gives

 U_M(x)-U_d(y)
  = V_L/M+(V_R+LR)/D
    +[Theta||a_p-r||^2
       +2h(1+C/(D+k))Np+2h C/(D+k)Nr]/M
    +RP+RR.                                        (6.3)

Every term on the right is nonnegative after substituting the complete
H2 formula (3.5). Equations (3.5)-(3.6), (4.5), and the explicit two
normal rows after (5.1) make (6.3) a nonnegative original-row identity
on the physical and actual auxiliary samples of one fixed body.
In particular it retains the actual adjacent-gradient reserves

 epsilon^2/(2M) sum_(i=0)^2||g_i-g_(i+1)||^2
 +epsilon^2/(2D) sum_(i=4)^6||g_i-g_(i+1)||^2,

as well as the root auxiliary square of coefficientTheta/M.
No claim is made that the adjacent squares alone pay the root defect.

This proves the stated all-body H3 inequality WITHIN THIS JOINT DRAFT.
At d=1, U1=2F; (2.2) also yields

    2F(T_H3 x)<=dist(x,K0)^2/(1+2S3).

This remains a projection-gradient-class assertion. No arbitrary-height
representation or full-function asymptotic conclusion is being used.

## 7. Exact reconciliation with the negative R2 term

To show explicitly that the old off-root term has been paid, retain
also the full-parent objects demanded by the assignment:

 zF=[x-y+(d+S)q]/M,
 RF=Rem(x,M,zF), LF=Lrad(S,y,d).

These are generally DIFFERENT from zP and RP. Formula (4.4), followed
by (3.8), gives the same parent gap as

 M[U_M(x)-U_d(y)]
  =V_L+K2 V_R-(K2-1)E2
        +R2(||a_p||^2-||b||^2)+LF+M RF.             (7.1)

Multiplying (6.3) by M gives its second, nonnegative representation:

 V_L+(M/D)(V_R+LR)+Theta||a_p-r||^2
   +2h(1+C/(D+k))Np+2h C/(D+k)Nr+M(RP+RR).          (7.2)

Equations (7.1) and (7.2) are an exact identity with all actual
maximizers retained. Since R2<0, their equality proves

 (-R2)(||a_p||^2-||b||^2)
   <=V_L+K2 V_R-(K2-1)E2+LF+M RF.                  (7.3)

No lower bound for RF from a different path is imported. The proof of
(7.3) is precisely the nonnegative decomposition (7.2), with the actual
right-input maximizer r. It is not the unsupported assertion that RF
must automatically have the needed size. In particular neither RF=RP
nor RF=RP+RR is asserted.

## 8. Mandatory fixed-interval cap/cap check

Use the ONE interval D0=[-k/C,1], put rcap=k/C, and start x=C.
Every left H2 prefix of mass t in[0,C-1] has point C-t>=1 and gradient1.
The endpoint is v=1. The root bridge sends it to w=-k. Every right H2
prefix of mass t has point-k+t rcap<=-rcap and gradient-rcap. Its actual
endpoint is y=-rcap. All seven physical steps, both children and the
terminal gradient are present. These statements follow symbolically
from nonnegative step masses; no trajectory was executed.

Both child energies and all adjacent-gradient reserves are zero. Every
E2 row is zero: the physical normal at v=1 is zero, the terminal normal
at y=-rcap is zero, and all right gradients agree. Nonetheless

 V_H3=C^2-(2C+k)^2 rcap^2=R2(1-rcap^2)<0.           (8.1)

Thus (6.3) has not inadvertently reinstated primitive positivity.
For every d>=1 all the relevant maximizers are interior or boundary
points of this SAME interval, with explicit values

 D=d+2(C-1), M=d+2S,
 p0=C/M, q=-rcap/d, r=-k/D.

The full-parent mixture and remainder are

 zF=(C-S rcap/d)/M,
 x-Mp0=0, RF=S^2 rcap^2/(d^2 M).                   (8.2)

The two new mixtures and their actual remainders are

 zP=(C-kh/D)/M, RP=k^2 h^2/(D^2 M),
 zR=-k[1+(C-1)/(Cd)]/D,
 w-D r=0, RR=k^2(C-1)^2/(C^2 d^2 D).              (8.3)

Both root normal terms Np,Nr are zero; a_p-r=1+k/D. The right radial
term is

 LR=rcap^2[2(C-1)(1-1/d)+(C-1)^2(1-1/d^2)].        (8.4)

Substitution into (6.3) gives the two genuine telescoping gaps

 [C^2/M-k^2/D]+[k^2/D-rcap^2/d]
     =C^2/M-rcap^2/d.                              (8.5)

Equations (8.2) and (4.2) give the same full-parent gap directly:
[C^2-rcap^2(1+S/d)^2]/M+S^2 rcap^2/(d^2M).
Thus both representations retain the actual RF payment, including d=1
where the full radial term vanishes and V_H3 is negative.

This family has the strict relative margin

 U_d(y)/U_M(x)=rcap^2 M/(d C^2)
    <(5C-1)/(4C^2)<1/4.                            (8.6)

Here rcap<1/2, S=2C+k-1<(5/2)C-1, and C>5; the final comparison is
C^2-5C+1>0. This diagnostic agrees with the universal candidate, but
its remainder formulas are used ONLY for this history.

## 9. Mechanism2: a concrete reusable U-only join is false

After the H3 outcome, test the following natural extension of (5.5):

 Proposed U-only join. Suppose each child word has
 U_d(T_child z)<=U_(d+2mass(child))(z) for every fixed compact convex
 body containing0, every start and every d>=1. Set C_L=1+s_L and
 D=d+2s_R. If C_L D>=k(C_L+D+k), then their physical join
 (left,1+k,right) has the same U law at its actual total mass.

This statement keeps d and the two masses independent. Its difference
from (5.5) is precisely replacement of the actual left primitive reserve
by a U-only hypothesis. The following family disproves it.

First derive the needed universal child law jointly. For one physical
step l=3/2, write a=P(x), y=x-l a, q=P(y/d), M=d+2l=d+3. Set

 Awt=Ml/(M-1), Bwt=M-Awt,
 zbar=(Awt a+Bwt q)/M, p=P(x/M).

Both weights are nonnegative and sum to M. For d>=1 one has Awt<=2,
because (3/2)(d+3)<=2(d+2). Put e=a-q and N=<x-a,a-q>>=0.
Direct expansion, using Ml-(M-1)Awt=0, gives

 2<x,Awt a+Bwt q>-||Awt a+Bwt q||^2-M U_d(y)
       =Awt(2-Awt)||e||^2+2Awt N.                  (9.1)

For the coefficient check before imposing the defining relation, the
left side is
2Awt<x,e>+2M(l-Awt)<q,e>-Awt^2||e||^2.
Substituting <x,e>=<q,e>+||e||^2+N cancels the <q,e> coefficient.
Comparison with the actual maximizer p therefore gives

 U_M(x)-U_d(y)
   =[Awt(2-Awt)||a-q||^2+2Awt N]/M
     +Rem(x,M,zbar)>=0.                            (9.2)

No curvature assumption beyond the fixed-body projection class was
introduced. Thus each single-step3/2 child satisfies the universal
hypothesis, for every d>=1, not merely on the counterexample body.

Now choose BOTH actual children to be that step, and use k=5/4, hence
bridge1+k=9/4. Then s_L=s_R=3/2, C_L=5/2, D=d+3, and

 C_L D-k(C_L+D+k)=(5/4)(d-3/4)>0 for d>=1.          (9.3)

Use the fixed body D0=[-1,1] and initial x=1. The actual physical word
(3/2,9/4,3/2) has points

    x0=1, x1=-1/2, x2=5/8, x3=-5/16.              (9.4)

All lie in the quadratic core, so each displayed gradient equals its
point. The total physical mass is21/4. For every d>=1 the relevant
actual maximizers also lie in the same interval, giving

 U_d(x3)=25/(256d),
 U_(d+21/2)(x0)=1/(d+21/2),
 U_d(x3)/U_(d+21/2)(x0)=25(d+21/2)/(256d).           (9.5)

This ratio exceeds1 exactly when d<175/154. Thus the ONE fixed legal
history disproves the proposed join for the entire auxiliary-parameter
interval1<=d<175/154. Scaling the start to any x in(0,1] preserves the
violation and all memberships in the same body. At d=1 the ratio is
575/512 and the parent gap is-63/5888. The physical start and gradients
are explicit, so this is not a formal negative direction without a body.

The precise missing reserve is visible already in one child. At start1,
step3/2 and trailing1, the child has

 V_child=1-(5/2)^2(1/2)^2=-9/16,
 q=-1/2, p=1/4, canonical zbar=1/16,
 canonical Rem=9/64, U4(1)-U1(-1/2)=0.              (9.6)

The actual U gap is zero because V_child/4 is exactly cancelled by its
actual remainder. That cancellation cannot be reused as a nonnegative
primitive reserve when composing the next bridge. This is the legal
failure mode anticipated by retaining all auxiliary maximizers.

The counterexample does not use the original H_j children, and does not
refute an eventual bound on H4 or higher. It refutes this concrete
U-only closure rule and shows that the zero-loss rule cannot be repaired
by an unspecified vanishing error uniformly over its stated child class:
its worst-case relative loss is at least575/512 already here.

## 10. Exact remaining interface and limits

For arbitrary left and right words the useful identity is still

 parent U gap = V_L/M + right U gap
                +bridge-square-and-normal terms/M+actual RP.  (10.1)

The bridge terms are exactly those in (5.2); they remain explicitly
quantified for independent C_L,D,k. If the left U comparison is known
at some parameter eta>=1, its exact relation to V_L is

 V_L=(eta+2s_L)[U_(eta+2s_L)(x)-U_eta(v)]
       -Lrad(s_L,v,eta)
       -(eta+2s_L)Rem_left(eta).                    (10.2)

The two SUBTRACTED actual terms are nonnegative and cannot be discarded.
Equation (9.6) supplies a legal instance where they are indispensable.
Thus (10.1)-(10.2) identify the remaining two-parameter reserve-transfer
problem; they are not presented as a solved all-depth invariant.

For actual H4 the left child is H3. Section8 gives an actual history
where its V_L is negative. A proof at higher depths must control that
negative V_L through the new parent auxiliary square, its normal rows,
and actual maximization remainders, with quantified coefficients and
uniform total loss. Child U inequalities alone, even together with the
scalar condition (5.5), cannot perform this transfer in general.
No C_j^2 factor has been hidden in O(epsilon), and no asymptotic error
bound has been claimed. The two allotted mechanisms end at this precise
interface. No third mechanism is opened.
